GENESIS — LATIN–ENGLISH (CLEMENTINE VULGATE)
Captured from the Saints.Bible staging Parallel Reader, 2 November 2026.
Editorial revisions dated 9 and 13 November 2026. The capture date above identifies the source snapshot.
Verse numbers follow the Saints (Greek/Hebrew) column; 'vulgate_reference' in verses.json gives the Vulgate's own number where it differs.

GENESIS 1

1:1  LA: In principio creavit Deus cælum et terram.
     EN: In the beginning God created heaven and earth.

1:2  LA: Terra autem erat inanis et vacua, et tenebræ erant super faciem abyssi : et spiritus Dei ferebatur super aquas.
     EN: The earth was empty and vacant, and darkness lay over the face of the deep, and the Spirit of God moved over the waters.

1:3  LA: Dixitque Deus : Fiat lux. Et facta est lux.
     EN: God said, “Let there be light.” And there was light.

1:4  LA: Et vidit Deus lucem quod esset bona : et divisit lucem a tenebris.
     EN: God saw that the light was good, and He separated the light from the darkness.

1:5  LA: Appellavitque lucem Diem, et tenebras Noctem : factumque est vespere et mane, dies unus.
     EN: God called the light Day, and the darkness Night. Evening came, and morning came: one day.

1:6  LA: Dixit quoque Deus : Fiat firmamentum in medio aquarum : et dividat aquas ab aquis.
     EN: God also said, “Let there be a firmament in the midst of the waters, and let it separate waters from waters.”

1:7  LA: Et fecit Deus firmamentum, divisitque aquas, quæ erant sub firmamento, ab his, quæ erant super firmamentum. Et factum est ita.
     EN: God made the firmament and separated the waters beneath the firmament from those above the firmament. And so it was.

1:8  LA: Vocavitque Deus firmamentum, Cælum : et factum est vespere et mane, dies secundus.
     EN: God called the firmament Heaven. Evening came, and morning came: the second day.

1:9  LA: Dixit vero Deus : Congregentur aquæ, quæ sub cælo sunt, in locum unum : et appareat arida. Et factum est ita.
     EN: God said, “Let the waters beneath heaven gather into one place, and let dry land appear.” And so it was.

1:10  LA: Et vocavit Deus aridam Terram, congregationesque aquarum appellavit Maria. Et vidit Deus quod esset bonum.
     EN: God called the dry land Earth, and the gatherings of water He called Seas. God saw that it was good.

1:11  LA: Et ait : Germinet terra herbam virentem, et facientem semen, et lignum pomiferum faciens fructum juxta genus suum, cujus semen in semetipso sit super terram. Et factum est ita.
     EN: He said, “Let the earth bring forth green plants bearing seed, and fruit trees bearing fruit according to their kind, with their seed within them upon the earth.” And so it was.

1:12  LA: Et protulit terra herbam virentem, et facientem semen juxta genus suum, lignumque faciens fructum, et habens unumquodque sementem secundum speciem suam. Et vidit Deus quod esset bonum.
     EN: The earth brought forth green plants bearing seed according to their kind, and trees bearing fruit, each having seed according to its kind. God saw that it was good.

1:13  LA: Et factum est vespere et mane, dies tertius.
     EN: Evening came, and morning came: the third day.

1:14  LA: Dixit autem Deus : Fiant luminaria in firmamento cæli, et dividant diem ac noctem, et sint in signa et tempora, et dies et annos :
     EN: God said, “Let there be lights in the firmament of heaven, and let them separate day from night. Let them serve as signs and mark seasons, days, and years,

1:15  LA: ut luceant in firmamento cæli, et illuminent terram. Et factum est ita.
     EN: shining in the firmament of heaven and giving light to the earth.” And so it was.

1:16  LA: Fecitque Deus duo luminaria magna : luminare majus, ut præesset diei : et luminare minus, ut præesset nocti : et stellas.
     EN: God made two great lights: the greater light to rule the day, and the lesser light to rule the night, and the stars.

1:17  LA: Et posuit eas in firmamento cæli, ut lucerent super terram,
     EN: He placed them in the firmament of heaven to shine upon the earth,

1:18  LA: et præessent diei ac nocti, et dividerent lucem ac tenebras. Et vidit Deus quod esset bonum.
     EN: to rule the day and night, and to separate light from darkness. God saw that it was good.

1:19  LA: Et factum est vespere et mane, dies quartus.
     EN: Evening came, and morning came: the fourth day.

1:20  LA: Dixit etiam Deus : Producant aquæ reptile animæ viventis, et volatile super terram sub firmamento cæli.
     EN: God also said, “Let the waters bring forth living things that creep, and winged creatures above the earth beneath the firmament of heaven.”

1:21  LA: Creavitque Deus cete grandia, et omnem animam viventem atque motabilem, quam produxerant aquæ in species suas, et omne volatile secundum genus suum. Et vidit Deus quod esset bonum.
     EN: God created the great sea creatures and every living, moving creature that the waters brought forth according to their kinds, and every winged creature according to its kind. God saw that it was good.

1:22  LA: Benedixitque eis, dicens : Crescite, et multiplicamini, et replete aquas maris : avesque multiplicentur super terram.
     EN: He blessed them, saying, “Increase and multiply, and fill the waters of the sea; and let birds multiply upon the earth.”

1:23  LA: Et factum est vespere et mane, dies quintus.
     EN: Evening came, and morning came: the fifth day.

1:24  LA: Dixit quoque Deus : Producat terra animam viventem in genere suo, jumenta, et reptilia, et bestias terræ secundum species suas. Factumque est ita.
     EN: God also said, “Let the earth bring forth living creatures according to their kinds: livestock, creeping creatures, and wild animals of the earth according to their kinds.” And so it was.

1:25  LA: Et fecit Deus bestias terræ juxta species suas, et jumenta, et omne reptile terræ in genere suo. Et vidit Deus quod esset bonum,
     EN: God made the wild animals of the earth according to their kinds, the livestock, and every creeping creature of the earth according to its kind. God saw that it was good,

1:26  LA: et ait : Faciamus hominem ad imaginem et similitudinem nostram : et præsit piscibus maris, et volatilibus cæli, et bestiis, universæque terræ, omnique reptili, quod movetur in terra.
     EN: and said, “Let Us make man in Our image and likeness, and let him rule the fish of the sea, the birds of heaven, the animals, the whole earth, and every creeping creature that moves upon the earth.”

1:27  LA: Et creavit Deus hominem ad imaginem suam : ad imaginem Dei creavit illum, masculum et feminam creavit eos.
     EN: God created man in His image. In the image of God He created him. Male and female He created them.

1:28  LA: Benedixitque illis Deus, et ait : Crescite et multiplicamini, et replete terram, et subjicite eam, et dominamini piscibus maris, et volatilibus cæli, et universis animantibus, quæ moventur super terram.
     EN: God blessed them and said, “Increase and multiply; fill the earth and subdue it. Rule the fish of the sea, the birds of heaven, and every living creature that moves upon the earth.”

1:29  LA: Dixitque Deus : Ecce dedi vobis omnem herbam afferentem semen super terram, et universa ligna quæ habent in semetipsis sementem generis sui, ut sint vobis in escam :
     EN: God said, “See, I have given you every seed-bearing plant upon the earth, and all trees that have within them seed of their own kind, to be your food,

1:30  LA: et cunctis animantibus terræ, omnique volucri cæli, et universis quæ moventur in terra, et in quibus est anima vivens, ut habeant ad vescendum. Et factum est ita.
     EN: and food for every animal of the earth, every bird of heaven, and everything that moves upon the earth and has living breath.” And so it was.

1:31  LA: Viditque Deus cuncta quæ fecerat, et erant valde bona. Et factum est vespere et mane, dies sextus.
     EN: God saw everything He had made, and it was very good. Evening came, and morning came: the sixth day.

GENESIS 2

2:1  LA: Igitur perfecti sunt cæli et terra, et omnis ornatus eorum.
     EN: Thus heaven and earth were completed, with all their adornment.

2:2  LA: Complevitque Deus die septimo opus suum quod fecerat : et requievit die septimo ab universo opere quod patrarat.
     EN: On the seventh day God completed the work He had done, and on the seventh day He rested from all the work He had accomplished.

2:3  LA: Et benedixit diei septimo, et sanctificavit illum, quia in ipso cessaverat ab omni opere suo quod creavit Deus ut faceret.
     EN: He blessed the seventh day and made it holy, because on it He had ceased from all His work, which God created to be made.

2:4  LA: Istæ sunt generationes cæli et terræ, quando creata sunt, in die quo fecit Dominus Deus cælum et terram,
     EN: These are the origins of heaven and earth when they were created, on the day the Lord God made heaven and earth,

2:5  LA: et omne virgultum agri antequam oriretur in terra, omnemque herbam regionis priusquam germinaret : non enim pluerat Dominus Deus super terram, et homo non erat qui operaretur terram :
     EN: and every shrub of the field before it arose upon the earth, and every plant of the countryside before it sprouted. For the Lord God had not sent rain upon the earth, and there was no human being to cultivate the ground;

2:6  LA: sed fons ascendebat e terra, irrigans universam superficiem terræ.
     EN: but a spring rose from the earth and watered the whole surface of the ground.

2:7  LA: Formavit igitur Dominus Deus hominem de limo terræ, et inspiravit in faciem ejus spiraculum vitæ, et factus est homo in animam viventem.
     EN: Then the Lord God formed the human being from the clay of the ground and breathed the breath of life into his face, and the human being became a living soul.

2:8  LA: Plantaverat autem Dominus Deus paradisum voluptatis a principio, in quo posuit hominem quem formaverat.
     EN: The Lord God had planted a garden of delight from the beginning, and there He placed the human being He had formed.

2:9  LA: Produxitque Dominus Deus de humo omne lignum pulchrum visu, et ad vescendum suave lignum etiam vitæ in medio paradisi, lignumque scientiæ boni et mali.
     EN: The Lord God brought forth from the ground every tree beautiful to see and pleasant to eat from, also the tree of life in the midst of the garden, and the tree of the knowledge of good and evil.

2:10  LA: Et fluvius egrediebatur de loco voluptatis ad irrigandum paradisum, qui inde dividitur in quatuor capita.
     EN: A river flowed out from the place of delight to water the garden; from there it divided into four branches.

2:11  LA: Nomen uni Phison : ipse est qui circuit omnem terram Hevilath, ubi nascitur aurum :
     EN: The name of the first is Pishon; it winds through the whole land of Havilah, where gold is found.

2:12  LA: et aurum terræ illius optimum est ; ibi invenitur bdellium, et lapis onychinus.
     EN: The gold of that land is excellent; bdellium and onyx stone are found there.

2:13  LA: Et nomen fluvii secundi Gehon ; ipse est qui circumit omnem terram Æthiopiæ.
     EN: The name of the second river is Gihon; it winds through the whole land of Ethiopia.

2:14  LA: Nomen vero fluminis tertii, Tigris : ipse vadit contra Assyrios. Fluvius autem quartus, ipse est Euphrates.
     EN: The name of the third river is Tigris; it flows toward the Assyrians. The fourth river is the Euphrates.

2:15  LA: Tulit ergo Dominus Deus hominem, et posuit eum in paradiso voluptatis, ut operaretur, et custodiret illum :
     EN: The Lord God took the human being and placed him in the garden of delight to cultivate and keep it.

2:16  LA: præcepitque ei, dicens : Ex omni ligno paradisi comede ;
     EN: He commanded him, saying, “Eat from every tree in the garden;

2:17  LA: de ligno autem scientiæ boni et mali ne comedas : in quocumque enim die comederis ex eo, morte morieris.
     EN: but do not eat from the tree of the knowledge of good and evil, for on whatever day you eat from it, you shall surely die.”

2:18  LA: Dixit quoque Dominus Deus : Non est bonum esse hominem solum : faciamus ei adjutorium simile sibi.
     EN: The Lord God also said, “It is not good for the human being to be alone; let Us make him a helper like himself.”

2:19  LA: Formatis igitur Dominus Deus de humo cunctis animantibus terræ, et universis volatilibus cæli, adduxit ea ad Adam, ut videret quid vocaret ea : omne enim quod vocavit Adam animæ viventis, ipsum est nomen ejus.
     EN: The Lord God, having formed from the ground all the animals of the earth and all the birds of heaven, brought them to Adam to see what he would call them. Whatever Adam called each living creature became its name.

2:20  LA: Appellavitque Adam nominibus suis cuncta animantia, et universa volatilia cæli, et omnes bestias terræ : Adæ vero non inveniebatur adjutor similis ejus.
     EN: Adam gave names to all the animals, all the birds of heaven, and all the wild animals of the earth; but for Adam no helper like himself was found.

2:21  LA: Immisit ergo Dominus Deus soporem in Adam : cumque obdormisset, tulit unam de costis ejus, et replevit carnem pro ea.
     EN: The Lord God sent a deep sleep upon Adam. When he had fallen asleep, He took one of his ribs and filled its place with flesh.

2:22  LA: Et ædificavit Dominus Deus costam, quam tulerat de Adam, in mulierem : et adduxit eam ad Adam.
     EN: The Lord God fashioned the rib He had taken from Adam into a woman and brought her to Adam.

2:23  LA: Dixitque Adam : Hoc nunc os ex ossibus meis, et caro de carne mea : hæc vocabitur Virago, quoniam de viro sumpta est.
     EN: Adam said, “This now is bone of my bones and flesh of my flesh. She shall be called Woman, because she was taken from man.”

2:24  LA: Quam ob rem relinquet homo patrem suum, et matrem, et adhærebit uxori suæ : et erunt duo in carne una.
     EN: For this reason a man shall leave his father and mother and cling to his wife, and the two shall be one flesh.

2:25  LA: Erat autem uterque nudus, Adam scilicet et uxor ejus : et non erubescebant.
     EN: Both were naked, Adam and his wife, and they were not ashamed.

GENESIS 3

3:1  LA: Sed et serpens erat callidior cunctis animantibus terræ quæ fecerat Dominus Deus. Qui dixit ad mulierem : Cur præcepit vobis Deus ut non comederetis de omni ligno paradisi ?
     EN: Now the serpent was more cunning than all the animals of the earth that the Lord God had made. It said to the woman, “Why has God commanded you not to eat from every tree in the garden?”

3:2  LA: Cui respondit mulier : De fructu lignorum, quæ sunt in paradiso, vescimur :
     EN: The woman answered it, “We eat the fruit of the trees that are in the garden;

3:3  LA: de fructu vero ligni quod est in medio paradisi, præcepit nobis Deus ne comederemus, et ne tangeremus illud, ne forte moriamur.
     EN: but concerning the fruit of the tree in the midst of the garden, God commanded us not to eat it or touch it, lest perhaps we die.”

3:4  LA: Dixit autem serpens ad mulierem : Nequaquam morte moriemini.
     EN: The serpent said to the woman, “You certainly shall not die.

3:5  LA: Scit enim Deus quod in quocumque die comederitis ex eo, aperientur oculi vestri, et eritis sicut dii, scientes bonum et malum.
     EN: For God knows that on whatever day you eat from it, your eyes will be opened, and you will be like gods, knowing good and evil.”

3:6  LA: Vidit igitur mulier quod bonum esset lignum ad vescendum, et pulchrum oculis, aspectuque delectabile : et tulit de fructu illius, et comedit : deditque viro suo, qui comedit.
     EN: The woman saw that the tree was good for food, beautiful to the eyes, and delightful to look upon. She took some of its fruit and ate, and gave some to her husband, who ate.

3:7  LA: Et aperti sunt oculi amborum ; cumque cognovissent se esse nudos, consuerunt folia ficus, et fecerunt sibi perizomata.
     EN: The eyes of both were opened. When they realized they were naked, they sewed fig leaves together and made coverings for themselves.

3:8  LA: Et cum audissent vocem Domini Dei deambulantis in paradiso ad auram post meridiem, abscondit se Adam et uxor ejus a facie Domini Dei in medio ligni paradisi.
     EN: When they heard the sound of the Lord God walking in the garden in the afternoon breeze, Adam and his wife hid from the presence of the Lord God among the trees of the garden.

3:9  LA: Vocavitque Dominus Deus Adam, et dixit ei : Ubi es ?
     EN: The Lord God called Adam and said to him, “Where are you?”

3:10  LA: Qui ait : Vocem tuam audivi in paradiso, et timui, eo quod nudus essem, et abscondi me.
     EN: He said, “I heard Your voice in the garden and was afraid because I was naked, so I hid.”

3:11  LA: Cui dixit : Quis enim indicavit tibi quod nudus esses, nisi quod ex ligno de quo præceperam tibi ne comederes, comedisti ?
     EN: He said to him, “Who told you that you were naked, unless you have eaten from the tree from which I commanded you not to eat?”

3:12  LA: Dixitque Adam : Mulier, quam dedisti mihi sociam, dedit mihi de ligno, et comedi.
     EN: Adam said, “The woman You gave me as a companion gave me some from the tree, and I ate.”

3:13  LA: Et dixit Dominus Deus ad mulierem : Quare hoc fecisti ? Quæ respondit : Serpens decepit me, et comedi.
     EN: The Lord God said to the woman, “Why have you done this?” She answered, “The serpent deceived me, and I ate.”

3:14  LA: Et ait Dominus Deus ad serpentem : [Quia fecisti hoc, maledictus es inter omnia animantia, et bestias terræ : super pectus tuum gradieris, et terram comedes cunctis diebus vitæ tuæ.
     EN: The Lord God said to the serpent, “Because you have done this, you are cursed among all animals and wild creatures of the earth. Upon your breast you shall move, and earth you shall eat all the days of your life.

3:15  LA: Inimicitias ponam inter te et mulierem, et semen tuum et semen illius : ipsa conteret caput tuum, et tu insidiaberis calcaneo ejus.]
     EN: I will put enmity between you and the woman, and between your offspring and hers; she shall crush your head, and you shall lie in wait for her heel.”

3:16  LA: Mulieri quoque dixit : Multiplicabo ærumnas tuas, et conceptus tuos : in dolore paries filios, et sub viri potestate eris, et ipse dominabitur tui.
     EN: To the woman He also said, “I will multiply your hardships and your pregnancies. In pain you shall bear children, and you shall be under your husband's power, and he shall rule over you.”

3:17  LA: Adæ vero dixit : Quia audisti vocem uxoris tuæ, et comedisti de ligno, ex quo præceperam tibi ne comederes, maledicta terra in opere tuo : in laboribus comedes ex ea cunctis diebus vitæ tuæ.
     EN: To Adam He said, “Because you listened to your wife's voice and ate from the tree from which I commanded you not to eat, the ground is cursed in your work. Through toil you shall eat from it all the days of your life.

3:18  LA: Spinas et tribulos germinabit tibi, et comedes herbam terræ.
     EN: It shall bring forth thorns and thistles for you, and you shall eat the plants of the earth.

3:19  LA: In sudore vultus tui vesceris pane, donec revertaris in terram de qua sumptus es : quia pulvis es et in pulverem reverteris.
     EN: By the sweat of your face you shall eat bread, until you return to the earth from which you were taken; for you are dust, and to dust you shall return.”

3:20  LA: Et vocavit Adam nomen uxoris suæ, Heva : eo quod mater esset cunctorum viventium.
     EN: Adam named his wife Eve, because she was the mother of all the living.

3:21  LA: Fecit quoque Dominus Deus Adæ et uxori ejus tunicas pelliceas, et induit eos :
     EN: The Lord God also made tunics of skin for Adam and his wife and clothed them.

3:22  LA: et ait : Ecce Adam quasi unus ex nobis factus est, sciens bonum et malum : nunc ergo ne forte mittat manum suam, et sumat etiam de ligno vitæ, et comedat, et vivat in æternum.
     EN: He said, “See, Adam has become like one of Us, knowing good and evil. Now, lest he stretch out his hand and take also from the tree of life, eat, and live forever—”

3:23  LA: Et emisit eum Dominus Deus de paradiso voluptatis, ut operaretur terram de qua sumptus est.
     EN: The Lord God sent him out of the garden of delight to cultivate the ground from which he was taken.

3:24  LA: Ejecitque Adam : et collocavit ante paradisum voluptatis cherubim, et flammeum gladium, atque versatilem, ad custodiendam viam ligni vitæ.
     EN: He drove Adam out and stationed cherubim before the garden of delight, and a flaming, turning sword, to guard the way to the tree of life.

GENESIS 4

4:1  LA: Adam vero cognovit uxorem suam Hevam, quæ concepit et peperit Cain, dicens : Possedi hominem per Deum.
     EN: Adam knew his wife Eve, and she conceived and bore Cain, saying, “I have obtained a man through God.”

4:2  LA: Rursumque peperit fratrem ejus Abel. Fuit autem Abel pastor ovium, et Cain agricola.
     EN: Again she gave birth, to his brother Abel. Abel was a shepherd, and Cain a cultivator of the ground.

4:3  LA: Factum est autem post multos dies ut offerret Cain de fructibus terræ munera Domino.
     EN: After many days Cain brought gifts to the Lord from the fruits of the ground.

4:4  LA: Abel quoque obtulit de primogenitis gregis sui, et de adipibus eorum : et respexit Dominus ad Abel, et ad munera ejus.
     EN: Abel also brought gifts from the firstborn of his flock and from their fat. The Lord regarded Abel and his gifts,

4:5  LA: Ad Cain vero, et ad munera illius non respexit : iratusque est Cain vehementer, et concidit vultus ejus.
     EN: but Cain and his gifts He did not regard. Cain became very angry, and his face fell.

4:6  LA: Dixitque Dominus ad eum : Quare iratus es ? et cur concidit facies tua ?
     EN: The Lord said to him, “Why are you angry, and why has your face fallen?

4:7  LA: nonne si bene egeris, recipies : sin autem male, statim in foribus peccatum aderit ? sed sub te erit appetitus ejus, et tu dominaberis illius.
     EN: If you do well, will you not receive? But if you do badly, will sin not be immediately at the door? Yet its desire shall be under you, and you shall rule over it.”

4:8  LA: Dixitque Cain ad Abel fratrem suum : Egrediamur foras. Cumque essent in agro, consurrexit Cain adversus fratrem suum Abel, et interfecit eum.
     EN: Cain said to his brother Abel, “Let us go outside.” When they were in the field, Cain rose against his brother Abel and killed him.

4:9  LA: Et ait Dominus ad Cain : Ubi est Abel frater tuus ? Qui respondit : Nescio : num custos fratris mei sum ego ?
     EN: The Lord said to Cain, “Where is your brother Abel?” He answered, “I do not know. Am I my brother's keeper?”

4:10  LA: Dixitque ad eum : Quid fecisti ? vox sanguinis fratris tui clamat ad me de terra.
     EN: He said to him, “What have you done? The voice of your brother's blood cries to Me from the ground.

4:11  LA: Nunc igitur maledictus eris super terram, quæ aperuit os suum, et suscepit sanguinem fratris tui de manu tua.
     EN: Now therefore you shall be cursed upon the earth, which opened its mouth and received your brother's blood from your hand.

4:12  LA: Cum operatus fueris eam, non dabit tibi fructus suos : vagus et profugus eris super terram.
     EN: When you cultivate it, it shall not give you its fruits. You shall be a wanderer and fugitive upon the earth.”

4:13  LA: Dixitque Cain ad Dominum : Major est iniquitas mea, quam ut veniam merear.
     EN: Cain said to the Lord, “My wrongdoing is too great for me to deserve forgiveness.

4:14  LA: Ecce ejicis me hodie a facie terræ, et a facie tua abscondar, et ero vagus et profugus in terra : omnis igitur qui invenerit me, occidet me.
     EN: See, today You drive me from the face of the earth, and I shall be hidden from Your face. I shall be a wanderer and fugitive upon the earth, and whoever finds me will kill me.”

4:15  LA: Dixitque ei Dominus : Nequaquam ita fiet : sed omnis qui occiderit Cain, septuplum punietur. Posuitque Dominus Cain signum, ut non interficeret eum omnis qui invenisset eum.
     EN: The Lord said to him, “It shall certainly not be so. Whoever kills Cain shall be punished sevenfold.” The Lord placed a mark on Cain, so that no one who found him would kill him.

4:16  LA: Egressusque Cain a facie Domini, habitavit profugus in terra ad orientalem plagam Eden.
     EN: Cain went out from the presence of the Lord and lived as a fugitive in the land to the east of Eden.

4:17  LA: Cognovit autem Cain uxorem suam, quæ concepit, et peperit Henoch : et ædificavit civitatem, vocavitque nomen ejus ex nomine filii sui, Henoch.
     EN: Cain knew his wife, and she conceived and bore Enoch. He built a city and named it Enoch after his son.

4:18  LA: Porro Henoch genuit Irad, et Irad genuit Maviaël, et Maviaël genuit Mathusaël, et Mathusaël genuit Lamech.
     EN: Enoch fathered Irad; Irad fathered Mehujael; Mehujael fathered Methushael; and Methushael fathered Lamech.

4:19  LA: Qui accepit duas uxores, nomen uni Ada, et nomen alteri Sella.
     EN: Lamech took two wives: one named Adah and the other named Zillah.

4:20  LA: Genuitque Ada Jabel, qui fuit pater habitantium in tentoriis, atque pastorum.
     EN: Adah bore Jabal, who was the father of those who live in tents and of shepherds.

4:21  LA: Et nomen fratris ejus Jubal : ipse fuit pater canentium cithara et organo.
     EN: His brother's name was Jubal; he was the father of those who play the lyre and pipe.

4:22  LA: Sella quoque genuit Tubalcain, qui fuit malleator et faber in cuncta opera æris et ferri. Soror vero Tubalcain, Noëma.
     EN: Zillah also bore Tubal-cain, a hammer-worker and craftsman in every kind of bronze and iron work. Tubal-cain's sister was Naamah.

4:23  LA: Dixitque Lamech uxoribus suis Adæ et Sellæ : [Audite vocem meam, uxores Lamech ; auscultate sermonem meum : quoniam occidi virum in vulnus meum, et adolescentulum in livorem meum.
     EN: Lamech said to his wives Adah and Zillah, “Hear my voice, wives of Lamech; listen to my words. For I have killed a man to my own wounding, and a young man to my own bruising.

4:24  LA: Septuplum ultio dabitur de Cain : de Lamech vero septuagies septies.]
     EN: Vengeance for Cain shall be sevenfold, but for Lamech seventy-sevenfold.”

4:25  LA: Cognovit quoque adhuc Adam uxorem suam : et peperit filium, vocavitque nomen ejus Seth, dicens : Posuit mihi Deus semen aliud pro Abel, quem occidit Cain.
     EN: Adam again knew his wife, and she bore a son and named him Seth, saying, “God has appointed another offspring for me in place of Abel, whom Cain killed.”

4:26  LA: Sed et Seth natus est filius, quem vocavit Enos : iste cœpit invocare nomen Domini.
     EN: Seth also had a son, whom he named Enosh. He began to call upon the name of the Lord.

GENESIS 5

5:1  LA: Hic est liber generationis Adam. In die qua creavit Deus hominem, ad similitudinem Dei fecit illum.
     EN: This is the book of Adam's generations. On the day God created the human being, He made him in the likeness of God.

5:2  LA: Masculum et feminam creavit eos, et benedixit illis : et vocavit nomen eorum Adam, in die quo creati sunt.
     EN: Male and female He created them, and blessed them; and He called their name Adam on the day they were created.

5:3  LA: Vixit autem Adam centum triginta annis : et genuit ad imaginem et similitudinem suam, vocavitque nomen ejus Seth.
     EN: Adam lived one hundred thirty years and fathered a son in his own image and likeness, and named him Seth.

5:4  LA: Et facti sunt dies Adam, postquam genuit Seth, octingenti anni : genuitque filios et filias.
     EN: After fathering Seth, Adam's days were eight hundred years, and he fathered sons and daughters.

5:5  LA: Et factum est omne tempus quod vixit Adam, anni nongenti triginta, et mortuus est.
     EN: The whole time Adam lived was nine hundred thirty years, and he died.

5:6  LA: Vixit quoque Seth centum quinque annis, et genuit Enos.
     EN: Seth lived one hundred five years and fathered Enosh.

5:7  LA: Vixitque Seth, postquam genuit Enos, octingentis septem annis, genuitque filios et filias.
     EN: After fathering Enosh, Seth lived eight hundred seven years and fathered sons and daughters.

5:8  LA: Et facti sunt omnes dies Seth nongentorum duodecim annorum, et mortuus est.
     EN: All Seth's days were nine hundred twelve years, and he died.

5:9  LA: Vixit vero Enos nonaginta annis, et genuit Cainan.
     EN: Enosh lived ninety years and fathered Kenan.

5:10  LA: Post cujus ortum vixit octingentis quindecim annis, et genuit filios et filias.
     EN: After Kenan’s birth he lived eight hundred fifteen years and fathered sons and daughters.

5:11  LA: Factique sunt omnes dies Enos nongenti quinque anni, et mortuus est.
     EN: All Enosh's days were nine hundred five years, and he died.

5:12  LA: Vixit quoque Cainan septuaginta annis, et genuit Malaleel.
     EN: Kenan lived seventy years and fathered Mahalalel.

5:13  LA: Et vixit Cainan, postquam genuit Malaleel, octingentis quadraginta annis, genuitque filios et filias.
     EN: After fathering Mahalalel, Kenan lived eight hundred forty years and fathered sons and daughters.

5:14  LA: Et facti sunt omnes dies Cainan nongenti decem anni, et mortuus est.
     EN: All Kenan's days were nine hundred ten years, and he died.

5:15  LA: Vixit autem Malaleel sexaginta quinque annis, et genuit Jared.
     EN: Mahalalel lived sixty-five years and fathered Jared.

5:16  LA: Et vixit Malaleel, postquam genuit Jared, octingentis triginta annis, et genuit filios et filias.
     EN: After fathering Jared, Mahalalel lived eight hundred thirty years and fathered sons and daughters.

5:17  LA: Et facti sunt omnes dies Malaleel octingenti nonaginta quinque anni, et mortuus est.
     EN: All Mahalalel's days were eight hundred ninety-five years, and he died.

5:18  LA: Vixitque Jared centum sexaginta duobus annis, et genuit Henoch.
     EN: Jared lived one hundred sixty-two years and fathered Enoch.

5:19  LA: Et vixit Jared, postquam genuit Henoch, octingentis annis, et genuit filios et filias.
     EN: After fathering Enoch, Jared lived eight hundred years and fathered sons and daughters.

5:20  LA: Et facti sunt omnes dies Jared nongenti sexaginta duo anni, et mortuus est.
     EN: All Jared's days were nine hundred sixty-two years, and he died.

5:21  LA: Porro Henoch vixit sexaginta quinque annis, et genuit Mathusalam.
     EN: Enoch lived sixty-five years and fathered Methuselah.

5:22  LA: Et ambulavit Henoch cum Deo : et vixit, postquam genuit Mathusalam, trecentis annis, et genuit filios et filias.
     EN: Enoch walked with God. After fathering Methuselah he lived three hundred years and fathered sons and daughters.

5:23  LA: Et facti sunt omnes dies Henoch trecenti sexaginta quinque anni.
     EN: All Enoch's days were three hundred sixty-five years.

5:24  LA: Ambulavitque cum Deo, et non apparuit : quia tulit eum Deus.
     EN: He walked with God and was seen no more, because God took him.

5:25  LA: Vixit quoque Mathusala centum octoginta septem annis, et genuit Lamech.
     EN: Methuselah lived one hundred eighty-seven years and fathered Lamech.

5:26  LA: Et vixit Mathusala, postquam genuit Lamech, septingentis octoginta duobus annis, et genuit filios et filias.
     EN: After fathering Lamech, Methuselah lived seven hundred eighty-two years and fathered sons and daughters.

5:27  LA: Et facti sunt omnes dies Mathusala nongenti sexaginta novem anni, et mortuus est.
     EN: All Methuselah's days were nine hundred sixty-nine years, and he died.

5:28  LA: Vixit autem Lamech centum octoginta duobus annis, et genuit filium :
     EN: Lamech lived one hundred eighty-two years and fathered a son.

5:29  LA: vocavitque nomen ejus Noë, dicens : Iste consolabitur nos ab operibus et laboribus manuum nostrarum in terra, cui maledixit Dominus.
     EN: He named him Noah, saying, “This one shall comfort us from the work and toil of our hands upon the ground the Lord has cursed.”

5:30  LA: Vixitque Lamech, postquam genuit Noë, quingentis nonaginta quinque annis, et genuit filios et filias.
     EN: After fathering Noah, Lamech lived five hundred ninety-five years and fathered sons and daughters.

5:31  LA: Et facti sunt omnes dies Lamech septingenti septuaginta septem anni, et mortuus est. Noë vero cum quingentorum esset annorum, genuit Sem, Cham et Japheth.
     EN: All Lamech's days were seven hundred seventy-seven years, and he died. When Noah was five hundred years old, he fathered Shem, Ham, and Japheth.

5:32 (Vg 5:31)  LA: Et facti sunt omnes dies Lamech septingenti septuaginta septem anni, et mortuus est. Noë vero cum quingentorum esset annorum, genuit Sem, Cham et Japheth.
     EN: All Lamech's days were seven hundred seventy-seven years, and he died. When Noah was five hundred years old, he fathered Shem, Ham, and Japheth.

GENESIS 6

6:1  LA: Cumque cœpissent homines multiplicari super terram, et filias procreassent,
     EN: When human beings began to multiply upon the earth and had daughters,

6:2  LA: videntes filii Dei filias hominum quod essent pulchræ, acceperunt sibi uxores ex omnibus, quas elegerant.
     EN: the sons of God saw that the daughters of men were beautiful, and took wives for themselves from all those they chose.

6:3  LA: Dixitque Deus : Non permanebit spiritus meus in homine in æternum, quia caro est : eruntque dies illius centum viginti annorum.
     EN: God said, “My spirit shall not remain in the human being forever, because he is flesh; his days shall be one hundred twenty years.”

6:4  LA: Gigantes autem erant super terram in diebus illis : postquam enim ingressi sunt filii Dei ad filias hominum, illæque genuerunt, isti sunt potentes a sæculo viri famosi.
     EN: Giants were upon the earth in those days. For after the sons of God went in to the daughters of men and they bore children, these were the mighty men of old, men of renown.

6:5  LA: Videns autem Deus quod multa malitia hominum esset in terra, et cuncta cogitatio cordis intenta esset ad malum omni tempore,
     EN: God saw that human wickedness was great upon the earth, and every thought of the heart was intent upon evil at all times.

6:6  LA: pœnituit eum quod hominum fecisset in terra. Et tactus dolore cordis intrinsecus,
     EN: He regretted having made human beings upon the earth, and, touched with grief deep within His heart,

6:7  LA: Delebo, inquit, hominem, quem creavi, a facie terræ, ab homine usque ad animantia, a reptili usque ad volucres cæli : pœnitet enim me fecisse eos.
     EN: He said, “I will wipe the human being whom I created from the face of the earth, from human beings to animals, from creeping creatures to birds of heaven; for I regret having made them.”

6:8  LA: Noë vero invenit gratiam coram Domino.
     EN: But Noah found favor before the Lord.

6:9  LA: Hæ sunt generationes Noë : Noë vir justus atque perfectus fuit in generationibus suis ; cum Deo ambulavit.
     EN: These are Noah's generations. Noah was a righteous and blameless man among his generations; he walked with God.

6:10  LA: Et genuit tres filios, Sem, Cham et Japheth.
     EN: He fathered three sons: Shem, Ham, and Japheth.

6:11  LA: Corrupta est autem terra coram Deo, et repleta est iniquitate.
     EN: The earth was corrupt before God and filled with wickedness.

6:12  LA: Cumque vidisset Deus terram esse corruptam (omnis quippe caro corruperat viam suam super terram),
     EN: When God saw that the earth was corrupt—for all flesh had corrupted its way upon the earth—

6:13  LA: dixit ad Noë : Finis universæ carnis venit coram me : repleta est terra iniquitate a facie eorum, et ego disperdam eos cum terra.
     EN: He said to Noah, “The end of all flesh has come before Me. The earth is filled with wickedness because of them, and I will destroy them with the earth.

6:14  LA: Fac tibi arcam de lignis lævigatis ; mansiunculas in arca facies, et bitumine linies intrinsecus et extrinsecus.
     EN: Make yourself an ark of smooth timbers. Make compartments in the ark, and coat it inside and outside with bitumen.

6:15  LA: Et sic facies eam : trecentorum cubitorum erit longitudo arcæ, quinquaginta cubitorum latitudo, et triginta cubitorum altitudo illius.
     EN: This is how you shall make it: the ark's length shall be three hundred cubits, its width fifty cubits, and its height thirty cubits.

6:16  LA: Fenestram in arca facies, et in cubito consummabis summitatem ejus : ostium autem arcæ pones ex latere ; deorsum, cœnacula et tristega facies in ea.
     EN: Make a window in the ark, and finish its top within a cubit. Put the ark's door in its side, and make lower, second, and third stories in it.

6:17  LA: Ecce ego adducam aquas diluvii super terram, ut interficiam omnem carnem, in qua spiritus vitæ est subter cælum : universa quæ in terra sunt, consumentur.
     EN: See, I will bring floodwaters upon the earth to kill all flesh beneath heaven that has the breath of life. Everything upon the earth shall perish.

6:18  LA: Ponamque fœdus meum tecum : et ingredieris arcam tu et filii tui, uxor tua, et uxores filiorum tuorum tecum.
     EN: But I will establish My covenant with you. You shall enter the ark, you and your sons, your wife and your sons' wives with you.

6:19  LA: Et ex cunctis animantibus universæ carnis bina induces in arcam, ut vivant tecum : masculini sexus et feminini.
     EN: From every living creature of all flesh, bring two into the ark to live with you, male and female.

6:20  LA: De volucribus juxta genus suum, et de jumentis in genere suo, et ex omni reptili terræ secundum genus suum : bina de omnibus ingredientur tecum, ut possint vivere.
     EN: Of birds according to their kinds, of livestock according to their kinds, and of every creeping creature of the earth according to its kind, two of each shall enter with you, so that they may live.

6:21  LA: Tolles igitur tecum ex omnibus escis, quæ mandi possunt, et comportabis apud te : et erunt tam tibi, quam illis in cibum.
     EN: Take with you every kind of food that can be eaten, and gather it beside you; it shall be food for both you and them.”

6:22  LA: Fecit igitur Noë omnia quæ præceperat illi Deus.
     EN: Noah did everything God had commanded him.

GENESIS 7

7:1  LA: Dixitque Dominus ad eum : Ingredere tu et omnis domus tua in arcam : te enim vidi justum coram me in generatione hac.
     EN: The Lord said to him, “Enter the ark, you and all your household, for I have seen you to be righteous before Me in this generation.

7:2  LA: Ex omnibus animantibus mundis tolle septena et septena, masculum et feminam : de animantibus vero immundis duo et duo, masculum et feminam.
     EN: Of all clean animals take seven and seven, male and female; but of unclean animals two and two, male and female.

7:3  LA: Sed et de volatilibus cæli septena et septena, masculum et feminam : ut salvetur semen super faciem universæ terræ.
     EN: Also of the birds of heaven take seven and seven, male and female, so that offspring may be preserved upon the face of the whole earth.

7:4  LA: Adhuc enim, et post dies septem ego pluam super terram quadraginta diebus et quadraginta noctibus : et delebo omnem substantiam, quam feci, de superficie terræ.
     EN: For in seven days I will send rain upon the earth for forty days and forty nights, and I will wipe away from the surface of the earth every being I have made.”

7:5  LA: Fecit ergo Noë omnia quæ mandaverat ei Dominus.
     EN: Noah did everything the Lord had commanded him.

7:6  LA: Eratque sexcentorum annorum quando diluvii aquæ inundaverunt super terram.
     EN: He was six hundred years old when the floodwaters inundated the earth.

7:7  LA: Et ingressus est Noë et filii ejus, uxor ejus et uxores filiorum ejus cum eo in arcam propter aquas diluvii.
     EN: Noah entered the ark with his sons, his wife, and his sons' wives, because of the floodwaters.

7:8  LA: De animantibus quoque mundis et immundis, et de volucribus, et ex omni quod movetur super terram,
     EN: Of the clean and unclean animals, the birds, and everything that moves upon the earth,

7:9  LA: duo et duo ingressa sunt ad Noë in arcam, masculus et femina, sicut præceperat Dominus Noë.
     EN: two and two entered the ark with Noah, male and female, as the Lord had commanded Noah.

7:10  LA: Cumque transissent septem dies, aquæ diluvii inundaverunt super terram.
     EN: When seven days had passed, the floodwaters inundated the earth.

7:11  LA: Anno sexcentesimo vitæ Noë, mense secundo, septimodecimo die mensis, rupti sunt omnes fontes abyssi magnæ, et cataractæ cæli apertæ sunt :
     EN: In the six hundredth year of Noah's life, in the second month, on the seventeenth day of the month, all the springs of the great deep burst open, and the floodgates of heaven were opened.

7:12  LA: et facta est pluvia super terram quadraginta diebus et quadraginta noctibus.
     EN: Rain fell upon the earth for forty days and forty nights.

7:13  LA: In articulo diei illius ingressus est Noë, et Sem, et Cham, et Japheth filii ejus ; uxor illius, et tres uxores filiorum ejus cum eis in arcam :
     EN: On that very day Noah entered the ark, with his sons Shem, Ham, and Japheth, his wife, and the three wives of his sons.

7:14  LA: ipsi et omne animal secundum genus suum, universaque jumenta in genere suo, et omne quod movetur super terram in genere suo, cunctumque volatile secundum genus suum, universæ aves, omnesque volucres,
     EN: They and every wild animal according to its kind, all livestock according to their kinds, everything that moves upon the earth according to its kind, and every winged creature according to its kind, all birds and all flying creatures,

7:15  LA: ingressæ sunt ad Noë in arcam, bina et bina ex omni carne, in qua erat spiritus vitæ.
     EN: entered the ark with Noah, two and two of all flesh that had the breath of life.

7:16  LA: Et quæ ingressa sunt, masculus et femina ex omni carne introierunt, sicut præceperat ei Deus : et inclusit eum Dominus deforis.
     EN: Those that entered went in male and female of all flesh, as God had commanded him; and the Lord shut him in from outside.

7:17  LA: Factumque est diluvium quadraginta diebus super terram : et multiplicatæ sunt aquæ, et elevaverunt arcam in sublime a terra.
     EN: The flood came upon the earth for forty days. The waters increased and lifted the ark high above the earth.

7:18  LA: Vehementer enim inundaverunt, et omnia repleverunt in superficie terræ : porro arca ferebatur super aquas.
     EN: They flooded mightily and filled everything upon the surface of the earth, while the ark floated upon the waters.

7:19  LA: Et aquæ prævaluerunt nimis super terram : opertique sunt omnes montes excelsi sub universo cælo.
     EN: The waters grew exceedingly strong upon the earth, and all the high mountains beneath the whole heaven were covered.

7:20  LA: Quindecim cubitis altior fuit aqua super montes, quos operuerat.
     EN: The water rose fifteen cubits above the mountains it covered.

7:21  LA: Consumptaque est omnis caro quæ movebatur super terram, volucrum, animantium, bestiarum, omniumque reptilium, quæ reptant super terram : universi homines,
     EN: All flesh that moved upon the earth perished: birds, animals, wild beasts, and all creeping creatures that creep upon the earth, and all human beings.

7:22  LA: et cuncta, in quibus spiraculum vitæ est in terra, mortua sunt.
     EN: Everything upon the land that had the breath of life died.

7:23  LA: Et delevit omnem substantiam quæ erat super terram, ab homine usque ad pecus, tam reptile quam volucres cæli : et deleta sunt de terra. Remansit autem solus Noë, et qui cum eo erant in arca.
     EN: He wiped away every being upon the earth, from human beings to cattle, creeping creatures and birds of heaven; they were wiped from the earth. Only Noah remained, and those with him in the ark.

7:24  LA: Obtinueruntque aquæ terram centum quinquaginta diebus.
     EN: The waters held the earth for one hundred fifty days.

GENESIS 8

8:1  LA: Recordatus autem Deus Noë, cunctorumque animantium, et omnium jumentorum, quæ erant cum eo in arca, adduxit spiritum super terram, et imminutæ sunt aquæ.
     EN: God remembered Noah and all the wild animals and livestock with him in the ark. He brought a wind over the earth, and the waters diminished.

8:2  LA: Et clausi sunt fontes abyssi, et cataractæ cæli : et prohibitæ sunt pluviæ de cælo.
     EN: The springs of the deep and the floodgates of heaven were closed, and rain from heaven was restrained.

8:3  LA: Reversæque sunt aquæ de terra euntes et redeuntes : et cœperunt minui post centum quinquaginta dies.
     EN: The waters receded from the earth, flowing back and forth; after one hundred fifty days they began to diminish.

8:4  LA: Requievitque arca mense septimo, vigesimo septimo die mensis, super montes Armeniæ.
     EN: In the seventh month, on the twenty-seventh day of the month, the ark rested upon the mountains of Armenia.

8:5  LA: At vero aquæ ibant et decrescebant usque ad decimum mensem : decimo enim mense, primo die mensis, apparuerunt cacumina montium.
     EN: The waters continued to decrease until the tenth month. In the tenth month, on the first day of the month, the mountain peaks appeared.

8:6  LA: Cumque transissent quadraginta dies, aperiens Noë fenestram arcæ, quam fecerat, dimisit corvum,
     EN: When forty days had passed, Noah opened the window he had made in the ark and released a raven,

8:7  LA: qui egrediebatur, et non revertebatur, donec siccarentur aquæ super terram.
     EN: which went out and did not return until the waters upon the earth had dried up.

8:8  LA: Emisit quoque columbam post eum, ut videret si jam cessassent aquæ super faciem terræ.
     EN: He also sent out a dove after it, to see whether the waters had now subsided from the face of the earth.

8:9  LA: Quæ cum non invenisset ubi requiesceret pes ejus, reversa est ad eum in arcam : aquæ enim erant super universam terram : extenditque manum, et apprehensam intulit in arcam.
     EN: Finding no place to rest its foot, it returned to him in the ark, for water lay over the whole earth. He stretched out his hand, took it, and brought it into the ark.

8:10  LA: Expectatis autem ultra septem diebus aliis, rursum dimisit columbam ex arca.
     EN: After waiting another seven days, he again released the dove from the ark.

8:11  LA: At illa venit ad eum ad vesperam, portans ramum olivæ virentibus foliis in ore suo : intellexit ergo Noë quod cessassent aquæ super terram.
     EN: It came to him in the evening, carrying in its beak an olive branch with green leaves. Noah understood that the waters upon the earth had subsided.

8:12  LA: Expectavitque nihilominus septem alios dies : et emisit columbam, quæ non est reversa ultra ad eum.
     EN: He waited another seven days and sent out the dove, which no longer returned to him.

8:13  LA: Igitur sexcentesimo primo anno, primo mense, prima die mensis, imminutæ sunt aquæ super terram : et aperiens Noë tectum arcæ, aspexit, viditque quod exsiccata esset superficies terræ.
     EN: In the six hundred first year, in the first month, on the first day of the month, the waters upon the earth had diminished. Noah opened the ark's roof, looked out, and saw that the surface of the ground was dry.

8:14  LA: Mense secundo, septimo et vigesimo die mensis arefacta est terra.
     EN: In the second month, on the twenty-seventh day of the month, the earth was dry.

8:15  LA: Locutus est autem Deus ad Noë, dicens :
     EN: God spoke to Noah, saying,

8:16  LA: Egredere de arca, tu et uxor tua, filii tui et uxores filiorum tuorum tecum.
     EN: “Come out of the ark, you and your wife, your sons and your sons' wives with you.

8:17  LA: Cuncta animantia, quæ sunt apud te, ex omni carne, tam in volatilibus quam in bestiis et universis reptilibus, quæ reptant super terram, educ tecum, et ingredimini super terram : crescite et multiplicamini super eam.
     EN: Bring out with you every living creature of all flesh that is with you—birds, animals, and all creeping creatures that creep upon the earth. Go forth upon the earth; increase and multiply upon it.”

8:18  LA: Egressus est ergo Noë, et filii ejus : uxor illius, et uxores filiorum ejus cum eo.
     EN: Noah came out with his sons, his wife, and his sons' wives.

8:19  LA: Sed et omnia animantia, jumenta, et reptilia quæ reptant super terram, secundum genus suum, egressa sunt de arca.
     EN: All the wild animals, livestock, and creeping creatures that creep upon the earth also came out of the ark according to their kinds.

8:20  LA: Ædificavit autem Noë altare Domino : et tollens de cunctis pecoribus et volucribus mundis, obtulit holocausta super altare.
     EN: Noah built an altar to the Lord. Taking from all the clean animals and birds, he offered whole burnt offerings upon the altar.

8:21  LA: Odoratusque est Dominus odorem suavitatis, et ait : Nequaquam ultra maledicam terræ propter homines : sensus enim et cogitatio humani cordis in malum prona sunt ab adolescentia sua : non igitur ultra percutiam omnem animam viventem sicut feci.
     EN: The Lord smelled the pleasing fragrance and said, “Never again will I curse the earth because of human beings; for the inclinations and thoughts of the human heart are prone to evil from youth. Therefore I will never again strike down every living creature as I have done.

8:22  LA: Cunctis diebus terræ, sementis et messis, frigus et æstus, æstas et hiems, nox et dies non requiescent.
     EN: For all the days of the earth, sowing and harvest, cold and heat, summer and winter, night and day shall not cease.”

GENESIS 9

9:1  LA: Benedixitque Deus Noë et filiis ejus. Et dixit ad eos : Crescite, et multiplicamini, et replete terram.
     EN: God blessed Noah and his sons and said to them, “Increase and multiply, and fill the earth.

9:2  LA: Et terror vester ac tremor sit super cuncta animalia terræ, et super omnes volucres cæli, cum universis quæ moventur super terram : omnes pisces maris manui vestræ traditi sunt.
     EN: Let the fear and dread of you fall upon every animal of the earth and every bird of heaven, with everything that moves upon the earth. All the fish of the sea are delivered into your hands.

9:3  LA: Et omne, quod movetur et vivit, erit vobis in cibum : quasi olera virentia tradidi vobis omnia.
     EN: Everything that moves and lives shall be food for you. As with green plants, I have given you everything,

9:4  LA: Excepto, quod carnem cum sanguine non comedetis.
     EN: except that you shall not eat flesh with blood.

9:5  LA: Sanguinem enim animarum vestrarum requiram de manu cunctarum bestiarum : et de manu hominis, de manu viri, et fratris ejus requiram animam hominis.
     EN: For the blood of your lives I will require from the hand of every beast; and from the hand of man, from the hand of a man and his brother, I will require a human life.

9:6  LA: Quicumque effuderit humanum sanguinem, fundetur sanguis illius : ad imaginem quippe Dei factus est homo.
     EN: Whoever sheds human blood, his blood shall be shed; for the human being was made in the image of God.

9:7  LA: Vos autem crescite et multiplicamini, et ingredimini super terram, et implete eam.
     EN: But you, increase and multiply; go forth upon the earth and fill it.”

9:8  LA: Hæc quoque dixit Deus ad Noë, et ad filios ejus cum eo :
     EN: God also said to Noah and his sons with him,

9:9  LA: Ecce ego statuam pactum meum vobiscum, et cum semine vestro post vos :
     EN: “See, I will establish My covenant with you and with your offspring after you,

9:10  LA: et ad omnem animam viventem, quæ est vobiscum, tam in volucribus quam in jumentis et pecudibus terræ cunctis, quæ egressa sunt de arca, et universis bestiis terræ.
     EN: and with every living creature with you—birds, livestock, and all the animals of the earth that came out of the ark, and all the wild animals of the earth.

9:11  LA: Statuam pactum meum vobiscum, et nequaquam ultra interficietur omnis caro aquis diluvii, neque erit deinceps diluvium dissipans terram.
     EN: I will establish My covenant with you: never again shall all flesh be killed by floodwaters, nor shall there again be a flood destroying the earth.”

9:12  LA: Dixitque Deus : Hoc signum fœderis quod do inter me et vos, et ad omnem animam viventem, quæ est vobiscum in generationes sempiternas :
     EN: God said, “This is the sign of the covenant I give between Me and you and every living creature with you for everlasting generations:

9:13  LA: arcum meum ponam in nubibus, et erit signum fœderis inter me et inter terram.
     EN: I will place My bow in the clouds, and it shall be a sign of the covenant between Me and the earth.

9:14  LA: Cumque obduxero nubibus cælum, apparebit arcus meus in nubibus :
     EN: When I cover heaven with clouds, My bow shall appear in the clouds,

9:15  LA: et recordabor fœderis mei vobiscum, et cum omni anima vivente quæ carnem vegetat : et non erunt ultra aquæ diluvii ad delendum universam carnem.
     EN: and I will remember My covenant with you and with every living soul that gives life to flesh. Never again shall floodwaters destroy all flesh.

9:16  LA: Eritque arcus in nubibus, et videbo illum, et recordabor fœderis sempiterni quod pactum est inter Deum et omnem animam viventem universæ carnis quæ est super terram.
     EN: The bow shall be in the clouds, and I will see it and remember the everlasting covenant established between God and every living creature of all flesh upon the earth.”

9:17  LA: Dixitque Deus ad Noë : Hoc erit signum fœderis, quod constitui inter me et omnem carnem super terram.
     EN: God said to Noah, “This shall be the sign of the covenant I have established between Me and all flesh upon the earth.”

9:18  LA: Erant ergo filii Noë, qui egressi sunt de arca, Sem, Cham et Japheth : porro Cham ipse est pater Chanaan.
     EN: The sons of Noah who came out of the ark were Shem, Ham, and Japheth. Ham was the father of Canaan.

9:19  LA: Tres isti filii sunt Noë : et ab his disseminatum est omne genus hominum super universam terram.
     EN: These three are Noah's sons, and from them the whole human race spread over all the earth.

9:20  LA: Cœpitque Noë vir agricola exercere terram, et plantavit vineam.
     EN: Noah, a cultivator, began to work the ground and planted a vineyard.

9:21  LA: Bibensque vinum inebriatus est, et nudatus in tabernaculo suo.
     EN: He drank wine, became drunk, and lay uncovered in his tent.

9:22  LA: Quod cum vidisset Cham, pater Chanaan, verenda scilicet patris sui esse nudata, nuntiavit duobus fratribus suis foras.
     EN: Ham, Canaan's father, saw that his father's private parts were uncovered, and told his two brothers outside.

9:23  LA: At vero Sem et Japheth pallium imposuerunt humeris suis, et incedentes retrorsum, operuerunt verenda patris sui : faciesque eorum aversæ erant, et patris virilia non viderunt.
     EN: But Shem and Japheth laid a cloak upon their shoulders, walked backward, and covered their father's private parts. Their faces were turned away, and they did not see their father's nakedness.

9:24  LA: Evigilans autem Noë ex vino, cum didicisset quæ fecerat ei filius suus minor,
     EN: When Noah awoke from his wine and learned what his younger son had done to him,

9:25  LA: ait : [Maledictus Chanaan, servus servorum erit fratribus suis.]
     EN: he said, “Cursed be Canaan; a slave of slaves shall he be to his brothers.”

9:26  LA: Dixitque : [Benedictus Dominus Deus Sem, sit Chanaan servus ejus.
     EN: He also said, “Blessed be the Lord, the God of Shem; let Canaan be his slave.

9:27  LA: Dilatet Deus Japheth, et habitet in tabernaculis Sem, sitque Chanaan servus ejus.]
     EN: May God enlarge Japheth, and may he dwell in the tents of Shem; and let Canaan be his slave.”

9:28  LA: Vixit autem Noë post diluvium trecentis quinquaginta annis.
     EN: After the flood Noah lived three hundred fifty years.

9:29  LA: Et impleti sunt omnes dies ejus nongentorum quinquaginta annorum : et mortuus est.
     EN: All his days came to nine hundred fifty years, and he died.

GENESIS 10

10:1  LA: Hæ sunt generationes filiorum Noë, Sem, Cham et Japheth : natique sunt eis filii post diluvium.
     EN: These are the generations of Noah's sons, Shem, Ham, and Japheth. Sons were born to them after the flood.

10:2  LA: Filii Japheth : Gomer, et Magog, et Madai, et Javan, et Thubal, et Mosoch, et Thiras.
     EN: Japheth's sons were Gomer, Magog, Madai, Javan, Tubal, Meshech, and Tiras.

10:3  LA: Porro filii Gomer : Ascenez et Riphath et Thogorma.
     EN: Gomer's sons were Ashkenaz, Riphath, and Togarmah.

10:4  LA: Filii autem Javan : Elisa et Tharsis, Cetthim et Dodanim.
     EN: Javan's sons were Elishah, Tarshish, Kittim, and Dodanim.

10:5  LA: Ab his divisæ sunt insulæ gentium in regionibus suis, unusquisque secundum linguam suam et familias suas in nationibus suis.
     EN: From these the islands of the nations were divided into their regions, each according to its language and its families among its nations.

10:6  LA: Filii autem Cham : Chus, et Mesraim, et Phuth, et Chanaan.
     EN: Ham's sons were Cush, Mizraim, Put, and Canaan.

10:7  LA: Filii Chus : Saba, et Hevila, et Sabatha, et Regma, et Sabatacha. Filii Regma : Saba et Dadan.
     EN: Cush's sons were Seba, Havilah, Sabtah, Raamah, and Sabteca. Raamah's sons were Sheba and Dedan.

10:8  LA: Porro Chus genuit Nemrod : ipse cœpit esse potens in terra,
     EN: Cush fathered Nimrod, who began to be mighty upon the earth.

10:9  LA: et erat robustus venator coram Domino. Ob hoc exivit proverbium : Quasi Nemrod robustus venator coram Domino.
     EN: He was a mighty hunter before the Lord. Hence the saying arose, “Like Nimrod, a mighty hunter before the Lord.”

10:10  LA: Fuit autem principium regni ejus Babylon, et Arach et Achad, et Chalanne, in terra Sennaar.
     EN: The beginning of his kingdom was Babylon, Erech, Accad, and Calneh, in the land of Shinar.

10:11  LA: De terra illa egressus est Assur, et ædificavit Niniven, et plateas civitatis, et Chale.
     EN: Asshur went out from that land and built Nineveh, the city's streets, and Calah,

10:12  LA: Resen quoque inter Niniven et Chale : hæc est civitas magna.
     EN: and also Resen, between Nineveh and Calah; this is the great city.

10:13  LA: At vero Mesraim genuit Ludim, et Anamim et Laabim, Nephthuim,
     EN: Mizraim fathered the Ludim, Anamim, Lehabim, Naphtuhim,

10:14  LA: et Phetrusim, et Chasluim : de quibus egressi sunt Philisthiim et Caphtorim.
     EN: Pathrusim, and Casluhim, from whom came the Philistines and Caphtorim.

10:15  LA: Chanaan autem genuit Sidonem primogenitum suum. Hethæum,
     EN: Canaan fathered Sidon his firstborn, the Hittite,

10:16  LA: et Jebusæum, et Amorrhæum, Gergesæum,
     EN: the Jebusite, the Amorite, the Girgashite,

10:17  LA: Hevæum, et Aracæum : Sinæum,
     EN: the Hivite, the Arkite, the Sinite,

10:18  LA: et Aradium, Samaræum, et Amathæum : et post hæc disseminati sunt populi Chananæorum.
     EN: the Arvadite, the Zemarite, and the Hamathite. After this the Canaanite peoples spread abroad.

10:19  LA: Factique sunt termini Chanaan venientibus a Sidone Geraram usque Gazam, donec ingrediaris Sodomam et Gomorrham, et Adamam, et Seboim usque Lesa.
     EN: Canaan's boundaries extended from Sidon toward Gerar as far as Gaza, and toward Sodom, Gomorrah, Admah, and Zeboiim, as far as Lasha.

10:20  LA: Hi sunt filii Cham in cognationibus, et linguis, et generationibus, terrisque et gentibus suis.
     EN: These are Ham's sons according to their families, languages, generations, lands, and nations.

10:21  LA: De Sem quoque nati sunt, patre omnium filiorum Heber, fratre Japheth majore.
     EN: Sons were also born to Shem, father of all Eber's children and elder brother of Japheth.

10:22  LA: Filii Sem : Ælam, et Assur, et Arphaxad, et Lud, et Aram.
     EN: Shem's sons were Elam, Asshur, Arpachshad, Lud, and Aram.

10:23  LA: Filii Aram : Us, et Hul, et Gether, et Mes.
     EN: Aram's sons were Uz, Hul, Gether, and Mash.

10:24  LA: At vero Arphaxad genuit Sale, de quo ortus est Heber.
     EN: Arpachshad fathered Shelah, from whom Eber was born.

10:25  LA: Natique sunt Heber filii duo : nomen uni Phaleg, eo quod in diebus ejus divisa sit terra : et nomen fratris ejus Jectan.
     EN: Two sons were born to Eber. One was named Peleg, because in his days the earth was divided; his brother's name was Joktan.

10:26  LA: Qui Jectan genuit Elmodad, et Saleph, et Asarmoth, Jare,
     EN: Joktan fathered Almodad, Sheleph, Hazarmaveth, Jerah,

10:27  LA: et Aduram, et Uzal, et Decla,
     EN: Hadoram, Uzal, Diklah,

10:28  LA: et Ebal, et Abimaël, Saba,
     EN: Ebal, Abimael, Sheba,

10:29  LA: et Ophir, et Hevila, et Jobab : omnes isti, filii Jectan.
     EN: Ophir, Havilah, and Jobab. All these were Joktan's sons.

10:30  LA: Et facta est habitatio eorum de Messa pergentibus usque Sephar montem orientalem.
     EN: Their dwelling extended from Mesha toward Sephar, the eastern mountain.

10:31  LA: Isti filii Sem secundum cognationes, et linguas, et regiones in gentibus suis.
     EN: These are Shem's sons according to their families, languages, and regions among their nations.

10:32  LA: Hæ familiæ Noë juxta populos et nationes suas. Ab his divisæ sunt gentes in terra post diluvium.
     EN: These are Noah's families according to their peoples and nations. From these the nations were divided upon the earth after the flood.

GENESIS 11

11:1  LA: Erat autem terra labii unius, et sermonum eorumdem.
     EN: The earth had one language and the same words.

11:2  LA: Cumque proficiscerentur de oriente, invenerunt campum in terra Sennaar, et habitaverunt in eo.
     EN: As they journeyed from the east, they found a plain in the land of Shinar and settled there.

11:3  LA: Dixitque alter ad proximum suum : Venite, faciamus lateres, et coquamus eos igni. Habueruntque lateres pro saxis, et bitumen pro cæmento :
     EN: They said to one another, “Come, let us make bricks and bake them with fire.” They used bricks for stones and bitumen for mortar.

11:4  LA: et dixerunt : Venite, faciamus nobis civitatem et turrim, cujus culmen pertingat ad cælum : et celebremus nomen nostrum antequam dividamur in universas terras.
     EN: They said, “Come, let us build ourselves a city and a tower whose summit reaches heaven, and make our name famous before we are scattered throughout all lands.”

11:5  LA: Descendit autem Dominus ut videret civitatem et turrim, quam ædificabant filii Adam,
     EN: The Lord came down to see the city and tower that the children of Adam were building.

11:6  LA: et dixit : Ecce, unus est populus, et unum labium omnibus : cœperuntque hoc facere, nec desistent a cogitationibus suis, donec eas opere compleant.
     EN: He said, “See, they are one people, and all have one language. They have begun to do this, and they will not abandon their plans until they accomplish them in deed.

11:7  LA: Venite igitur, descendamus, et confundamus ibi linguam eorum, ut non audiat unusquisque vocem proximi sui.
     EN: Come, therefore, let Us go down and confuse their language there, so that none of them may understand the speech of his neighbor.”

11:8  LA: Atque ita divisit eos Dominus ex illo loco in universas terras, et cessaverunt ædificare civitatem.
     EN: Thus the Lord scattered them from that place throughout all lands, and they stopped building the city.

11:9  LA: Et idcirco vocatum est nomen ejus Babel, quia ibi confusum est labium universæ terræ : et inde dispersit eos Dominus super faciem cunctarum regionum.
     EN: For this reason it was named Babel, because there the language of the whole earth was confused, and from there the Lord scattered them over the face of every region.

11:10  LA: Hæ sunt generationes Sem : Sem erat centum annorum quando genuit Arphaxad, biennio post diluvium.
     EN: These are Shem's generations. Shem was one hundred years old when he fathered Arpachshad, two years after the flood.

11:11  LA: Vixitque Sem, postquam genuit Arphaxad, quingentis annis : et genuit filios et filias.
     EN: After fathering Arpachshad, Shem lived five hundred years and fathered sons and daughters.

11:12  LA: Porro Arphaxad vixit triginta quinque annis, et genuit Sale.
     EN: Arpachshad lived thirty-five years and fathered Shelah.

11:13  LA: Vixitque Arphaxad, postquam genuit Sale, trecentis tribus annis : et genuit filios et filias.
     EN: After fathering Shelah, Arpachshad lived three hundred three years and fathered sons and daughters.

11:14  LA: Sale quoque vixit triginta annis, et genuit Heber.
     EN: Shelah lived thirty years and fathered Eber.

11:15  LA: Vixitque Sale, postquam genuit Heber, quadringentis tribus annis : et genuit filios et filias.
     EN: After fathering Eber, Shelah lived four hundred three years and fathered sons and daughters.

11:16  LA: Vixit autem Heber triginta quatuor annis, et genuit Phaleg.
     EN: Eber lived thirty-four years and fathered Peleg.

11:17  LA: Et vixit Heber postquam genuit Phaleg, quadringentis triginta annis : et genuit filios et filias.
     EN: After fathering Peleg, Eber lived four hundred thirty years and fathered sons and daughters.

11:18  LA: Vixit quoque Phaleg triginta annis, et genuit Reu.
     EN: Peleg lived thirty years and fathered Reu.

11:19  LA: Vixitque Phaleg, postquam genuit Reu, ducentis novem annis : et genuit filios et filias.
     EN: After fathering Reu, Peleg lived two hundred nine years and fathered sons and daughters.

11:20  LA: Vixit autem Reu triginta duobus annis, et genuit Sarug.
     EN: Reu lived thirty-two years and fathered Serug.

11:21  LA: Vixit quoque Reu, postquam genuit Sarug, ducentis septem annis : et genuit filios et filias.
     EN: After fathering Serug, Reu lived two hundred seven years and fathered sons and daughters.

11:22  LA: Vixit vero Sarug triginta annis, et genuit Nachor.
     EN: Serug lived thirty years and fathered Nahor.

11:23  LA: Vixitque Sarug, postquam genuit Nachor, ducentis annis : et genuit filios et filias.
     EN: After fathering Nahor, Serug lived two hundred years and fathered sons and daughters.

11:24  LA: Vixit autem Nachor viginti novem annis, et genuit Thare.
     EN: Nahor lived twenty-nine years and fathered Terah.

11:25  LA: Vixitque Nachor, postquam genuit Thare, centum decem et novem annis : et genuit filios et filias.
     EN: After fathering Terah, Nahor lived one hundred nineteen years and fathered sons and daughters.

11:26  LA: Vixitque Thare septuaginta annis, et genuit Abram, et Nachor, et Aran.
     EN: Terah lived seventy years and fathered Abram, Nahor, and Haran.

11:27  LA: Hæ sunt autem generationes Thare : Thare genuit Abram, Nachor et Aran. Porro Aran genuit Lot.
     EN: These are Terah's generations. Terah fathered Abram, Nahor, and Haran; and Haran fathered Lot.

11:28  LA: Mortuusque est Aran ante Thare patrem suum, in terra nativitatis suæ, in Ur Chaldæorum.
     EN: Haran died before his father Terah in the land of his birth, in Ur of the Chaldeans.

11:29  LA: Duxerunt autem Abram et Nachor uxores : nomen uxoris Abram, Sarai : et nomen uxoris Nachor, Melcha filia Aran, patris Melchæ, et patris Jeschæ.
     EN: Abram and Nahor took wives. Abram's wife was named Sarai, and Nahor's wife was named Milcah, daughter of Haran, the father of Milcah and Iscah.

11:30  LA: Erat autem Sarai sterilis, nec habebat liberos.
     EN: Sarai was barren and had no children.

11:31  LA: Tulit itaque Thare Abram filium suum, et Lot filium Aran, filium filii sui, et Sarai nurum suam, uxorem Abram filii sui, et eduxit eos de Ur Chaldæorum, ut irent in terram Chanaan : veneruntque usque Haran, et habitaverunt ibi.
     EN: Terah took his son Abram, his grandson Lot the son of Haran, and his daughter-in-law Sarai, the wife of his son Abram, and led them out of Ur of the Chaldeans to go to the land of Canaan. They came as far as Haran and settled there.

11:32  LA: Et facti sunt dies Thare ducentorum quinque annorum, et mortuus est in Haran.
     EN: Terah's days were two hundred five years, and he died in Haran.

GENESIS 12

12:1  LA: Dixit autem Dominus ad Abram : Egredere de terra tua, et de cognatione tua, et de domo patris tui, et veni in terram quam monstrabo tibi.
     EN: The Lord said to Abram, “Go out from your land, your kindred, and your father's house, and come into the land I will show you.

12:2  LA: Faciamque te in gentem magnam, et benedicam tibi, et magnificabo nomen tuum, erisque benedictus.
     EN: I will make you into a great nation, bless you, and make your name great, and you shall be blessed.

12:3  LA: Benedicam benedicentibus tibi, et maledicam maledicentibus tibi, atque in te benedicentur universæ cognationes terræ.
     EN: I will bless those who bless you and curse those who curse you, and in you all the families of the earth shall be blessed.”

12:4  LA: Egressus est itaque Abram sicut præceperat ei Dominus, et ivit cum eo Lot : septuaginta quinque annorum erat Abram cum egrederetur de Haran.
     EN: Abram went out as the Lord had commanded him, and Lot went with him. Abram was seventy-five years old when he left Haran.

12:5  LA: Tulitque Sarai uxorem suam, et Lot filium fratris sui, universamque substantiam quam possederant, et animas quas fecerant in Haran : et egressi sunt ut irent in terram Chanaan. Cumque venissent in eam,
     EN: He took his wife Sarai, his brother's son Lot, all the possessions they had acquired, and the people they had gathered in Haran, and they set out for the land of Canaan. When they arrived there,

12:6  LA: pertransivit Abram terram usque ad locum Sichem, usque ad convallem illustrem : Chananæus autem tunc erat in terra.
     EN: Abram passed through the land as far as the place of Shechem, as far as the renowned valley. At that time the Canaanite was in the land.

12:7  LA: Apparuit autem Dominus Abram, et dixit ei : Semini tuo dabo terram hanc. Qui ædificavit ibi altare Domino, qui apparuerat ei.
     EN: The Lord appeared to Abram and said to him, “To your offspring I will give this land.” He built an altar there to the Lord who had appeared to him.

12:8  LA: Et inde transgrediens ad montem, qui erat contra orientem Bethel, tetendit ibi tabernaculum suum, ab occidente habens Bethel, et ab oriente Hai : ædificavit quoque ibi altare Domino, et invocavit nomen ejus.
     EN: From there he moved to the mountain east of Bethel and pitched his tent there, with Bethel to the west and Ai to the east. There also he built an altar to the Lord and called upon His name.

12:9  LA: Perrexitque Abram vadens, et ultra progrediens ad meridiem.
     EN: Abram journeyed onward, advancing farther toward the south.

12:10  LA: Facta est autem fames in terra : descenditque Abram in Ægyptum, ut peregrinaretur ibi : prævaluerat enim fames in terra.
     EN: A famine arose in the land. Abram went down to Egypt to stay there, for the famine had become severe in the land.

12:11  LA: Cumque prope esset ut ingrederetur Ægyptum, dixit Sarai uxori suæ : Novi quod pulchra sis mulier :
     EN: As he was about to enter Egypt, he said to his wife Sarai, “I know you are a beautiful woman.

12:12  LA: et quod cum viderint te Ægyptii, dicturi sunt : Uxor ipsius est : et interficient me, et te reservabunt.
     EN: When the Egyptians see you, they will say, ‘She is his wife,’ and they will kill me but keep you alive.

12:13  LA: Dic ergo, obsecro te, quod soror mea sis : ut bene sit mihi propter te, et vivat anima mea ob gratiam tui.
     EN: Please say that you are my sister, so that it may go well with me because of you, and my life may be spared for your sake.”

12:14  LA: Cum itaque ingressus esset Abram Ægyptum, viderunt Ægyptii mulierem quod esset pulchra nimis.
     EN: When Abram entered Egypt, the Egyptians saw that the woman was very beautiful.

12:15  LA: Et nuntiaverunt principes Pharaoni, et laudaverunt eam apud illum : et sublata est mulier in domum Pharaonis.
     EN: The princes reported her to Pharaoh and praised her before him, and the woman was taken into Pharaoh's house.

12:16  LA: Abram vero bene usi sunt propter illam : fueruntque ei oves et boves et asini, et servi et famulæ, et asinæ et cameli.
     EN: They treated Abram well because of her, and he had sheep, oxen, male donkeys, male and female servants, female donkeys, and camels.

12:17  LA: Flagellavit autem Dominus Pharaonem plagis maximis, et domum ejus, propter Sarai uxorem Abram.
     EN: But the Lord struck Pharaoh and his house with very great plagues because of Sarai, Abram's wife.

12:18  LA: Vocavitque Pharao Abram, et dixit ei : Quidnam est hoc quod fecisti mihi ? quare non indicasti quod uxor tua esset ?
     EN: Pharaoh summoned Abram and said to him, “What is this you have done to me? Why did you not tell me that she was your wife?

12:19  LA: quam ob causam dixisti esse sororem tuam, ut tollerem eam mihi in uxorem ? Nunc igitur ecce conjux tua, accipe eam, et vade.
     EN: Why did you say she was your sister, so that I took her as my wife? Now, here is your wife; take her and go.”

12:20  LA: Præcepitque Pharao super Abram viris : et deduxerunt eum, et uxorem illius, et omnia quæ habebat.
     EN: Pharaoh gave his men orders concerning Abram, and they escorted him away with his wife and everything he had.

GENESIS 13

13:1  LA: Ascendit ergo Abram de Ægypto, ipse et uxor ejus, et omnia quæ habebat, et Lot cum eo, ad australem plagam.
     EN: Abram went up from Egypt into the southern region, with his wife, everything he had, and Lot with him.

13:2  LA: Erat autem dives valde in possessione auri et argenti.
     EN: He was very rich in possessions of gold and silver.

13:3  LA: Reversusque est per iter, quo venerat, a meridie in Bethel, usque ad locum ubi prius fixerat tabernaculum inter Bethel et Hai,
     EN: He returned along the way he had come, from the south to Bethel, as far as the place where he had first pitched his tent between Bethel and Ai,

13:4  LA: in loco altaris quod fecerat prius : et invocavit ibi nomen Domini.
     EN: at the site of the altar he had made before. There he called upon the name of the Lord.

13:5  LA: Sed et Lot qui erat cum Abram, fuerunt greges ovium, et armenta, et tabernacula.
     EN: Lot, who was with Abram, also had flocks of sheep, herds, and tents.

13:6  LA: Nec poterat eos capere terra, ut habitarent simul : erat quippe substantia eorum multa, et nequibant habitare communiter.
     EN: The land could not support them dwelling together, for their possessions were great, and they could not live in the same place.

13:7  LA: Unde et facta est rixa inter pastores gregum Abram et Lot. Eo autem tempore Chananæus et Pherezæus habitabant in terra illa.
     EN: So a quarrel arose between the herdsmen of Abram and Lot. At that time the Canaanite and the Perizzite lived in that land.

13:8  LA: Dixit ergo Abram ad Lot : Ne quæso sit jurgium inter me et te, et inter pastores meos et pastores tuos : fratres enim sumus.
     EN: Abram said to Lot, “Please let there be no strife between me and you, or between my herdsmen and yours, for we are brothers.

13:9  LA: Ecce universa terra coram te est : recede a me, obsecro : si ad sinistram ieris, ego dexteram tenebo : si tu dexteram elegeris, ego ad sinistram pergam.
     EN: See, the whole land lies before you. Please separate from me. If you go left, I will take the right; if you choose the right, I will go left.”

13:10  LA: Elevatis itaque Lot oculis, vidit omnem circa regionem Jordanis, quæ universa irrigabatur antequam subverteret Dominus Sodomam et Gomorrham, sicut paradisus Domini, et sicut Ægyptus venientibus in Segor.
     EN: Lot raised his eyes and saw the whole region around the Jordan. Before the Lord destroyed Sodom and Gomorrah, it was all well watered, like the garden of the Lord and like Egypt as one approaches Zoar.

13:11  LA: Elegitque sibi Lot regionem circa Jordanem, et recessit ab oriente : divisique sunt alterutrum a fratre suo.
     EN: Lot chose for himself the region around the Jordan and departed from the east. Thus each separated from his brother.

13:12  LA: Abram habitavit in terra Chanaan ; Lot vero moratus est in oppidis, quæ erant circa Jordanem, et habitavit in Sodomis.
     EN: Abram lived in the land of Canaan, while Lot stayed in the towns around the Jordan and lived in Sodom.

13:13  LA: Homines autem Sodomitæ pessimi erant, et peccatores coram Domino nimis.
     EN: The people of Sodom were very wicked, and exceedingly great sinners before the Lord.

13:14  LA: Dixitque Dominus ad Abram, postquam divisus est ab eo Lot : Leva oculos tuos et vide a loco, in quo nunc es, ad aquilonem et meridiem, ad orientem et occidentem.
     EN: After Lot had separated from him, the Lord said to Abram, “Raise your eyes and look from the place where you now are, north and south, east and west.

13:15  LA: Omnem terram, quam conspicis, tibi dabo, et semini tuo usque in sempiternum.
     EN: All the land you see I will give to you and to your offspring forever.

13:16  LA: Faciamque semen tuum sicut pulverem terræ : si quis potest hominum numerare pulverem terræ, semen quoque tuum numerare poterit.
     EN: I will make your offspring like the dust of the earth. If anyone can count the dust of the earth, then your offspring too can be counted.

13:17  LA: Surge, et perambula terram in longitudine et in latitudine sua : quia tibi daturus sum eam.
     EN: Rise and walk through the land in its length and breadth, for I will give it to you.”

13:18  LA: Movens igitur tabernaculum suum Abram, venit, et habitavit juxta convallem Mambre, quæ est in Hebron : ædificavitque ibi altare Domino.
     EN: Abram moved his tent, came, and settled beside the valley of Mamre, which is in Hebron. There he built an altar to the Lord.

GENESIS 14

14:1  LA: Factum est autem in illo tempore, ut Amraphel rex Sennaar, et Arioch rex Ponti, et Chodorlahomor rex Elamitarum, et Thadal rex gentium
     EN: At that time Amraphel king of Shinar, Arioch king of Pontus, Chedorlaomer king of the Elamites, and Tidal king of the nations

14:2  LA: inirent bellum contra Bara regem Sodomorum, et contra Bersa regem Gomorrhæ, et contra Sennaab regem Adamæ, et contra Semeber regem Seboim, contraque regem Balæ, ipsa est Segor.
     EN: made war against Bera king of Sodom, Birsha king of Gomorrah, Shinab king of Admah, Shemeber king of Zeboiim, and the king of Bela, which is Zoar.

14:3  LA: Omnes hi convenerunt in vallem Silvestrem, quæ nunc est mare salis.
     EN: All these gathered in the Wooded Valley, which is now the Salt Sea.

14:4  LA: Duodecim enim annis servierunt Chodorlahomor, et tertiodecimo anno recesserunt ab eo.
     EN: For twelve years they had served Chedorlaomer, but in the thirteenth year they rebelled against him.

14:5  LA: Igitur quartodecimo anno venit Chodorlahomor, et reges qui erant cum eo : percusseruntque Raphaim in Astarothcarnaim, et Zuzim cum eis, et Emim in Save Cariathaim,
     EN: In the fourteenth year Chedorlaomer came with the kings who were with him. They struck the Rephaim in Ashteroth-karnaim, the Zuzim with them, and the Emim in Shaveh-kiriathaim,

14:6  LA: et Chorræos in montibus Seir, usque ad Campestria Pharan, quæ est in solitudine.
     EN: and the Horites in the mountains of Seir as far as the Plains of Paran, which is in the wilderness.

14:7  LA: Reversique sunt, et venerunt ad fontem Misphat, ipsa est Cades : et percusserunt omnem regionem Amalecitarum, et Amorrhæum, qui habitabat in Asasonthamar.
