25:3  LA: Jecsan quoque genuit Saba et Dadan. Filii Dadan fuerunt Assurim, et Latusim, et Loomin.
     EN: Jokshan fathered Sheba and Dedan. Dedan's sons were Asshurim, Letushim, and Leummim.

25:4  LA: At vero ex Madian ortus est Epha, et Opher, et Henoch, et Abida, et Eldaa : omnes hi filii Ceturæ.
     EN: From Midian were born Ephah, Epher, Enoch, Abida, and Eldaah. All these were Keturah's sons.

25:5  LA: Deditque Abraham cuncta quæ possederat, Isaac :
     EN: Abraham gave Isaac everything he possessed.

25:6  LA: filiis autem concubinarum largitus est munera, et separavit eos ab Isaac filio suo, dum adhuc ipse viveret, ad plagam orientalem.
     EN: To the sons of his concubines he gave gifts, and while he was still alive he sent them away from his son Isaac toward the eastern region.

25:7  LA: Fuerunt autem dies vitæ Abrahæ, centum septuaginta quinque anni.
     EN: The days of Abraham's life were one hundred seventy-five years.

25:8  LA: Et deficiens mortuus est in senectute bona, provectæque ætatis et plenus dierum : congregatusque est ad populum suum.
     EN: He breathed his last and died in a good old age, advanced in years and full of days, and was gathered to his people.

25:9  LA: Et sepelierunt eum Isaac et Ismaël filii sui in spelunca duplici, quæ sita est in agro Ephron filii Seor Hethæi, e regione Mambre,
     EN: His sons Isaac and Ishmael buried him in the double cave situated in the field of Ephron son of Zohar the Hittite, opposite Mamre,

25:10  LA: quem emerat a filiis Heth : ibi sepultus est ipse, et Sara uxor ejus.
     EN: which he had bought from the sons of Heth. There he and his wife Sarah were buried.

25:11  LA: Et post obitum illius benedixit Deus Isaac filio ejus, qui habitabat juxta puteum nomine Viventis et videntis.
     EN: After his death, God blessed his son Isaac, who lived beside the well called The Living One Who Sees.

25:12  LA: Hæ sunt generationes Ismaël filii Abrahæ, quem peperit ei Agar Ægyptia, famula Saræ : et
     EN: These are the generations of Ishmael, Abraham's son, whom Hagar the Egyptian, Sarah's maidservant, bore him; and

25:13  LA: hæc nomina filiorum ejus in vocabulis et generationibus suis. Primogenitus Ismaëlis Nabaioth, deinde Cedar, et Adbeel, et Mabsam,
     EN: these are the names of his sons by their names and generations: Ishmael's firstborn Nebaioth, then Kedar, Adbeel, Mibsam,

25:14  LA: Masma quoque, et Duma, et Massa,
     EN: Mishma, Dumah, Massa,

25:15  LA: Hadar, et Thema, et Jethur, et Naphis, et Cedma.
     EN: Hadar, Tema, Jetur, Naphish, and Kedemah.

25:16  LA: Isti sunt filii Ismaëlis : et hæc nomina per castella et oppida eorum, duodecim principes tribuum suarum.
     EN: These are Ishmael's sons and their names by their settlements and towns, twelve princes of their tribes.

25:17  LA: Et facti sunt anni vitæ Ismaëlis centum triginta septem, deficiensque mortuus est, et appositus ad populum suum.
     EN: The years of Ishmael's life were one hundred thirty-seven. He breathed his last and died, and was joined to his people.

25:18  LA: Habitavit autem ab Hevila usque Sur, quæ respicit Ægyptum introëuntibus Assyrios ; coram cunctis fratribus suis obiit.
     EN: He lived from Havilah as far as Shur, which faces Egypt as one goes toward the Assyrians. He died in the presence of all his brothers.

25:19  LA: Hæ quoque sunt generationes Isaac filii Abraham : Abraham genuit Isaac :
     EN: These also are the generations of Isaac, Abraham's son. Abraham fathered Isaac,

25:20  LA: qui cum quadraginta esset annorum, duxit uxorem Rebeccam filiam Bathuelis Syri de Mesopotamia, sororem Laban.
     EN: who at forty years old married Rebekah, daughter of Bethuel the Syrian from Mesopotamia and sister of Laban.

25:21  LA: Deprecatusque est Isaac Dominum pro uxore sua, eo quod esset sterilis : qui exaudivit eum, et dedit conceptum Rebeccæ.
     EN: Isaac pleaded with the Lord for his wife, because she was barren. He heard him and granted Rebekah conception.

25:22  LA: Sed collidebantur in utero ejus parvuli ; quæ ait : Si sic mihi futurum erat, quid necesse fuit concipere ? perrexitque ut consuleret Dominum.
     EN: But the little ones struggled together in her womb. She said, “If it was to be like this for me, why was there any need to conceive?” And she went to consult the Lord.

25:23  LA: Qui respondens ait : [Duæ gentes sunt in utero tuo, et duo populi ex ventre tuo dividentur, populusque populum superabit, et major serviet minori.]
     EN: He answered, “Two nations are in your womb, and two peoples shall be divided from your body. One people shall overcome the other, and the elder shall serve the younger.”

25:24  LA: Jam tempus pariendi advenerat, et ecce gemini in utero ejus reperti sunt.
     EN: The time to give birth arrived, and twins were found in her womb.

25:25  LA: Qui prior egressus est, rufus erat, et totus in morem pellis hispidus : vocatumque est nomen ejus Esau. Protinus alter egrediens, plantam fratris tenebat manu : et idcirco appellavit eum Jacob.
     EN: The first to come out was red and hairy all over like a pelt, and he was named Esau. Immediately the other came out, holding his brother's heel in his hand, and for this reason he was called Jacob.

25:26  LA: Sexagenarius erat Isaac quando nati sunt ei parvuli.
     EN: Isaac was sixty years old when the children were born to him.

25:27  LA: Quibus adultis, factus est Esau vir gnarus venandi, et homo agricola : Jacob autem vir simplex habitabat in tabernaculis.
     EN: When they grew up, Esau became a skilled hunter and a farmer, while Jacob was a simple man who lived in tents.

25:28  LA: Isaac amabat Esau, eo quod de venationibus illius vesceretur : et Rebecca diligebat Jacob.
     EN: Isaac loved Esau because he ate his game, but Rebekah loved Jacob.

25:29  LA: Coxit autem Jacob pulmentum : ad quem cum venisset Esau de agro lassus,
     EN: Jacob cooked a stew, and Esau came to him weary from the field

25:30  LA: ait : Da mihi de coctione hac rufa, quia oppido lassus sum. Quam ob causam vocatum est nomen ejus Edom.
     EN: and said, “Give me some of this red stew, for I am utterly weary.” For this reason he was named Edom.

25:31  LA: Cui dixit Jacob : Vende mihi primogenita tua.
     EN: Jacob said to him, “Sell me your birthright.”

25:32  LA: Ille respondit : En morior, quid mihi proderunt primogenita ?
     EN: He answered, “See, I am dying. What use is a birthright to me?”

25:33  LA: Ait Jacob : Jura ergo mihi. Juravit ei Esau et vendidit primogenita.
     EN: Jacob said, “Then swear to me.” Esau swore to him and sold his birthright.

25:34  LA: Et sic, accepto pane et lentis edulio, comedit et bibit, et abiit, parvipendens quod primogenita vendidisset.
     EN: So he received bread and a dish of lentils, ate and drank, and went away, making little of having sold his birthright.

GENESIS 26

26:1  LA: Orta autem fame super terram post eam sterilitatem, quæ acciderat in diebus Abraham, abiit Isaac ad Abimelech regem Palæstinorum in Gerara.
     EN: A famine arose in the land, after the barrenness that had occurred in Abraham's days. Isaac went to Abimelech king of the Philistines in Gerar.

26:2  LA: Apparuitque ei Dominus, et ait : Ne descendas in Ægyptum, sed quiesce in terra quam dixero tibi,
     EN: The Lord appeared to him and said, “Do not go down to Egypt, but remain in the land I shall tell you,

26:3  LA: et peregrinare in ea : eroque tecum, et benedicam tibi : tibi enim et semini tuo dabo universas regiones has, complens juramentum quod spopondi Abraham patri tuo.
     EN: and sojourn in it. I will be with you and bless you, for to you and your offspring I will give all these regions, fulfilling the oath I pledged to your father Abraham.

26:4  LA: Et multiplicabo semen tuum sicut stellas cæli : daboque posteris tuis universas regiones has : et benedicentur in semine tuo omnes gentes terræ,
     EN: I will multiply your offspring like the stars of heaven and give your descendants all these regions, and in your offspring all the nations of the earth shall be blessed,

26:5  LA: eo quod obedierit Abraham voci meæ, et custodierit præcepta et mandata mea, et cæremonias legesque servaverit.
     EN: because Abraham obeyed My voice, kept My precepts and commandments, and observed My rites and laws.”

26:6  LA: Mansit itaque Isaac in Geraris.
     EN: So Isaac remained in Gerar.

26:7  LA: Qui cum interrogaretur a viris loci illius super uxore sua, respondit : Soror mea est : timuerat enim confiteri quod sibi esset sociata conjugio, reputans ne forte interficerent eum propter illius pulchritudinem.
     EN: When the men of that place asked him about his wife, he answered, “She is my sister.” For he feared to acknowledge that she was married to him, thinking they might kill him because of her beauty.

26:8  LA: Cumque pertransissent dies plurimi, et ibidem moraretur, prospiciens Abimelech rex Palæstinorum per fenestram, vidit eum jocantem cum Rebecca uxore sua.
     EN: After many days had passed while he stayed there, Abimelech king of the Philistines looked through a window and saw him playing affectionately with his wife Rebekah.

26:9  LA: Et accersito eo, ait : Perspicuum est quod uxor tua sit : cur mentitus es eam sororem tuam esse ? Respondit : Timui ne morerer propter eam.
     EN: He summoned him and said, “Clearly she is your wife. Why did you lie and say she was your sister?” He answered, “I feared I might die because of her.”

26:10  LA: Dixitque Abimelech : Quare imposuisti nobis ? potuit coire quispiam de populo cum uxore tua, et induxeras super nos grande peccatum. Præcepitque omni populo, dicens :
     EN: Abimelech said, “Why did you deceive us? Someone among the people might have lain with your wife, and you would have brought a great sin upon us.” He commanded all the people, saying,

26:11  LA: Qui tetigerit hominis hujus uxorem, morte morietur.
     EN: “Whoever touches this man's wife shall surely die.”

26:12  LA: Sevit autem Isaac in terra illa, et invenit in ipso anno centuplum : benedixitque ei Dominus.
     EN: Isaac sowed in that land and reaped a hundredfold that same year, and the Lord blessed him.

26:13  LA: Et locupletatus est homo, et ibat proficiens atque succrescens, donec magnus vehementer effectus est :
     EN: The man grew rich and continued to prosper and increase until he became exceedingly great.

26:14  LA: habuit quoque possessiones ovium et armentorum, et familiæ plurimum. Ob hoc invidentes ei Palæstini,
     EN: He possessed flocks of sheep, herds, and a very large household. Because of this the Philistines envied him,

26:15  LA: omnes puteos, quos foderant servi patris illius Abraham, illo tempore obstruxerunt, implentes humo :
     EN: and at that time they stopped up all the wells his father Abraham's servants had dug, filling them with earth,

26:16  LA: in tantum, ut ipse Abimelech diceret ad Isaac : Recede a nobis, quoniam potentior nobis factus es valde.
     EN: until Abimelech himself said to Isaac, “Depart from us, for you have become far stronger than we are.”

26:17  LA: Et ille discedens, ut veniret ad torrentem Geraræ, habitaretque ibi,
     EN: He departed, coming to the streambed of Gerar to live there,

26:18  LA: rursum fodit alios puteos, quos foderant servi patris sui Abraham, et quos, illo mortuo, olim obstruxerant Philisthiim : appellavitque eos eisdem nominibus quibus ante pater vocaverat.
     EN: and again dug other wells that his father Abraham's servants had dug, which the Philistines had formerly stopped up after his death. He called them by the same names his father had given them before.

26:19  LA: Foderuntque in torrente, et repererunt aquam vivam.
     EN: They dug in the streambed and found flowing water.

26:20  LA: Sed et ibi jurgium fuit pastorum Geraræ adversus pastores Isaac, dicentium : Nostra est aqua, quam ob rem nomen putei ex eo, quod acciderat, vocavit Calumniam.
     EN: But there too the herdsmen of Gerar quarreled with Isaac's herdsmen, saying, “The water is ours.” Therefore, because of what happened, he named the well Calumny.

26:21  LA: Foderunt autem et alium : et pro illo quoque rixati sunt, appellavitque eum Inimicitias.
     EN: They dug another, and quarreled over that one also, so he named it Hostility.

26:22  LA: Profectus inde fodit alium puteum, pro quo non contenderunt : itaque vocavit nomen ejus Latitudo, dicens : Nunc dilatavit nos Dominus, et fecit crescere super terram.
     EN: Moving from there, he dug another well, over which they did not contend. He named it Spaciousness, saying, “Now the Lord has made room for us and caused us to increase upon the earth.”

26:23  LA: Ascendit autem ex illo loco in Bersabee,
     EN: From that place he went up to Beersheba,

26:24  LA: ubi apparuit ei Dominus in ipsa nocte, dicens : Ego sum Deus Abraham patris tui : noli timere, quia ego tecum sum : benedicam tibi, et multiplicabo semen tuum propter servum meum Abraham.
     EN: where the Lord appeared to him that night, saying, “I am the God of Abraham your father. Do not fear, for I am with you. I will bless you and multiply your offspring for the sake of My servant Abraham.”

26:25  LA: Itaque ædificavit ibi altare : et invocato nomine Domini, extendit tabernaculum, præcepitque servis suis ut foderent puteum.
     EN: He built an altar there and called upon the name of the Lord. He pitched his tent and commanded his servants to dig a well.

26:26  LA: Ad quem locum cum venissent de Geraris Abimelech, et Ochozath amicus illius, et Phicol dux militum,
     EN: Abimelech came there from Gerar with his friend Ahuzzath and Phicol, commander of the soldiers.

26:27  LA: locutus est eis Isaac : Quid venistis ad me, hominem quem odistis, et expulistis a vobis ?
     EN: Isaac said to them, “Why have you come to me, a man whom you hate and have driven away from you?”

26:28  LA: Qui responderunt : Vidimus tecum esse Dominum, et idcirco nos diximus : Sit juramentum inter nos, et ineamus fœdus,
     EN: They answered, “We have seen that the Lord is with you, and therefore we said, ‘Let there be an oath between us, and let us make a covenant,

26:29  LA: ut non facias nobis quidquam mali, sicut et nos nihil tuorum attigimus, nec fecimus quod te læderet : sed cum pace dimisimus auctum benedictione Domini.
     EN: that you will do us no harm, just as we have touched nothing of yours or done anything to hurt you, but sent you away in peace, enriched by the Lord's blessing.’”

26:30  LA: Fecit ergo eis convivium, et post cibum et potum
     EN: He made a feast for them, and after food and drink,

26:31  LA: surgentes mane, juraverunt sibi mutuo : dimisitque eos Isaac pacifice in locum suum.
     EN: they rose in the morning and swore to one another. Isaac sent them away peacefully to their own place.

26:32  LA: Ecce autem venerunt in ipso die servi Isaac annuntiantes ei de puteo, quem foderant, atque dicentes : Invenimus aquam.
     EN: That very day Isaac's servants came and told him about the well they had dug, saying, “We have found water.”

26:33  LA: Unde appellavit eum Abundantiam : et nomen urbi impositum est Bersabee, usque in præsentem diem.
     EN: So he named it Abundance, and the city has been called Beersheba to the present day.

26:34  LA: Esau vero quadragenarius duxit uxores, Judith filiam Beeri Hethæi, et Basemath filiam Elon ejusdem loci :
     EN: At forty years old Esau took as wives Judith, daughter of Beeri the Hittite, and Basemath, daughter of Elon from the same place.

26:35  LA: quæ ambæ offenderant animum Isaac et Rebeccæ.
     EN: Both grieved the hearts of Isaac and Rebekah.

GENESIS 27

27:1  LA: Senuit autem Isaac, et caligaverunt oculi ejus, et videre non poterat : vocavitque Esau filium suum majorem, et dixit ei : Fili mi ? Qui respondit : Adsum.
     EN: Isaac grew old, and his eyes became dim so that he could not see. He called his elder son Esau and said to him, “My son?” He answered, “Here I am.”

27:2  LA: Cui pater : Vides, inquit, quod senuerim, et ignorem diem mortis meæ.
     EN: His father said, “You see that I have grown old, and I do not know the day of my death.

27:3  LA: Sume arma tua, pharetram, et arcum, et egredere foras : cumque venatu aliquid apprehenderis,
     EN: Take your weapons, your quiver and bow, and go out. When you have caught something by hunting,

27:4  LA: fac mihi inde pulmentum sicut velle me nosti, et affer ut comedam : et benedicat tibi anima mea antequam moriar.
     EN: make me a dish from it, as you know I like, and bring it for me to eat, so that my soul may bless you before I die.”

27:5  LA: Quod cum audisset Rebecca, et ille abiisset in agrum ut jussionem patris impleret,
     EN: Rebekah heard this, and when he had gone into the field to fulfill his father's command,

27:6  LA: dixit filio suo Jacob : Audivi patrem tuum loquentem cum Esau fratre tuo, et dicentem ei :
     EN: she said to her son Jacob, “I heard your father speaking with your brother Esau and saying to him,

27:7  LA: Affer mihi de venatione tua, et fac cibos ut comedam, et benedicam tibi coram Domino antequam moriar.
     EN: ‘Bring me some of your game and prepare food for me to eat, that I may bless you before the Lord before I die.’

27:8  LA: Nunc ergo, fili mi, acquiesce consiliis meis :
     EN: Now therefore, my son, follow my counsel.

27:9  LA: et pergens ad gregem, affer mihi duos hædos optimos, ut faciam ex eis escas patri tuo, quibus libenter vescitur :
     EN: Go to the flock and bring me two choice young goats, so that I may prepare from them the food your father enjoys.

27:10  LA: quas cum intuleris, et comederit, benedicat tibi priusquam moriatur.
     EN: When you bring it to him and he has eaten, he may bless you before he dies.”

27:11  LA: Cui ille respondit : Nosti quod Esau frater meus homo pilosus sit, et ego lenis :
     EN: He answered her, “You know that my brother Esau is hairy, and I am smooth.

27:12  LA: si attrectaverit me pater meus, et senserit, timeo ne putet me sibi voluisse illudere, et inducam super me maledictionem pro benedictione.
     EN: If my father touches me and notices, I fear he will think I intended to mock him, and I shall bring a curse upon myself instead of a blessing.”

27:13  LA: Ad quem mater : In me sit, ait, ista maledictio, fili mi : tantum audi vocem meam, et pergens, affer quæ dixi.
     EN: His mother said to him, “Let that curse be upon me, my son. Only listen to my voice; go and bring what I have said.”

27:14  LA: Abiit, et attulit, deditque matri. Paravit illa cibos, sicut velle noverat patrem illius.
     EN: He went, brought the goats, and gave them to his mother. She prepared the food as she knew his father liked it.

27:15  LA: Et vestibus Esau valde bonis, quas apud se habebat domi, induit eum :
     EN: She dressed him in Esau's finest garments, which she had with her in the house,

27:16  LA: pelliculasque hædorum circumdedit manibus, et colli nuda protexit :
     EN: wrapped the skins of the young goats around his hands, and covered the bare part of his neck.

27:17  LA: deditque pulmentum, et panes, quos coxerat, tradidit.
     EN: She gave him the dish and handed him the bread she had baked.

27:18  LA: Quibus illatis, dixit : Pater mi ? At ille respondit : Audio. Quis es tu, fili mi ?
     EN: He brought these in and said, “My father?” He answered, “I hear you. Who are you, my son?”

27:19  LA: Dixitque Jacob : Ego sum primogenitus tuus Esau : feci sicut præcepisti mihi : surge, sede, et comede de venatione mea, ut benedicat mihi anima tua.
     EN: Jacob said, “I am Esau your firstborn. I have done as you commanded me. Rise, sit, and eat some of my game, so that your soul may bless me.”

27:20  LA: Rursumque Isaac ad filium suum : Quomodo, inquit, tam cito invenire potuisti, fili mi ? Qui respondit : Voluntas Dei fuit ut cito occurreret mihi quod volebam.
     EN: Isaac said again to his son, “How were you able to find it so quickly, my son?” He answered, “It was God's will that what I wanted should meet me quickly.”

27:21  LA: Dixitque Isaac : Accede huc, ut tangam te, fili mi, et probem utrum tu sis filius meus Esau, an non.
     EN: Isaac said, “Come here, my son, so that I may touch you and determine whether you are really my son Esau.”

27:22  LA: Accessit ille ad patrem, et palpato eo, dixit Isaac : Vox quidem, vox Jacob est : sed manus, manus sunt Esau.
     EN: He approached his father. Isaac felt him and said, “The voice is indeed Jacob's voice, but the hands are Esau's hands.”

27:23  LA: Et non cognovit eum, quia pilosæ manus similitudinem majoris expresserant. Benedicens ergo illi,
     EN: He did not recognize him, because the hairy hands resembled those of the elder son. So, blessing him,

27:24  LA: ait : Tu es filius meus Esau ? Respondit : Ego sum.
     EN: he said, “Are you my son Esau?” He answered, “I am.”

27:25  LA: At ille : Affer mihi, inquit, cibos de venatione tua, fili mi, ut benedicat tibi anima mea. Quos cum oblatos comedisset, obtulit ei etiam vinum. Quo hausto,
     EN: He said, “Bring me food from your game, my son, so that my soul may bless you.” When it was brought and he had eaten, he also offered him wine. After drinking it,

27:26  LA: dixit ad eum : Accede ad me, et da mihi osculum, fili mi.
     EN: he said to him, “Come near and give me a kiss, my son.”

27:27  LA: Accessit, et osculatus est eum. Statimque ut sensit vestimentorum illius fragrantiam, benedicens illi, ait : [Ecce odor filii mei sicut odor agri pleni, cui benedixit Dominus.
     EN: He came near and kissed him. As soon as he smelled the fragrance of his garments, he blessed him, saying, “See, my son's fragrance is like the fragrance of a full field that the Lord has blessed.

27:28  LA: Det tibi Deus de rore cæli et de pinguedine terræ abundantiam frumenti et vini.
     EN: May God give you from the dew of heaven and the richness of the earth an abundance of grain and wine.

27:29  LA: Et serviant tibi populi, et adorent te tribus : esto dominus fratrum tuorum, et incurventur ante te filii matris tuæ : qui maledixerit tibi, sit ille maledictus, et qui benedixerit tibi, benedictionibus repleatur.]
     EN: May peoples serve you and tribes bow to you. Be lord over your brothers, and may your mother's sons bow before you. Let whoever curses you be cursed, and whoever blesses you be filled with blessings.”

27:30  LA: Vix Isaac sermonem impleverat, et egresso Jacob foras, venit Esau,
     EN: Isaac had scarcely finished speaking, and Jacob had gone out, when Esau arrived.

27:31  LA: coctosque de venatione cibos intulit patri, dicens : Surge, pater mi, et comede de venatione filii tui, ut benedicat mihi anima tua.
     EN: He brought his father the food he had cooked from his game, saying, “Rise, my father, and eat your son's game, that your soul may bless me.”

27:32  LA: Dixitque illi Isaac : Quis enim es tu ? Qui respondit : Ego sum filius tuus primogenitus Esau.
     EN: Isaac said to him, “Who are you, then?” He answered, “I am your firstborn son Esau.”

27:33  LA: Expavit Isaac stupore vehementi : et ultra quam credi potest admirans, ait : Quis igitur ille est qui dudum captam venationem attulit mihi, et comedi ex omnibus priusquam tu venires ; benedixique ei, et erit benedictus ?
     EN: Isaac trembled in great astonishment and, amazed beyond belief, said, “Who, then, was the one who just brought me the game he had caught? I ate of it all before you came, and I blessed him—and blessed he shall be.”

27:34  LA: Auditis Esau sermonibus patris, irrugiit clamore magno : et consternatus, ait : Benedic etiam et mihi, pater mi.
     EN: When Esau heard his father's words, he cried out with a great cry and said in dismay, “Bless me also, my father.”

27:35  LA: Qui ait : Venit germanus tuus fraudulenter, et accepit benedictionem tuam.
     EN: He said, “Your brother came deceitfully and took your blessing.”

27:36  LA: At ille subjunxit : Juste vocatum est nomen ejus Jacob : supplantavit enim me en altera vice : primogenita mea ante tulit, et nunc secundo surripuit benedictionem meam. Rursumque ad patrem : Numquid non reservasti, ait, et mihi benedictionem ?
     EN: He added, “Rightly is he named Jacob, for see, he has supplanted me a second time. First he took my birthright, and now a second time he has stolen my blessing.” Again he said to his father, “Have you not reserved a blessing for me also?”

27:37  LA: Respondit Isaac : Dominum tuum illum constitui, et omnes fratres ejus servituti illius subjugavi ; frumento et vino stabilivi eum : et tibi post hæc, fili mi, ultra quid faciam ?
     EN: Isaac answered, “I have appointed him your lord and subjected all his brothers to his service. With grain and wine I have sustained him. After this, what more can I do for you, my son?”

27:38  LA: Cui Esau : Num unam, inquit, tantum benedictionem habes, pater ? mihi quoque obsecro ut benedicas. Cumque ejulatu magno fleret,
     EN: Esau said to him, “Have you only one blessing, father? I beg you, bless me also.” As he wept with loud lamentation,

27:39  LA: motus Isaac, dixit ad eum : [In pinguedine terræ, et in rore cæli desuper,
     EN: Isaac was moved and said to him, “In the richness of the earth and in the dew of heaven above

27:40  LA: erit benedictio tua. Vives in gladio, et fratri tuo servies : tempusque veniet, cum excutias et solvas jugum ejus de cervicibus tuis.]
     EN: shall your blessing be. By the sword you shall live, and you shall serve your brother. But the time shall come when you shake off and loosen his yoke from your neck.”

27:41  LA: Oderat ergo semper Esau Jacob pro benedictione qua benedixerat ei pater : dixitque in corde suo : Venient dies luctus patris mei, et occidam Jacob fratrem meum.
     EN: Esau continually hated Jacob because of the blessing with which his father had blessed him. He said in his heart, “The days of mourning for my father will come, and I shall kill my brother Jacob.”

27:42  LA: Nuntiata sunt hæc Rebeccæ : quæ mittens et vocans Jacob filium suum, dixit ad eum : Ecce Esau frater tuus minatur ut occidat te.
     EN: These things were reported to Rebekah. She sent for her son Jacob and said to him, “See, your brother Esau threatens to kill you.

27:43  LA: Nunc ergo, fili mi, audi vocem meam, et consurgens fuge ad Laban fratrem meum in Haran :
     EN: Now therefore, my son, listen to my voice: rise and flee to my brother Laban in Haran.

27:44  LA: habitabisque cum eo dies paucos, donec requiescat furor fratris tui,
     EN: Stay with him for a few days until your brother's fury subsides,

27:45  LA: et cesset indignatio ejus, obliviscaturque eorum quæ fecisti in eum : postea mittam, et adducam te inde huc : cur utroque orbabor filio in uno die ?
     EN: his anger ceases, and he forgets what you have done to him. Then I will send and bring you back here from there. Why should I be bereaved of both sons in one day?”

27:46  LA: Dixitque Rebecca ad Isaac : Tædet me vitæ meæ propter filias Heth : si acceperit Jacob uxorem de stirpe hujus terræ, nolo vivere.
     EN: Rebekah said to Isaac, “I am weary of my life because of the daughters of Heth. If Jacob takes a wife from the people of this land, I do not wish to live.”

GENESIS 28

28:1  LA: Vocavit itaque Isaac Jacob, et benedixit eum, præcepitque ei dicens : Noli accipere conjugem de genere Chanaan :
     EN: Isaac called Jacob, blessed him, and commanded him, saying, “Do not take a wife from the people of Canaan,

28:2  LA: sed vade, et proficiscere in Mesopotamiam Syriæ, ad domum Bathuel patris matris tuæ, et accipe tibi inde uxorem de filiabus Laban avunculi tui.
     EN: but go and journey to Mesopotamia of Syria, to the house of Bethuel, your mother's father, and take a wife there from the daughters of your uncle Laban.

28:3  LA: Deus autem omnipotens benedicat tibi, et crescere te faciat, atque multiplicet, ut sis in turbas populorum.
     EN: May God Almighty bless you, make you increase, and multiply you, so that you become a multitude of peoples.

28:4  LA: Et det tibi benedictiones Abrahæ, et semini tuo post te : ut possideas terram peregrinationis tuæ, quam pollicitus est avo tuo.
     EN: May He give the blessings of Abraham to you and to your offspring after you, so that you possess the land of your sojourning, which He promised your grandfather.”

28:5  LA: Cumque dimisisset eum Isaac, profectus venit in Mesopotamiam Syriæ ad Laban filium Bathuel Syri, fratrem Rebeccæ matris suæ.
     EN: Isaac sent him away, and he journeyed to Mesopotamia of Syria, to Laban son of Bethuel the Syrian, the brother of his mother Rebekah.

28:6  LA: Videns autem Esau quod benedixisset pater suus Jacob, et misisset eum in Mesopotamiam Syriæ, ut inde uxorem duceret ; et quod post benedictionem præcepisset ei, dicens : Non accipies uxorem de filiabus Chanaan :
     EN: Esau saw that his father had blessed Jacob and sent him to Mesopotamia of Syria to take a wife from there, and that after blessing him he had commanded him, “You shall not take a wife from the daughters of Canaan.”

28:7  LA: quodque obediens Jacob parentibus suis isset in Syriam :
     EN: He saw that Jacob had obeyed his parents and gone to Syria,

28:8  LA: probans quoque quod non libenter aspiceret filias Chanaan pater suus :
     EN: and understood also that his father did not look favorably upon the daughters of Canaan.

28:9  LA: ivit ad Ismaëlem, et duxit uxorem absque iis, quas prius habebat, Maheleth filiam Ismaël filii Abraham, sororem Nabaioth.
     EN: So he went to Ishmael and took as a wife, in addition to those he already had, Mahalath, daughter of Ishmael the son of Abraham and sister of Nebaioth.

28:10  LA: Igitur egressus Jacob de Bersabee, pergebat Haran.
     EN: Jacob departed from Beersheba and traveled toward Haran.

28:11  LA: Cumque venisset ad quemdam locum, et vellet in eo requiescere post solis occubitum, tulit de lapidibus qui jacebant, et supponens capiti suo, dormivit in eodem loco.
     EN: He came to a certain place and wished to rest there after sunset. He took one of the stones lying there, placed it beneath his head, and slept in that place.

28:12  LA: Viditque in somnis scalam stantem super terram, et cacumen illius tangens cælum : angelos quoque Dei ascendentes et descendentes per eam,
     EN: In a dream he saw a ladder standing upon the earth, its top touching heaven, and angels of God ascending and descending upon it.

28:13  LA: et Dominum innixum scalæ dicentem sibi : Ego sum Dominus Deus Abraham patris tui, et Deus Isaac : terram, in qua dormis, tibi dabo et semini tuo.
     EN: The Lord leaned upon the ladder and said to him, “I am the Lord, the God of Abraham your father and the God of Isaac. The land on which you sleep I will give to you and your offspring.

28:14  LA: Eritque semen tuum quasi pulvis terræ : dilataberis ad occidentem, et orientem, et septentrionem, et meridiem : et benedicentur in te et in semine tuo cunctæ tribus terræ.
     EN: Your offspring shall be like the dust of the earth. You shall spread west and east, north and south, and in you and your offspring all the tribes of the earth shall be blessed.

28:15  LA: Et ero custos tuus quocumque perrexeris, et reducam te in terram hanc : nec dimittam nisi complevero universa quæ dixi.
     EN: I will be your guardian wherever you go and bring you back to this land. I will not leave you until I have fulfilled everything I have said.”

28:16  LA: Cumque evigilasset Jacob de somno, ait : Vere Dominus est in loco isto, et ego nesciebam.
     EN: Jacob awoke from sleep and said, “Truly the Lord is in this place, and I did not know it.”

28:17  LA: Pavensque, Quam terribilis est, inquit, locus iste ! non est hic aliud nisi domus Dei, et porta cæli.
     EN: Filled with fear, he said, “How awe-inspiring this place is! This is nothing other than the house of God and the gate of heaven.”

28:18  LA: Surgens ergo Jacob mane, tulit lapidem quem supposuerat capiti suo, et erexit in titulum, fundens oleum desuper.
     EN: Jacob rose early, took the stone he had placed beneath his head, and erected it as a pillar, pouring oil upon it.

28:19  LA: Appellavitque nomen urbis Bethel, quæ prius Luza vocabatur.
     EN: He named the city Bethel; previously it had been called Luz.

28:20  LA: Vovit etiam votum, dicens : Si fuerit Deus mecum, et custodierit me in via, per quam ego ambulo, et dederit mihi panem ad vescendum, et vestimentum ad induendum,
     EN: He also made a vow, saying, “If God is with me, guards me on the way I travel, and gives me bread to eat and clothing to wear,

28:21  LA: reversusque fuero prospere ad domum patris mei : erit mihi Dominus in Deum,
     EN: and I return safely to my father's house, then the Lord shall be my God.

28:22  LA: et lapis iste, quem erexi in titulum, vocabitur Domus Dei : cunctorumque quæ dederis mihi, decimas offeram tibi.
     EN: This stone, which I have erected as a pillar, shall be called the House of God; and of everything You give me, I will offer You a tenth.”

GENESIS 29

29:1  LA: Profectus ergo Jacob venit in terram orientalem.
     EN: Jacob journeyed on and came to the eastern land.

29:2  LA: Et vidit puteum in agro, tres quoque greges ovium accubantes juxta eum : nam ex illo adaquabantur pecora, et os ejus grandi lapide claudebatur.
     EN: He saw a well in the field, and three flocks of sheep lying beside it, for the animals were watered from it. Its mouth was closed with a large stone.

29:3  LA: Morisque erat ut cunctis ovibus congregatis devolverent lapidem, et refectis gregibus rursum super os putei ponerent.
     EN: The custom was to roll the stone away when all the sheep had gathered, and after refreshing the flocks, to put it back over the well's mouth.

29:4  LA: Dixitque ad pastores : Fratres, unde estis ? Qui responderunt : De Haran.
     EN: He said to the shepherds, “Brothers, where are you from?” They answered, “From Haran.”

29:5  LA: Quos interrogans, Numquid, ait, nostis Laban filium Nachor ? Dixerunt : Novimus.
     EN: He asked them, “Do you know Laban son of Nahor?” They said, “We know him.”

29:6  LA: Sanusne est ? inquit. Valet, inquiunt : et ecce Rachel filia ejus venit cum grege suo.
     EN: “Is he well?” he asked. “He is well,” they said, “and here comes his daughter Rachel with her flock.”

29:7  LA: Dixitque Jacob : Adhuc multum diei superest, nec est tempus ut reducantur ad caulas greges : date ante potum ovibus, et sic eas ad pastum reducite.
     EN: Jacob said, “Much of the day still remains, and it is not time to bring the flocks back to the folds. First give the sheep water, and then lead them back to pasture.”

29:8  LA: Qui responderunt : Non possumus, donec omnia pecora congregentur, et amoveamus lapidem de ore putei, ut adaquemus greges.
     EN: They answered, “We cannot until all the animals gather and we remove the stone from the well's mouth to water the flocks.”

29:9  LA: Adhuc loquebantur, et ecce Rachel veniebat cum ovibus patris sui : nam gregem ipsa pascebat.
     EN: While they were still speaking, Rachel came with her father's sheep, for she herself tended the flock.

29:10  LA: Quam cum vidisset Jacob, et sciret consobrinam suam, ovesque Laban avunculi sui, amovit lapidem quo puteus claudebatur.
     EN: When Jacob saw her and knew she was his cousin, and saw the sheep of his uncle Laban, he removed the stone that closed the well.

29:11  LA: Et adaquato grege, osculatus est eam : et elevata voce flevit,
     EN: After watering the flock, he kissed her, raised his voice, and wept.

29:12  LA: et indicavit ei quod frater esset patris sui, et filius Rebeccæ : at illa festinans nuntiavit patri suo.
     EN: He told her that he was her father's kinsman and Rebekah's son. She hurried and told her father.

29:13  LA: Qui cum audisset venisse Jacob filium sororis suæ, cucurrit obviam ei : complexusque eum, et in oscula ruens, duxit in domum suam. Auditis autem causis itineris,
     EN: When he heard that Jacob, his sister's son, had come, he ran to meet him, embraced him, kissed him eagerly, and led him into his house. After hearing the reasons for his journey,

29:14  LA: respondit : Os meum es, et caro mea. Et postquam impleti sunt dies mensis unius,
     EN: he answered, “You are my bone and my flesh.” When the days of one month had passed,

29:15  LA: dixit ei : Num quia frater meus es, gratis servies mihi ? dic quid mercedis accipias.
     EN: he said to him, “Because you are my kinsman, should you serve me for nothing? Tell me what wages you will take.”

29:16  LA: Habebat vero duas filias : nomen majoris Lia, minor vero appellabatur Rachel.
     EN: He had two daughters: the elder was named Leah, and the younger Rachel.

29:17  LA: Sed Lia lippis erat oculis : Rachel decora facie, et venusto aspectu.
     EN: Leah's eyes were weak, but Rachel was beautiful in face and lovely in appearance.

29:18  LA: Quam diligens Jacob, ait : Serviam tibi pro Rachel filia tua minore, septem annis.
     EN: Jacob loved her and said, “I will serve you seven years for your younger daughter Rachel.”

29:19  LA: Respondit Laban : Melius est ut tibi eam dem quam alteri viro : mane apud me.
     EN: Laban answered, “It is better that I give her to you than to another man. Stay with me.”

29:20  LA: Servivit ergo Jacob pro Rachel septem annis : et videbantur illi pauci dies præ amoris magnitudine.
     EN: Jacob served seven years for Rachel, and they seemed to him only a few days because of the greatness of his love.

29:21  LA: Dixitque ad Laban : Da mihi uxorem meam : quia jam tempus impletum est, ut ingrediar ad illam.
     EN: He said to Laban, “Give me my wife, for the time is now fulfilled, so that I may go in to her.”

29:22  LA: Qui vocatis multis amicorum turbis ad convivium, fecit nuptias.
     EN: Laban called a great gathering of friends to a feast and celebrated the wedding.

29:23  LA: Et vespere Liam filiam suam introduxit ad eum,
     EN: In the evening he brought his daughter Leah in to him,

29:24  LA: dans ancillam filiæ, Zelpham nomine. Ad quam cum ex more Jacob fuisset ingressus, facto mane vidit Liam :
     EN: giving his daughter a maidservant named Zilpah. Jacob went in to her according to custom, but when morning came, he saw Leah.

29:25  LA: et dixit ad socerum suum : Quid est quod facere voluisti ? nonne pro Rachel servivi tibi ? quare imposuisti mihi ?
     EN: He said to his father-in-law, “What have you intended by doing this? Did I not serve you for Rachel? Why have you deceived me?”

29:26  LA: Respondit Laban : Non est in loco nostro consuetudinis, ut minores ante tradamus ad nuptias.
     EN: Laban answered, “It is not our local custom to give the younger in marriage first.

29:27  LA: Imple hebdomadam dierum hujus copulæ : et hanc quoque dabo tibi pro opere quo serviturus es mihi septem annis aliis.
     EN: Complete the week of this wedding, and I will also give you the other for the service you shall render me another seven years.”

29:28  LA: Acquievit placito : et hebdomada transacta, Rachel duxit uxorem :
     EN: He agreed to the arrangement, and when the week was over, he married Rachel,

29:29  LA: cui pater servam Balam tradiderat.
     EN: to whom her father had given the maidservant Bilhah.

29:30  LA: Tandemque potitus optatis nuptiis, amorem sequentis priori prætulit, serviens apud eum septem annis aliis.
     EN: At last obtaining the marriage he desired, he loved the second wife more than the first, serving Laban another seven years.

29:31  LA: Videns autem Dominus quod despiceret Liam, aperuit vulvam ejus, sorore sterili permanente.
     EN: The Lord saw that he slighted Leah and opened her womb, while her sister remained barren.

29:32  LA: Quæ conceptum genuit filium, vocavitque nomen ejus Ruben, dicens : Vidit Dominus humilitatem meam : nunc amabit me vir meus.
     EN: She conceived and bore a son, and named him Reuben, saying, “The Lord has seen my humiliation; now my husband will love me.”

29:33  LA: Rursumque concepit et peperit filium, et ait : Quoniam audivit me Dominus haberi contemptui, dedit etiam istum mihi ; vocavitque nomen ejus Simeon.
     EN: She conceived again and bore a son, and said, “Because the Lord heard that I was held in contempt, He has given me this one also.” She named him Simeon.

29:34  LA: Concepitque tertio, et genuit alium filium : dixitque : Nunc quoque copulabitur mihi maritus meus : eo quod pepererim ei tres filios : et idcirco appellavit nomen ejus Levi.
     EN: She conceived a third time and bore another son, saying, “Now my husband will also be joined to me, because I have borne him three sons.” Therefore she named him Levi.

29:35  LA: Quarto concepit, et peperit filium, et ait : Modo confitebor Domino, et ob hoc vocavit eum Judam : cessavitque parere.
     EN: She conceived a fourth time and bore a son, and said, “Now I will praise the Lord.” For this reason she named him Judah, and she stopped bearing children.

GENESIS 30

30:1  LA: Cernens autem Rachel quod infecunda esset, invidit sorori suæ, et ait marito suo : Da mihi liberos, alioquin moriar.
     EN: Rachel saw that she was barren and envied her sister. She said to her husband, “Give me children, or I shall die.”

30:2  LA: Cui iratus respondit Jacob : Num pro Deo ego sum, qui privavit te fructu ventris tui ?
     EN: Jacob answered her angrily, “Am I in the place of God, who has denied you the fruit of your womb?”

30:3  LA: At illa : Habeo, inquit, famulam Balam : ingredere ad illam, ut pariat super genua mea, et habeam ex illa filios.
     EN: She said, “I have a maidservant, Bilhah. Go in to her, so that she may give birth upon my knees and I may have children through her.”

30:4  LA: Deditque illi Balam in conjugium : quæ,
     EN: She gave him Bilhah in marriage, and she,

30:5  LA: ingresso ad se viro, concepit, et peperit filium.
     EN: when her husband went in to her, conceived and bore a son.

30:6  LA: Dixitque Rachel : Judicavit mihi Dominus, et exaudivit vocem meam, dans mihi filium, et idcirco appellavit nomen ejus Dan.
     EN: Rachel said, “The Lord has judged in my favor and heard my voice, giving me a son.” Therefore she named him Dan.

30:7  LA: Rursumque Bala concipiens, peperit alterum,
     EN: Bilhah conceived again and bore another son.

30:8  LA: pro quo ait Rachel : Comparavit me Deus cum sorore mea, et invalui : vocavitque eum Nephthali.
     EN: Concerning him Rachel said, “God has matched me with my sister, and I have prevailed.” She named him Naphtali.

30:9  LA: Sentiens Lia quod parere desiisset, Zelpham ancillam suam marito tradidit.
     EN: Leah, realizing she had stopped bearing children, gave her maidservant Zilpah to her husband.

30:10  LA: Qua post conceptum edente filium,
     EN: When Zilpah conceived and bore a son,

30:11  LA: dixit : Feliciter, et idcirco vocavit nomen ejus Gad.
     EN: Leah said, “Good fortune!” Therefore she named him Gad.

30:12  LA: Peperit quoque Zelpha alterum.
     EN: Zilpah also bore another son.

30:13  LA: Dixitque Lia : Hoc pro beatitudine mea : beatam quippe me dicent mulieres : propterea appellavit eum Aser.
     EN: Leah said, “This is for my happiness, for women will call me happy.” Therefore she named him Asher.

30:14  LA: Egressus autem Ruben tempore messis triticeæ in agrum, reperit mandragoras, quas matri Liæ detulit. Dixitque Rachel : Da mihi partem de mandragoris filii tui.
     EN: Reuben went out into the field at the time of the wheat harvest and found mandrakes, which he brought to his mother Leah. Rachel said, “Give me some of your son's mandrakes.”

30:15  LA: Illa respondit : Parumne tibi videtur quod præripueris maritum mihi, nisi etiam mandragoras filii mei tuleris ? Ait Rachel : Dormiat tecum hac nocte pro mandragoris filii tui.
     EN: She answered, “Does it seem little to you that you have taken my husband away from me, unless you also take my son's mandrakes?” Rachel said, “Let him sleep with you tonight in exchange for your son's mandrakes.”

30:16  LA: Redeuntique ad vesperam Jacob de agro, egressa est in occursum ejus Lia, et Ad me, inquit, intrabis : quia mercede conduxi te pro mandragoris filii mei. Dormivitque cum ea nocte illa.
     EN: When Jacob returned from the field in the evening, Leah went out to meet him and said, “You shall come in to me, for I have hired you for my son's mandrakes.” He slept with her that night.

30:17  LA: Et exaudivit Deus preces ejus, concepitque et peperit filium quintum,
     EN: God heard her prayers, and she conceived and bore a fifth son.

30:18  LA: et ait : Dedit Deus mercedem mihi, quia dedi ancillam meam viro meo : appellavitque nomen ejus Issachar.
     EN: She said, “God has given me my wages, because I gave my maidservant to my husband.” She named him Issachar.

30:19  LA: Rursum Lia concipiens, peperit sextum filium,
     EN: Leah conceived again and bore a sixth son.

30:20  LA: et ait : Dotavit me Deus dote bona : etiam hac vice mecum erit maritus meus, eo quod genuerim ei sex filios : et idcirco appellavit nomen ejus Zabulon.
     EN: She said, “God has endowed me with a good dowry. This time also my husband will be with me, because I have borne him six sons.” Therefore she named him Zebulun.

30:21  LA: Post quem peperit filiam, nomine Dinam.
     EN: After him she bore a daughter named Dinah.

30:22  LA: Recordatus quoque Dominus Rachelis, exaudivit eam, et aperuit vulvam ejus.
     EN: The Lord also remembered Rachel, heard her, and opened her womb.

30:23  LA: Quæ concepit, et peperit filium, dicens : Abstulit Deus opprobrium meum.
     EN: She conceived and bore a son, saying, “God has taken away my reproach.”

30:24  LA: Et vocavit nomen ejus Joseph, dicens : Addat mihi Dominus filium alterum.
     EN: She named him Joseph, saying, “May the Lord add another son for me.”

30:25  LA: Nato autem Joseph, dixit Jacob socero suo : Dimitte me ut revertar in patriam, et ad terram meam.
     EN: After Joseph was born, Jacob said to his father-in-law, “Send me away, so that I may return to my homeland and my own country.

30:26  LA: Da mihi uxores, et liberos meos, pro quibus servivi tibi, ut abeam : tu nosti servitutem qua servivi tibi.
     EN: Give me my wives and children, for whom I have served you, so that I may go. You know the service I have rendered you.”

30:27  LA: Ait illi Laban : Inveniam gratiam in conspectu tuo, experimento didici, quia benedixerit mihi Deus propter te :
     EN: Laban said to him, “Let me find favor in your sight. I have learned by experience that God has blessed me because of you.

30:28  LA: constitue mercedem tuam quam dem tibi.
     EN: Fix the wages I shall give you.”

30:29  LA: At ille respondit : Tu nosti quomodo servierim tibi, et quanta in manibus meis fuerit possessio tua.
     EN: He answered, “You know how I have served you, and how great your possessions have become in my hands.

30:30  LA: Modicum habuisti antequam venirem ad te, et nunc dives effectus es : benedixitque tibi Dominus ad introitum meum. Justum est igitur ut aliquando provideam etiam domui meæ.
     EN: You had little before I came to you, and now you have become rich. The Lord blessed you at my arrival. It is right, then, that at last I should provide for my own household also.”

30:31  LA: Dixitque Laban : Quid tibi dabo ? At ille ait : Nihil volo : sed si feceris quod postulo, iterum pascam, et custodiam pecora tua.
     EN: Laban said, “What shall I give you?” He said, “I want nothing; but if you do what I ask, I will again pasture and guard your animals.

30:32  LA: Gyra omnes greges tuos, et separa cunctas oves varias, et sparso vellere ; quodcumque furvum, et maculosum, variumque fuerit, tam in ovibus quam in capris, erit merces mea.
     EN: Go through all your flocks and separate all the variegated sheep and those with speckled fleece. Whatever is dark, spotted, or variegated, among both sheep and goats, shall be my wages.

30:33  LA: Respondebitque mihi cras justitia mea, quando placiti tempus advenerit coram te : et omnia quæ non fuerint varia, et maculosa, et furva, tam in ovibus quam in capris, furti me arguent.
     EN: My righteousness shall answer for me tomorrow, when the agreed time comes before you. Anything among sheep or goats that is not variegated, spotted, or dark shall convict me of theft.”

30:34  LA: Dixitque Laban : Gratum habeo quod petis.
     EN: Laban said, “I accept what you ask.”

30:35  LA: Et separavit in die illa capras, et oves, et hircos, et arietes varios, atque maculosos : cunctum autem gregem unicolorem, id est albi et nigri velleris, tradidit in manu filiorum suorum.
     EN: That day he separated the goats, sheep, male goats, and rams that were variegated and spotted; but the whole flock of a single color, that is, of white or black fleece, he entrusted to his sons.

30:36  LA: Et posuit spatium itineris trium dierum inter se et generum, qui pascebat reliquos greges ejus.
     EN: He set a distance of three days' journey between himself and his son-in-law, who tended his remaining flocks.

30:37  LA: Tollens ergo Jacob virgas populeas virides, et amygdalinas, et ex platanis, ex parte decorticavit eas : detractisque corticibus, in his, quæ spoliata fuerant, candor apparuit : illa vero quæ integra fuerant, viridia permanserunt : atque in hunc modum color effectus est varius.
     EN: Jacob took fresh branches of poplar, almond, and plane trees and partly peeled them. Where the bark was stripped, white appeared, while the untouched parts remained green. In this way their color became variegated.

30:38  LA: Posuitque eas in canalibus, ubi effundebatur aqua : ut cum venissent greges ad bibendum, ante oculos haberent virgas, et in aspectu earum conciperent.
     EN: He placed them in the troughs where water was poured, so that when the flocks came to drink, they would have the branches before their eyes and conceive while looking at them.

30:39  LA: Factumque est ut in ipso calore coitus, oves intuerentur virgas, et parerent maculosa, et varia, et diverso colore respersa.
     EN: Thus, in the very heat of mating, the sheep looked at the branches and bore young that were spotted, variegated, and marked with different colors.

30:40  LA: Divisitque gregem Jacob, et posuit virgas in canalibus ante oculos arietum : erant autem alba et nigra quæque, Laban ; cetera vero, Jacob, separatis inter se gregibus.
     EN: Jacob divided the flock and placed the branches in the troughs before the rams' eyes. All the white and black animals belonged to Laban, but the rest to Jacob, their flocks kept separate from one another.

30:41  LA: Igitur quando primo tempore ascendebantur oves, ponebat Jacob virgas in canalibus aquarum ante oculos arietum et ovium, ut in earum contemplatione conciperent :
     EN: When the sheep mated in the early season, Jacob placed the branches in the water troughs before the eyes of the rams and ewes, so that they would conceive while looking at them.

30:42  LA: quando vero serotina admissura erat, et conceptus extremus, non ponebat eas. Factaque sunt ea quæ erant serotina, Laban : et quæ primi temporis, Jacob.
     EN: But when mating was late and conception at the end of the season, he did not place them there. Thus the late-born became Laban's, and those of the early season Jacob's.

30:43  LA: Ditatusque est homo ultra modum, et habuit greges multos, ancillas et servos, camelos et asinos.
     EN: The man became exceedingly rich and had many flocks, maidservants, male servants, camels, and donkeys.

GENESIS 31

31:1  LA: Postquam autem audivit verba filiorum Laban dicentium : Tulit Jacob omnia quæ fuerunt patris nostri, et de illius facultate ditatus, factus est inclytus :
     EN: Jacob heard Laban's sons saying, “Jacob has taken everything that belonged to our father, and from his wealth he has grown rich and become renowned.”

31:2  LA: animadvertit quoque faciem Laban, quod non esset erga se sicut heri et nudiustertius,
     EN: He also noticed that Laban's face toward him was not as it had been in former days,

31:3  LA: maxime dicente sibi Domino : Revertere in terram patrum tuorum, et ad generationem tuam, eroque tecum.
     EN: and above all the Lord was saying to him, “Return to the land of your fathers and your kindred, and I will be with you.”

31:4  LA: Misit, et vocavit Rachel et Liam in agrum, ubi pascebat greges,
     EN: He sent and called Rachel and Leah into the field where he tended the flocks,

31:5  LA: dixitque eis : Video faciem patris vestri quod non sit erga me sicut heri et nudiustertius : Deus autem patris mei fuit mecum.
     EN: and said to them, “I see that your father's face toward me is not as it was in former days, but the God of my father has been with me.

31:6  LA: Et ipsæ nostis quod totis viribus meis servierim patri vestro.
     EN: You yourselves know that I have served your father with all my strength.

31:7  LA: Sed et pater vester circumvenit me et mutavit mercedem meam decem vicibus : et tamen non dimisit eum Deus ut noceret mihi.
     EN: Yet your father has cheated me and changed my wages ten times. Still, God has not allowed him to harm me.

31:8  LA: Si quando dixit : Variæ erunt mercedes tuæ : pariebant omnes oves varios fœtus ; quando vero e contrario, ait : Alba quæque accipies pro mercede : omnes greges alba pepererunt.
     EN: Whenever he said, ‘The variegated animals shall be your wages,’ all the sheep bore variegated young. But when, on the contrary, he said, ‘You shall take every white animal as wages,’ all the flocks bore white young.

31:9  LA: Tulitque Deus substantiam patris vestri, et dedit mihi.
     EN: God has taken your father's possessions and given them to me.

31:10  LA: Postquam enim conceptus ovium tempus advenerat, levavi oculos meos, et vidi in somnis ascendentes mares super feminas, varios et maculosos, et diversorum colorum.
     EN: For when the season of the sheep's conception arrived, I raised my eyes and saw in a dream that the males mounting the females were variegated, spotted, and of different colors.

31:11  LA: Dixitque angelus Dei ad me in somnis : Jacob ? Et ego respondi : Adsum.
     EN: The angel of God said to me in the dream, ‘Jacob?’ I answered, ‘Here I am.’

31:12  LA: Qui ait : Leva oculos tuos, et vide universos masculos ascendentes super feminas, varios, maculosos, atque respersos. Vidi enim omnia quæ fecit tibi Laban.
     EN: He said, ‘Raise your eyes and see that all the males mounting the females are variegated, spotted, and speckled, for I have seen everything Laban has done to you.

31:13  LA: Ego sum Deus Bethel, ubi unxisti lapidem, et votum vovisti mihi. Nunc ergo surge, et egredere de terra hac, revertens in terram nativitatis tuæ.
     EN: I am the God of Bethel, where you anointed the stone and made a vow to Me. Now rise, depart from this land, and return to the land of your birth.’”

31:14  LA: Responderuntque Rachel et Lia : Numquid habemus residui quidquam in facultatibus et hæreditate domus patris nostri ?
     EN: Rachel and Leah answered, “Have we anything left in the wealth and inheritance of our father's house?

31:15  LA: nonne quasi alienas reputavit nos, et vendidit, comeditque pretium nostrum ?
     EN: Has he not regarded us as strangers, sold us, and eaten up the price paid for us?

31:16  LA: Sed Deus tulit opes patris nostri, et eas tradidit nobis, ac filiis nostris : unde omnia quæ præcepit tibi Deus, fac.
     EN: But God has taken our father's riches and delivered them to us and our children. Therefore do everything God has commanded you.”

31:17  LA: Surrexit itaque Jacob, et impositis liberis ac conjugibus suis super camelos, abiit.
     EN: Jacob rose, placed his children and wives upon the camels, and departed.

31:18  LA: Tulitque omnem substantiam suam, et greges, et quidquid in Mesopotamia acquisierat, pergens ad Isaac patrem suum in terram Chanaan.
     EN: He took all his possessions, his flocks, and everything he had acquired in Mesopotamia, traveling to his father Isaac in the land of Canaan.

31:19  LA: Eo tempore ierat Laban ad tondendas oves, et Rachel furata est idola patris sui.
     EN: At that time Laban had gone to shear his sheep, and Rachel stole her father's idols.

31:20  LA: Noluitque Jacob confiteri socero suo quod fugeret.
     EN: Jacob was unwilling to tell his father-in-law that he was fleeing.

31:21  LA: Cumque abiisset tam ipse quam omnia quæ juris sui erant, et amne transmisso pergeret contra montem Galaad,
     EN: He departed with everything that belonged to him, crossed the river, and journeyed toward the mountain of Gilead.

31:22  LA: nuntiatum est Laban die tertio quod fugeret Jacob.
     EN: On the third day Laban was told that Jacob had fled.

31:23  LA: Qui, assumptis fratribus suis, persecutus est eum diebus septem : et comprehendit eum in monte Galaad.
     EN: He took his kinsmen, pursued him for seven days, and caught up with him in the mountain of Gilead.

31:24  LA: Viditque in somnis dicentem sibi Deum : Cave ne quidquam aspere loquaris contra Jacob.
     EN: In a dream he saw God saying to him, “Take care not to speak harshly against Jacob.”

31:25  LA: Jamque Jacob extenderat in monte tabernaculum : cumque ille consecutus fuisset eum cum fratribus suis, in eodem monte Galaad fixit tentorium.
     EN: Jacob had already pitched his tent on the mountain. When Laban overtook him with his kinsmen, he pitched his own tent on the same mountain of Gilead.

31:26  LA: Et dixit ad Jacob : Quare ita egisti, ut clam me abigeres filias meas quasi captivas gladio ?
     EN: He said to Jacob, “Why have you acted like this, secretly driving my daughters away from me like captives taken by the sword?

31:27  LA: cur ignorante me fugere voluisti, nec indicare mihi, ut prosequerer te cum gaudio, et canticis, et tympanis, et citharis ?
     EN: Why did you choose to flee without my knowledge and not tell me, so that I might send you away with joy, songs, tambourines, and lyres?

31:28  LA: Non es passus ut oscularer filios meos et filias : stulte operatus es : et nunc quidem
     EN: You did not allow me to kiss my sons and daughters. You have acted foolishly. And now indeed

31:29  LA: valet manus mea reddere tibi malum : sed Deus patris vestri heri dixit mihi : Cave ne loquaris contra Jacob quidquam durius.
     EN: my hand has power to repay you with harm, but yesterday the God of your father said to me, ‘Take care not to speak anything harsh against Jacob.’

31:30  LA: Esto, ad tuos ire cupiebas, et desiderio erat tibi domus patris tui : cur furatus es deos meos ?
     EN: Granted, you wished to go to your own people and longed for your father's house; but why did you steal my gods?”

31:31  LA: Respondit Jacob : Quod inscio te profectus sum, timui ne violenter auferres filias tuas.
     EN: Jacob answered, “I departed without your knowledge because I feared you would take your daughters away by force.

31:32  LA: Quod autem furti me arguis : apud quemcumque inveneris deos tuos, necetur coram fratribus nostris : scrutare, quidquid tuorum apud me inveneris, et aufer. Hæc dicens, ignorabat quod Rachel furata esset idola.
     EN: As for your accusation of theft, whoever is found with your gods shall be killed before our kinsmen. Search for anything of yours you find with me and take it.” In saying this, he did not know that Rachel had stolen the idols.

31:33  LA: Ingressus itaque Laban tabernaculum Jacob, et Liæ, et utriusque famulæ, non invenit. Cumque intrasset tentorium Rachelis,
     EN: Laban entered Jacob's tent, Leah's, and those of both maidservants, but found nothing. When he entered Rachel's tent,

31:34  LA: illa festinans abscondit idola subter stramenta cameli, et sedit desuper : scrutantique omne tentorium, et nihil invenienti,
     EN: she quickly hid the idols beneath the camel's saddle covering and sat upon it. As he searched the whole tent and found nothing,

31:35  LA: ait : Ne irascatur dominus meus quod coram te assurgere nequeo : quia juxta consuetudinem feminarum nunc accidit mihi : sic delusa sollicitudo quærentis est.
     EN: she said, “Let my lord not be angry that I cannot rise before you, for the customary condition of women is now upon me.” Thus his careful search was thwarted.

31:36  LA: Tumensque Jacob, cum jurgio ait : Quam ob culpam meam, et ob quod peccatum meum sic exarsisti post me,
     EN: Jacob, swelling with anger, said in rebuke, “For what fault and what sin of mine have you pursued me so furiously

31:37  LA: et scrutatus es omnem supellectilem meam ? quid invenisti de cuncta substantia domus tuæ ? pone hic coram fratribus meis, et fratribus tuis, et judicent inter me et te.
     EN: and searched all my goods? What have you found from all your household's possessions? Put it here before my kinsmen and yours, and let them judge between me and you.

31:38  LA: Idcirco viginti annis fui tecum ? oves tuæ et capræ steriles non fuerunt, arietes gregis tui non comedi :
     EN: Was this why I was with you twenty years? Your sheep and goats were not barren; I did not eat the rams of your flock.

31:39  LA: nec captum a bestia ostendi tibi, ego damnum omne reddebam : quidquid furto peribat, a me exigebas :
     EN: I did not show you an animal taken by a wild beast; I repaid every loss myself. Whatever was lost by theft you demanded from me.

31:40  LA: die noctuque æstu urebar, et gelu, fugiebatque somnus ab oculis meis.
     EN: By day and night I was scorched by heat and frost, and sleep fled from my eyes.

31:41  LA: Sicque per viginti annos in domo tua servivi tibi, quatuordecim pro filiabus, et sex pro gregibus tuis : immutasti quoque mercedem meam decem vicibus.
     EN: Thus for twenty years I served you in your house: fourteen for your daughters and six for your flocks. You also changed my wages ten times.

31:42  LA: Nisi Deus patris mei Abraham, et timor Isaac affuisset mihi, forsitan modo nudum me dimisisses : afflictionem meam et laborem manuum mearum respexit Deus, et arguit te heri.
     EN: Had the God of my father Abraham and the Fear of Isaac not been with me, perhaps you would now have sent me away empty. God saw my affliction and the labor of my hands, and rebuked you yesterday.”

31:43  LA: Respondit ei Laban : Filiæ meæ et filii, et greges tui, et omnia quæ cernis, mea sunt : quid possum facere filiis et nepotibus meis ?
     EN: Laban answered him, “The daughters are mine, the sons are mine, your flocks and everything you see are mine. What can I do to my children and grandchildren?

31:44  LA: Veni ergo, et ineamus fœdus, ut sit in testimonium inter me et te.
     EN: Come, then, let us make a covenant, to be a testimony between me and you.”

31:45  LA: Tulit itaque Jacob lapidem, et erexit illum in titulum :
     EN: Jacob took a stone and erected it as a pillar.

31:46  LA: dixitque fratribus suis : Afferte lapides. Qui congregantes fecerunt tumulum, comederuntque super eum :
     EN: He said to his kinsmen, “Bring stones.” They gathered them, made a mound, and ate upon it.

31:47  LA: quem vocavit Laban Tumulum testis : et Jacob, Acervum testimonii, uterque juxta proprietatem linguæ suæ.
     EN: Laban called it Mound of Witness, and Jacob Heap of Testimony, each according to his own language.

31:48  LA: Dixitque Laban : Tumulus iste erit testis inter me et te hodie, et idcirco appellatum est nomen ejus Galaad, id est, Tumulus testis.
     EN: Laban said, “This mound shall be a witness between me and you today.” Therefore it was named Gilead, that is, Mound of Witness.

31:49  LA: Intueatur et judicet Dominus inter nos quando recesserimus a nobis,
     EN: “May the Lord watch and judge between us when we have departed from one another,

31:50  LA: si afflixeris filias meas, et si introduxeris alias uxores super eas : nullus sermonis nostri testis est absque Deo, qui præsens respicit.
     EN: if you afflict my daughters or take other wives besides them. No one witnesses our words except God, who is present and sees.”

31:51  LA: Dixitque rursus ad Jacob : En tumulus hic, et lapis quem erexi inter me et te,
     EN: He said again to Jacob, “See this mound and the stone I have erected between me and you;

31:52  LA: testis erit : tumulus, inquam, iste et lapis sint in testimonium, si aut ego transiero illum pergens ad te, aut tu præterieris, malum mihi cogitans.
     EN: they shall be a witness. This mound and stone, I say, shall be testimony if I cross beyond them to you, or if you cross beyond them intending harm to me.

31:53  LA: Deus Abraham, et Deus Nachor, judicet inter nos, Deus patris eorum. Juravit ergo Jacob per timorem patris sui Isaac :
     EN: May the God of Abraham and the God of Nahor, the God of their father, judge between us.” Jacob swore by the Fear of his father Isaac.

31:54  LA: immolatisque victimis in monte, vocavit fratres suos ut ederent panem. Qui cum comedissent, manserunt ibi :
     EN: He offered sacrifices on the mountain and called his kinsmen to eat bread. They ate and stayed there.

31:55  LA: Laban vero de nocte consurgens, osculatus est filios, et filias suas, et benedixit illis : reversusque est in locum suum.
     EN: Laban rose in the night, kissed his sons and daughters, blessed them, and returned to his own place.

GENESIS 32

32:1  LA: Jacob quoque abiit itinere quo cœperat : fueruntque ei obviam angeli Dei.
     EN: Jacob also went on the journey he had begun, and angels of God met him.

32:2  LA: Quos cum vidisset, ait : Castra Dei sunt hæc : et appellavit nomen loci illius Mahanaim, id est, Castra.
     EN: When he saw them, he said, “This is God's camp.” He named that place Mahanaim, that is, Camps.

32:3  LA: Misit autem et nuntios ante se ad Esau fratrem suum in terram Seir, in regionem Edom :
     EN: He sent messengers ahead of him to his brother Esau in the land of Seir, in the region of Edom,

32:4  LA: præcepitque eis, dicens : Sic loquimini domino meo Esau : Hæc dicit frater tuus Jacob : Apud Laban peregrinatus sum, et fui usque in præsentem diem.
     EN: and commanded them, saying, “Speak this way to my lord Esau: ‘Your brother Jacob says this: I have sojourned with Laban and remained until the present day.

32:5  LA: Habeo boves, et asinos, et oves, et servos, et ancillas : mittoque nunc legationem ad dominum meum, ut inveniam gratiam in conspectu tuo.
     EN: I have oxen, donkeys, sheep, male servants, and maidservants. Now I send a delegation to my lord, so that I may find favor in your sight.’”

32:6  LA: Reversique sunt nuntii ad Jacob, dicentes : Venimus ad Esau fratrem tuum, et ecce properat tibi in occursum cum quadringentis viris.
     EN: The messengers returned to Jacob, saying, “We came to your brother Esau, and he is hurrying to meet you with four hundred men.”

32:7  LA: Timuit Jacob valde : et perterritus divisit populum qui secum erat, greges quoque et oves, et boves, et camelos, in duas turmas,
     EN: Jacob was greatly afraid. In terror he divided the people with him, and the flocks, sheep, oxen, and camels, into two companies,

32:8  LA: dicens : Si venerit Esau ad unam turmam, et percusserit eam, alia turma, quæ relicta est, salvabitur.
     EN: saying, “If Esau comes upon one company and strikes it, the other company left behind shall escape.”

32:9  LA: Dixitque Jacob : Deus patris mei Abraham, et Deus patris mei Isaac : Domine qui dixisti mihi : Revertere in terram tuam, et in locum nativitatis tuæ, et benefaciam tibi :
     EN: Jacob said, “God of my father Abraham and God of my father Isaac, Lord who said to me, ‘Return to your land and birthplace, and I will do you good,’

32:10  LA: minor sum cunctis miserationibus tuis, et veritate tua quam explevisti servo tuo. In baculo meo transivi Jordanem istum : et nunc cum duabus turmis regredior.
     EN: I am unworthy of all Your mercies and the faithfulness You have shown Your servant. With my staff I crossed this Jordan, and now I return with two companies.

32:11  LA: Erue me de manu fratris mei Esau, quia valde eum timeo : ne forte veniens percutiat matrem cum filiis.
     EN: Deliver me from the hand of my brother Esau, for I greatly fear him, lest he come and strike down mother with children.

32:12  LA: Tu locutus es quod benefaceres mihi, et dilatares semen meum sicut arenam maris, quæ præ multitudine numerari non potest.
     EN: You have said that You would do me good and increase my offspring like the sand of the sea, which cannot be numbered for its multitude.”

32:13  LA: Cumque dormisset ibi nocte illa, separavit de his quæ habebat, munera Esau fratri suo,
     EN: After spending that night there, he set apart from what he had gifts for his brother Esau:

32:14  LA: capras ducentas, hircos viginti, oves ducentas, et arietes viginti,
     EN: two hundred female goats and twenty male goats, two hundred ewes and twenty rams,

32:15  LA: camelos fœtas cum pullis suis triginta, vaccas quadraginta, et tauros viginti, asinas viginti et pullos earum decem.
     EN: thirty nursing camels with their young, forty cows and twenty bulls, twenty female donkeys and ten of their young.

32:16  LA: Et misit per manus servorum suorum singulos seorsum greges, dixitque pueris suis : Antecedite me, et sit spatium inter gregem et gregem.
     EN: He sent each herd separately in the hands of his servants and said to them, “Go ahead of me, and leave a space between one herd and the next.”

32:17  LA: Et præcepit priori, dicens : Si obvium habueris fratrem meum Esau, et interrogaverit te : Cujus es ? aut, Quo vadis ? aut, Cujus sunt ista quæ sequeris ?
     EN: He commanded the first, saying, “If you meet my brother Esau and he asks you, ‘Whose are you?’ or ‘Where are you going?’ or ‘Whose are these that you follow?’

32:18  LA: respondebis : Servi tui Jacob, munera misit domino meo Esau, ipse quoque post nos venit.
     EN: you shall answer, ‘They belong to your servant Jacob. He has sent gifts to my lord Esau, and he himself is also coming behind us.’”

32:19  LA: Similiter dedit mandata secundo, et tertio, et cunctis qui sequebantur greges, dicens : Iisdem verbis loquimini ad Esau cum inveneritis eum.
     EN: He gave the same commands to the second, the third, and all who followed the herds, saying, “Speak these same words to Esau when you meet him.

32:20  LA: Et addetis : Ipse quoque servus tuus Jacob iter nostrum insequitur. Dixit enim : Placabo illum muneribus quæ præcedunt, et postea videbo illum : forsitan propitiabitur mihi.
     EN: Add, ‘Your servant Jacob himself is also following our route.’” For he said, “I will appease him with the gifts that go ahead, and afterward I will see him. Perhaps he will be favorable to me.”

32:21  LA: Præcesserunt itaque munera ante eum, ipse vero mansit nocte illa in castris.
     EN: So the gifts went ahead of him, while he remained that night in the camp.

32:22  LA: Cumque mature surrexisset, tulit duas uxores suas, et totidem famulas cum undecim filiis, et transivit vadum Jaboc.
     EN: He rose early, took his two wives, his two maidservants, and his eleven sons, and crossed the ford of the Jabbok.

32:23  LA: Traductisque omnibus quæ ad se pertinebant,
     EN: After bringing across everything that belonged to him,

32:24  LA: mansit solus : et ecce vir luctabatur cum eo usque mane.
     EN: he remained alone. And a man wrestled with him until morning.

32:25  LA: Qui cum videret quod eum superare non posset, tetigit nervum femoris ejus, et statim emarcuit.
     EN: When he saw that he could not overcome him, he touched the sinew of his thigh, and it immediately withered.

32:26  LA: Dixitque ad eum : Dimitte me : jam enim ascendit aurora. Respondit : Non dimittam te, nisi benedixeris mihi.
     EN: He said to him, “Let me go, for dawn is now rising.” He answered, “I will not let you go unless you bless me.”

32:27  LA: Ait ergo : Quod nomen est tibi ? Respondit : Jacob.
     EN: He said, “What is your name?” He answered, “Jacob.”

32:28  LA: At ille : Nequaquam, inquit, Jacob appellabitur nomen tuum, sed Israël : quoniam si contra Deum fortis fuisti, quanto magis contra homines prævalebis ?
     EN: He said, “Your name shall no longer be called Jacob, but Israel; for if you have been strong against God, how much more shall you prevail against men?”

32:29  LA: Interrogavit eum Jacob : Dic mihi, quo appellaris nomine ? Respondit : Cur quæris nomen meum ? Et benedixit ei in eodem loco.
     EN: Jacob asked him, “Tell me, by what name are you called?” He answered, “Why do you ask My name?” And He blessed him in that same place.

32:30  LA: Vocavitque Jacob nomen loci illius Phanuel, dicens : Vidi Deum facie ad faciem, et salva facta est anima mea.
     EN: Jacob named that place Peniel, saying, “I have seen God face to face, and my life has been saved.”

32:31  LA: Ortusque est ei statim sol, postquam transgressus est Phanuel : ipse vero claudicabat pede.
     EN: The sun rose upon him immediately after he passed Peniel, and he limped upon his foot.

32:32  LA: Quam ob causam non comedunt nervum filii Israël, qui emarcuit in femore Jacob, usque in præsentem diem : eo quod tetigerit nervum femoris ejus, et obstupuerit.
     EN: For this reason the children of Israel do not eat the sinew that withered in Jacob's thigh, to the present day, because he touched the sinew of his thigh and it became numb.

GENESIS 33

33:1  LA: Elevans autem Jacob oculos suos, vidit venientem Esau, et cum eo quadringentos viros : divisitque filios Liæ et Rachel, ambarumque famularum :
     EN: Jacob raised his eyes and saw Esau coming, and with him four hundred men. He divided the children of Leah, of Rachel, and of the two maidservants.

33:2  LA: et posuit utramque ancillam, et liberos earum, in principio : Liam vero, et filios ejus, in secundo loco : Rachel autem et Joseph novissimos.
     EN: He placed the two maidservants and their children first, Leah and her sons second, and Rachel and Joseph last.

33:3  LA: Et ipse progrediens adoravit pronus in terram septies, donec appropinquaret frater ejus.
     EN: He himself went ahead and bowed facedown to the ground seven times until his brother drew near.

33:4  LA: Currens itaque Esau obviam fratri suo, amplexatus est eum : stringensque collum ejus, et osculans flevit.
     EN: Esau ran to meet his brother, embraced him, clasped his neck, kissed him, and wept.

33:5  LA: Levatisque oculis, vidit mulieres et parvulos earum, et ait : Quid sibi volunt isti ? et si ad te pertinent ? Respondit : Parvuli sunt quos donavit mihi Deus servo tuo.
     EN: He raised his eyes, saw the women and their little ones, and said, “Who are these? Do they belong to you?” He answered, “They are the little ones God has given me, your servant.”

33:6  LA: Et appropinquantes ancillæ et filii earum, incurvati sunt.
     EN: The maidservants and their children approached and bowed.

33:7  LA: Accessit quoque Lia cum pueris suis : et cum similiter adorassent, extremi Joseph et Rachel adoraverunt.
     EN: Leah also approached with her children and bowed in the same way; last, Joseph and Rachel bowed.

33:8  LA: Dixitque Esau : Quænam sunt istæ turmæ quas obviam habui ? Respondit : Ut invenirem gratiam coram domino meo.
     EN: Esau said, “What are those companies I met?” He answered, “They are so that I might find favor before my lord.”

33:9  LA: At ille ait : Habeo plurima, frater mi, sint tua tibi.
     EN: He said, “I have plenty, my brother. Keep what is yours.”

33:10  LA: Dixitque Jacob : Noli ita, obsecro : sed si inveni gratiam in oculis tuis, accipe munusculum de manibus meis. Sic enim vidi faciem tuam, quasi viderim vultum Dei : esto mihi propitius,
     EN: Jacob said, “Please, not so. If I have found favor in your eyes, accept this little gift from my hands. For I have seen your face as though I had seen the face of God. Be favorable to me,

33:11  LA: et suscipe benedictionem quam attuli tibi, et quam donavit mihi Deus tribuens omnia. Vix fratre compellente, suscipiens,
     EN: and accept the blessing I have brought you, which God, who grants all things, gave me.” Only at his brother's urging did he accept it,

33:12  LA: ait : Gradiamur simul, eroque socius itineris tui.
     EN: and say, “Let us travel together, and I will accompany you on your journey.”

33:13  LA: Dixitque Jacob : Nosti, domine mi, quod parvulos habeam teneros, et oves, et boves fœtas mecum : quas si plus in ambulando fecero laborare, morientur una die cuncti greges.
     EN: Jacob said, “My lord, you know that my little ones are tender, and I have nursing sheep and cattle with me. If I make them work too hard on the journey, all the flocks will die in one day.

33:14  LA: Præcedat dominus meus ante servum suum : et ego sequar paulatim vestigia ejus, sicut videro parvulos meos posse, donec veniam ad dominum meum in Seir.
     EN: Let my lord go ahead of his servant, and I will follow his steps gradually, as I see that my little ones are able, until I come to my lord in Seir.”

33:15  LA: Respondit Esau : Oro te, ut de populo qui mecum est, saltem socii remaneant viæ tuæ. Non est, inquit, necesse : hoc uno tantum indigeo, ut inveniam gratiam in conspectu tuo, domine mi.
     EN: Esau answered, “Please let at least some companions from the people with me remain for your journey.” He said, “There is no need. I need only this: to find favor in your sight, my lord.”

33:16  LA: Reversus est itaque illo die Esau itinere quo venerat in Seir.
     EN: That day Esau returned to Seir by the way he had come.

33:17  LA: Et Jacob venit in Socoth : ubi ædificata domo et fixis tentoriis appellavit nomen loci illius Socoth, id est, Tabernacula.
     EN: Jacob came to Succoth, where he built a house and pitched tents, and named the place Succoth, that is, Tents.

33:18  LA: Transivitque in Salem urbem Sichimorum, quæ est in terra Chanaan, postquam reversus est de Mesopotamia Syriæ : et habitavit juxta oppidum.
     EN: After returning from Mesopotamia of Syria, he passed into Salem, a city of the Shechemites in the land of Canaan, and lived beside the town.

33:19  LA: Emitque partem agri, in qua fixerat tabernacula, a filiis Hemor patris Sichem centum agnis.
     EN: He bought the portion of the field where he had pitched his tents from the sons of Hamor, Shechem's father, for one hundred lambs.

33:20  LA: Et erecto ibi altari, invocavit super illud fortissimum Deum Israël.
     EN: He erected an altar there and at it called upon the most mighty God of Israel.

GENESIS 34

34:1  LA: Egressa est autem Dina filia Liæ ut videret mulieres regionis illius.
     EN: Dinah, Leah's daughter, went out to see the women of that region.

34:2  LA: Quam cum vidisset Sichem filius Hemor Hevæi, princeps terræ illius, adamavit eam : et rapuit, et dormivit cum illa, vi opprimens virginem.
     EN: Shechem son of Hamor the Hivite, prince of that land, saw her and fell in love with her. He seized her and lay with her, violating the virgin by force.

34:3  LA: Et conglutinata est anima ejus cum ea, tristemque delinivit blanditiis.
     EN: His soul clung to her, and he soothed her sadness with tender words.

34:4  LA: Et pergens ad Hemor patrem suum : Accipe, inquit, mihi puellam hanc conjugem.
     EN: He went to his father Hamor and said, “Take this young woman for me as a wife.”

34:5  LA: Quod cum audisset Jacob absentibus filiis, et in pastu pecorum occupatis, siluit donec redirent.
     EN: Jacob heard this while his sons were absent and occupied in tending the animals, and he kept silent until they returned.

34:6  LA: Egresso autem Hemor patre Sichem ut loqueretur ad Jacob,
     EN: Hamor, Shechem's father, went out to speak to Jacob,

34:7  LA: ecce filii ejus veniebant de agro : auditoque quod acciderat, irati sunt valde, eo quod fœdam rem operatus esset in Israël et, violata filia Jacob, rem illicitam perpetrasset.
