     EN: and his sons came in from the field. Hearing what had happened, they were very angry, because he had done a disgraceful thing in Israel and committed an unlawful act by violating Jacob's daughter.

34:8  LA: Locutus est itaque Hemor ad eos : Sichem filii mei adhæsit anima filiæ vestræ : date eam illi uxorem :
     EN: Hamor spoke to them: “My son Shechem's soul clings to your daughter. Give her to him as a wife.

34:9  LA: et jungamus vicissim connubia : filias vestras tradite nobis, et filias nostras accipite,
     EN: Let us intermarry: give us your daughters and take ours,

34:10  LA: et habitate nobiscum : terra in potestate vestra est : exercete, negotiamini, et possidete eam.
     EN: and live with us. The land is at your disposal; cultivate it, trade, and possess it.”

34:11  LA: Sed et Sichem ad patrem et ad fratres ejus ait : Inveniam gratiam coram vobis : et quæcumque statueritis, dabo :
     EN: Shechem also said to her father and brothers, “Let me find favor before you, and whatever you determine I will give.

34:12  LA: augete dotem, et munera postulate, et libenter tribuam quod petieritis : tantum date mihi puellam hanc uxorem.
     EN: Increase the marriage payment and ask for gifts, and I will gladly give whatever you request. Only give me this young woman as my wife.”

34:13  LA: Responderunt filii Jacob Sichem et patri ejus in dolo, sævientes ob stuprum sororis :
     EN: Jacob's sons answered Shechem and his father deceitfully, enraged at their sister's violation:

34:14  LA: Non possumus facere quod petitis, nec dare sororem nostram homini incircumciso : quod illicitum et nefarium est apud nos.
     EN: “We cannot do what you ask or give our sister to an uncircumcised man, for that is unlawful and shameful among us.

34:15  LA: Sed in hoc valebimus fœderari, si volueritis esse similes nostri, et circumcidatur in vobis omne masculini sexus ;
     EN: But we can enter a covenant on this condition: that you become like us and every male among you be circumcised.

34:16  LA: tunc dabimus et accipiemus mutuo filias vestras ac nostras : et habitabimus vobiscum, erimusque unus populus.
     EN: Then we shall give and receive your daughters and ours in marriage, live with you, and become one people.

34:17  LA: Si autem circumcidi nolueritis, tollemus filiam nostram, et recedemus.
     EN: But if you are unwilling to be circumcised, we will take our daughter and depart.”

34:18  LA: Placuit oblatio eorum Hemor, et Sichem filio ejus,
     EN: Their proposal pleased Hamor and his son Shechem.

34:19  LA: nec distulit adolescens quin statim quod petebatur expleret : amabat enim puellam valde, et ipse erat inclytus in omni domo patris sui.
     EN: The young man did not delay in immediately fulfilling what was requested, for he loved the young woman greatly, and he was renowned throughout his father's house.

34:20  LA: Ingressique portam urbis, locuti sunt ad populum :
     EN: They entered the city's gate and spoke to the people:

34:21  LA: Viri isti pacifici sunt, et volunt habitare nobiscum : negotientur in terra, et exerceant eam, quæ spatiosa et lata cultoribus indiget : filias eorum accipiemus uxores, et nostras illis dabimus.
     EN: “These men are peaceful and wish to live with us. Let them trade in the land and cultivate it, for it is spacious and wide and needs cultivators. We will take their daughters as wives and give them ours.

34:22  LA: Unum est quo differtur tantum bonum : si circumcidamus masculos nostros, ritum gentis imitantes.
     EN: There is only one condition delaying so great a benefit: that we circumcise our males, following the custom of their nation.

34:23  LA: Et substantia eorum, et pecora, et cuncta quæ possident, nostra erunt : tantum in hoc acquiescamus, et habitantes simul, unum efficiemus populum.
     EN: Their possessions, their livestock, and everything they own will be ours. Only let us agree to this, and by living together we shall become one people.”

34:24  LA: Assensique sunt omnes, circumcisis cunctis maribus.
     EN: Everyone agreed, and all the males were circumcised.

34:25  LA: Et ecce, die tertio, quando gravissimus vulnerum dolor est : arreptis duo filii Jacob, Simeon et Levi fratres Dinæ, gladiis, ingressi sunt urbem confidenter : interfectisque omnibus masculis,
     EN: On the third day, when the pain of the wounds is most severe, Jacob's two sons Simeon and Levi, Dinah's brothers, took swords and boldly entered the city. They killed every male,

34:26  LA: Hemor et Sichem pariter necaverunt, tollentes Dinam de domo Sichem sororem suam.
     EN: and also killed Hamor and Shechem, taking their sister Dinah from Shechem's house.

34:27  LA: Quibus egressis, irruerunt super occisos ceteri filii Jacob : et depopulati sunt urbem in ultionem stupri.
     EN: After they came out, Jacob's other sons fell upon the slain and plundered the city in revenge for the violation.

34:28  LA: Oves eorum, et armenta, et asinos, cunctaque vastantes quæ in domibus et in agris erant,
     EN: They seized their sheep, herds, and donkeys, and devastated everything in the houses and fields,

34:29  LA: parvulos quoque eorum et uxores duxerunt captivas.
     EN: and also led their little ones and wives away captive.

34:30  LA: Quibus patratis audacter, Jacob dixit ad Simeon et Levi : Turbastis me, et odiosum fecistis me Chananæis, et Pherezæis habitatoribus terræ hujus : nos pauci sumus ; illi congregati percutient me, et delebor ego, et domus mea.
     EN: When they had boldly done these things, Jacob said to Simeon and Levi, “You have troubled me and made me hateful to the Canaanites and Perizzites who inhabit this land. We are few; they will gather and strike me, and I and my household shall be destroyed.”

34:31  LA: Responderunt : Numquid ut scorto abuti debuere sorore nostra ?
     EN: They answered, “Should they have used our sister like a prostitute?”

GENESIS 35

35:1  LA: Interea locutus est Deus ad Jacob : Surge, et ascende Bethel, et habita ibi, facque altare Deo qui apparuit tibi quando fugiebas Esau fratrem tuum.
     EN: Meanwhile God spoke to Jacob: “Rise, go up to Bethel, and live there. Make an altar to God, who appeared to you when you were fleeing your brother Esau.”

35:2  LA: Jacob vero convocata omni domo sua, ait : Abjicite deos alienos qui in medio vestri sunt, et mundamini, ac mutate vestimenta vestra.
     EN: Jacob called together his whole household and said, “Cast away the foreign gods among you, purify yourselves, and change your garments.

35:3  LA: Surgite, et ascendamus in Bethel, ut faciamus ibi altare Deo : qui exaudivit me in die tribulationis meæ, et socius fuit itineris mei.
     EN: Rise, and let us go up to Bethel, so that we may make an altar there to God, who heard me in the day of my distress and accompanied me on my journey.”

35:4  LA: Dederunt ergo ei omnes deos alienos quos habebant, et inaures quæ erant in auribus eorum : at ille infodit ea subter terebinthum, quæ est post urbem Sichem.
     EN: They gave him all the foreign gods they had and the earrings in their ears, and he buried them beneath the terebinth behind the city of Shechem.

35:5  LA: Cumque profecti essent, terror Dei invasit omnes per circuitum civitates, et non sunt ausi persequi recedentes.
     EN: When they set out, the terror of God seized all the surrounding cities, and they did not dare pursue them as they departed.

35:6  LA: Venit igitur Jacob Luzam, quæ est in terra Chanaan, cognomento Bethel : ipse et omnis populus cum eo.
     EN: Jacob came to Luz, which is in the land of Canaan and is also called Bethel, he and all the people with him.

35:7  LA: Ædificavitque ibi altare, et appellavit nomen loci illius, Domus Dei : ibi enim apparuit ei Deus cum fugeret fratrem suum.
     EN: He built an altar there and named the place House of God, for there God had appeared to him when he fled his brother.

35:8  LA: Eodem tempore mortua est Debora nutrix Rebeccæ, et sepulta est ad radices Bethel subter quercum : vocatumque est nomen loci illius, Quercus fletus.
     EN: At that time Deborah, Rebekah's nurse, died and was buried at the foot of Bethel beneath an oak. That place was named Oak of Weeping.

35:9  LA: Apparuit autem iterum Deus Jacob postquam reversus est de Mesopotamia Syriæ, benedixitque ei
     EN: God appeared again to Jacob after he returned from Mesopotamia of Syria, and blessed him,

35:10  LA: dicens : Non vocaberis ultra Jacob, sed Israël erit nomen tuum. Et appellavit eum Israël,
     EN: saying, “You shall no longer be called Jacob, but Israel shall be your name.” He called him Israel

35:11  LA: dixitque ei : Ego Deus omnipotens : cresce, et multiplicare : gentes et populi nationum ex te erunt, reges de lumbis tuis egredientur,
     EN: and said to him, “I am God Almighty. Increase and multiply. Nations and peoples of nations shall come from you, and kings shall come forth from your loins.

35:12  LA: terramque quam dedi Abraham et Isaac, dabo tibi et semini tuo post te.
     EN: The land I gave to Abraham and Isaac I will give to you and your offspring after you.”

35:13  LA: Et recessit ab eo.
     EN: And He departed from him.

35:14  LA: Ille vero erexit titulum lapideum in loco quo locutus fuerat ei Deus : libans super eum libamina, et effundens oleum :
     EN: Jacob erected a stone pillar in the place where God had spoken to him, pouring drink offerings and oil upon it,

35:15  LA: vocansque nomen loci illius Bethel.
     EN: and naming that place Bethel.

35:16  LA: Egressus autem inde, venit verno tempore ad terram quæ ducit Ephratam : in qua cum parturiret Rachel,
     EN: He departed from there and in springtime came to the land leading to Ephrath. There Rachel was in labor,

35:17  LA: ob difficultatem partus periclitari cœpit. Dixitque ei obstetrix : Noli timere, quia et hunc habebis filium.
     EN: and because of the difficult birth she began to be in danger. The midwife said to her, “Do not fear, for you shall have this son also.”

35:18  LA: Egrediente autem anima præ dolore, et imminente jam morte, vocavit nomen filii sui Benomi, id est, Filius doloris mei : pater vero appellavit eum Benjamin, id est, Filius dextræ.
     EN: As her soul departed in pain and death drew near, she named her son Benoni, that is, Son of My Sorrow; but his father called him Benjamin, that is, Son of the Right Hand.

35:19  LA: Mortua est ergo Rachel, et sepulta est in via quæ ducit Ephratam, hæc est Bethlehem.
     EN: Rachel died and was buried on the road leading to Ephrath, which is Bethlehem.

35:20  LA: Erexitque Jacob titulum super sepulchrum ejus : hic est titulus monumenti Rachel, usque in præsentem diem.
     EN: Jacob erected a pillar over her grave; this is the pillar of Rachel's tomb to the present day.

35:21  LA: Egressus inde, fixit tabernaculum trans Turrem gregis.
     EN: He departed from there and pitched his tent beyond the Tower of the Flock.

35:22a (Vg 35:22)  LA: Cumque habitaret in illa regione, abiit Ruben, et dormivit cum Bala concubina patris sui : quod illum minime latuit. Erant autem filii Jacob duodecim.
     EN: While he lived in that region, Reuben went and slept with Bilhah, his father's concubine, and this did not escape his father's knowledge. Jacob had twelve sons.

35:22b (Vg 35:22)  LA: Cumque habitaret in illa regione, abiit Ruben, et dormivit cum Bala concubina patris sui : quod illum minime latuit. Erant autem filii Jacob duodecim.
     EN: While he lived in that region, Reuben went and slept with Bilhah, his father's concubine, and this did not escape his father's knowledge. Jacob had twelve sons.

35:23  LA: Filii Liæ : primogenitus Ruben, et Simeon, et Levi, et Judas, et Issachar, et Zabulon.
     EN: Leah's sons were Reuben the firstborn, Simeon, Levi, Judah, Issachar, and Zebulun.

35:24  LA: Filii Rachel : Joseph et Benjamin.
     EN: Rachel's sons were Joseph and Benjamin.

35:25  LA: Filii Balæ ancillæ Rachelis : Dan et Nephthali.
     EN: The sons of Bilhah, Rachel's maidservant, were Dan and Naphtali.

35:26  LA: Filii Zelphæ ancillæ Liæ : Gad et Aser : hi sunt filii Jacob, qui nati sunt ei in Mesopotamia Syriæ.
     EN: The sons of Zilpah, Leah's maidservant, were Gad and Asher. These are Jacob's sons who were born to him in Mesopotamia of Syria.

35:27  LA: Venit etiam ad Isaac patrem suum in Mambre, civitatem Arbee, hæc est Hebron, in qua peregrinatus est Abraham et Isaac.
     EN: He also came to his father Isaac in Mamre, the city of Arba, which is Hebron, where Abraham and Isaac had sojourned.

35:28  LA: Et completi sunt dies Isaac centum octoginta annorum.
     EN: Isaac's days were completed at one hundred eighty years.

35:29  LA: Consumptusque ætate mortuus est : et appositus est populo suo senex et plenus dierum : et sepelierunt eum Esau et Jacob filii sui.
     EN: Worn out with age, he died and was joined to his people, old and full of days. His sons Esau and Jacob buried him.

GENESIS 36

36:1  LA: Hæ sunt autem generationes Esau, ipse est Edom.
     EN: These are the generations of Esau, who is Edom.

36:2  LA: Esau accepit uxores de filiabus Chanaan : Ada filiam Elon Hethæi, et Oolibama filiam Anæ filiæ Sebeon Hevæi :
     EN: Esau took wives from the daughters of Canaan: Adah, daughter of Elon the Hittite, and Oholibamah, daughter of Anah, the daughter of Zibeon the Hivite,

36:3  LA: Basemath quoque filiam Ismaël sororem Nabaioth.
     EN: and also Basemath, daughter of Ishmael and sister of Nebaioth.

36:4  LA: Peperit autem Ada Eliphaz : Basemath genuit Rahuel :
     EN: Adah bore Eliphaz, and Basemath bore Reuel.

36:5  LA: Oolibama genuit Jehus et Ihelon et Core. Hi filii Esau qui nati sunt ei in terra Chanaan.
     EN: Oholibamah bore Jeush, Jalam, and Korah. These are Esau's sons who were born to him in the land of Canaan.

36:6  LA: Tulit autem Esau uxores suas et filios et filias, et omnem animam domus suæ, et substantiam, et pecora, et cuncta quæ habere poterat in terra Chanaan : et abiit in alteram regionem, recessitque a fratre suo Jacob.
     EN: Esau took his wives, sons, daughters, every person of his household, his possessions, livestock, and everything he could possess in the land of Canaan, and went to another region, away from his brother Jacob.

36:7  LA: Divites enim erant valde, et simul habitare non poterant : nec sustinebat eos terra peregrinationis eorum præ multitudine gregum.
     EN: For they were very rich and could not live together, and the land of their sojourning could not support them because of the multitude of their flocks.

36:8  LA: Habitavitque Esau in monte Seir, ipse est Edom.
     EN: Esau lived in the mountain of Seir; he is Edom.

36:9  LA: Hæ autem sunt generationes Esau patris Edom in monte Seir,
     EN: These are the generations of Esau, father of Edom, in the mountain of Seir,

36:10  LA: et hæc nomina filiorum ejus : Eliphaz filius Ada uxoris Esau : Rahuel quoque filius Basemath uxoris ejus.
     EN: and these are his sons' names: Eliphaz, son of Esau's wife Adah, and Reuel, son of his wife Basemath.

36:11  LA: Fueruntque Eliphaz filii : Theman, Omar, Sepho, et Gatham, et Cenez.
     EN: Eliphaz's sons were Teman, Omar, Zepho, Gatam, and Kenaz.

36:12  LA: Erat autem Thamna concubina Eliphaz filii Esau : quæ peperit ei Amalech. Hi sunt filii Ada uxoris Esau.
     EN: Timna was the concubine of Eliphaz, Esau's son, and bore him Amalek. These are the sons of Adah, Esau's wife.

36:13  LA: Filii autem Rahuel : Nahath et Zara, Samma et Meza : hi filii Basemath uxoris Esau.
     EN: Reuel's sons were Nahath, Zerah, Shammah, and Mizzah. These are the sons of Basemath, Esau's wife.

36:14  LA: Isti quoque erant filii Oolibama filiæ Anæ filiæ Sebeon, uxoris Esau, quos genuit ei, Jehus et Ihelon et Core.
     EN: These also were the sons whom Oholibamah, daughter of Anah, daughter of Zibeon, Esau's wife, bore him: Jeush, Jalam, and Korah.

36:15  LA: Hi duces filiorum Esau : filii Eliphaz primogeniti Esau : dux Theman, dux Omra, dux Sepho, dux Cenez,
     EN: These are the chiefs of Esau's sons, the sons of Eliphaz, Esau's firstborn: Chief Teman, Chief Omar, Chief Zepho, Chief Kenaz,

36:16  LA: dux Core, dux Gathan, dux Amalech. Hi filii Eliphaz in terra Edom, et hi filii Ada.
     EN: Chief Korah, Chief Gatam, and Chief Amalek. These are Eliphaz's sons in the land of Edom, and these are Adah's sons.

36:17  LA: Hi quoque filii Rahuel filii Esau : dux Nahath, dux Zara, dux Samma, dux Meza : hi autem duces Rahuel in terra Edom : isti filii Basemath uxoris Esau.
     EN: These also are the sons of Reuel, Esau's son: Chief Nahath, Chief Zerah, Chief Shammah, and Chief Mizzah. These are Reuel's chiefs in the land of Edom; these are the sons of Basemath, Esau's wife.

36:18  LA: Hi autem filii Oolibama uxoris Esau : dux Jehus, dux Ihelon, dux Core : hi duces Oolibama filiæ Anæ uxoris Esau.
     EN: These are the sons of Oholibamah, Esau's wife: Chief Jeush, Chief Jalam, and Chief Korah. These are the chiefs of Oholibamah, daughter of Anah, Esau's wife.

36:19  LA: Isti sunt filii Esau, et hi duces eorum : ipse est Edom.
     EN: These are Esau's sons, and these are their chiefs; he is Edom.

36:20  LA: Isti sunt filii Seir Horræi, habitatores terræ : Lotan, et Sobal, et Sebeon, et Ana,
     EN: These are the sons of Seir the Horite, inhabitants of the land: Lotan, Shobal, Zibeon, Anah,

36:21  LA: et Dison, et Eser, et Disan : hi duces Horræi, filii Seir in terra Edom.
     EN: Dishon, Ezer, and Dishan. These are the Horite chiefs, Seir's sons in the land of Edom.

36:22  LA: Facti sunt autem filii Lotan : Hori et Heman. Erat autem soror Lotan, Thamna.
     EN: Lotan's sons were Hori and Heman, and Lotan's sister was Timna.

36:23  LA: Et isti filii Sobal : Alvan et Manahat et Ebal, et Sepho et Onam.
     EN: These are Shobal's sons: Alvan, Manahath, Ebal, Shepho, and Onam.

36:24  LA: Et hi filii Sebeon : Aja et Ana. Iste est Ana qui invenit aquas calidas in solitudine, cum pasceret asinos Sebeon patris sui :
     EN: These are Zibeon's sons: Aiah and Anah. This is the Anah who discovered hot springs in the wilderness while tending the donkeys of his father Zibeon.

36:25  LA: habuitque filium Dison, et filiam Oolibama.
     EN: He had a son Dishon and a daughter Oholibamah.

36:26  LA: Et isti filii Dison : Hamdan, et Eseban, et Jethram, et Charan.
     EN: These are Dishon's sons: Hemdan, Eshban, Ithran, and Cheran.

36:27  LA: Hi quoque filii Eser : Balaan, et Zavan, et Acan.
     EN: These also are Ezer's sons: Bilhan, Zaavan, and Akan.

36:28  LA: Habuit autem filios Disan : Hus et Aram.
     EN: Dishan had sons Uz and Aran.

36:29  LA: Hi duces Horræorum : dux Lotan, dux Sobal, dux Sebeon, dux Ana,
     EN: These are the Horite chiefs: Chief Lotan, Chief Shobal, Chief Zibeon, Chief Anah,

36:30  LA: dux Dison, dux Eser, dux Disan : isti duces Horræorum qui imperaverunt in terra Seir.
     EN: Chief Dishon, Chief Ezer, and Chief Dishan. These are the Horite chiefs who ruled in the land of Seir.

36:31  LA: Reges autem qui regnaverunt in terra Edom antequam haberent regem filii Israël, fuerunt hi :
     EN: These were the kings who reigned in the land of Edom before the children of Israel had a king:

36:32  LA: Bela filius Beor, nomenque urbis ejus Denaba.
     EN: Bela son of Beor, whose city was named Dinhabah.

36:33  LA: Mortuus est autem Bela, et regnavit pro eo Jobab filius Zaræ de Bosra.
     EN: Bela died, and Jobab son of Zerah from Bozrah reigned in his place.

36:34  LA: Cumque mortuus esset Jobab, regnavit pro eo Husam de terra Themanorum.
     EN: When Jobab died, Husham from the land of the Temanites reigned in his place.

36:35  LA: Hoc quoque mortuo, regnavit pro eo Adad filius Badad, qui percussit Madian in regione Moab : et nomen urbis ejus Avith.
     EN: When he too died, Hadad son of Bedad, who struck Midian in the region of Moab, reigned in his place. His city was named Avith.

36:36  LA: Cumque mortuus esset Adad, regnavit pro eo Semla de Masreca.
     EN: When Hadad died, Samlah from Masrekah reigned in his place.

36:37  LA: Hoc quoque mortuo regnavit pro eo Saul de fluvio Rohoboth.
     EN: When he too died, Saul from the river Rehoboth reigned in his place.

36:38  LA: Cumque et hic obiisset, successit in regnum Balanan filius Achobor.
     EN: When he also died, Baal-hanan son of Achbor succeeded to the kingdom.

36:39  LA: Isto quoque mortuo regnavit pro eo Adar, nomenque urbis ejus Phau : et appellabatur uxor ejus Meetabel, filia Matred filiæ Mezaab.
     EN: When he too died, Hadar reigned in his place, and his city was named Pau. His wife was called Mehetabel, daughter of Matred, the daughter of Mezahab.

36:40  LA: Hæc ergo nomina ducum Esau in cognationibus, et locis, et vocabulis suis : dux Thamna, dux Alva, dux Jetheth,
     EN: These, then, are the names of Esau's chiefs according to their families, places, and names: Chief Timna, Chief Alvah, Chief Jetheth,

36:41  LA: dux Oolibama, dux Ela, dux Phinon,
     EN: Chief Oholibamah, Chief Elah, Chief Pinon,

36:42  LA: dux Cenez, dux Theman, dux Mabsar,
     EN: Chief Kenaz, Chief Teman, Chief Mibzar,

36:43  LA: dux Magdiel, dux Hiram : hi duces Edom habitantes in terra imperii sui, ipse est Esau pater Idumæorum.
     EN: Chief Magdiel, and Chief Iram. These are Edom's chiefs living in the land of their dominion. This is Esau, father of the Edomites.

GENESIS 37

37:1  LA: Habitavit autem Jacob in terra Chanaan, in qua pater suus peregrinatus est.
     EN: Jacob lived in the land of Canaan, where his father had sojourned.

37:2  LA: Et hæ sunt generationes ejus : Joseph cum sedecim esset annorum, pascebat gregem cum fratribus suis adhuc puer : et erat cum filiis Balæ et Zelphæ uxorum patris sui : accusavitque fratres suos apud patrem crimine pessimo.
     EN: These are his generations. Joseph, at sixteen years old, was still a boy tending the flock with his brothers, and was with the sons of Bilhah and Zilpah, his father's wives. He accused his brothers before his father of a very grave offense.

37:3  LA: Israël autem diligebat Joseph super omnes filios suos, eo quod in senectute genuisset eum : fecitque ei tunicam polymitam.
     EN: Israel loved Joseph more than all his sons, because he had fathered him in his old age, and made him a tunic woven in many colors.

37:4  LA: Videntes autem fratres ejus quod a patre plus cunctis filiis amaretur, oderant eum, nec poterant ei quidquam pacifice loqui.
     EN: His brothers saw that their father loved him more than all his sons, and hated him; they could not say anything peaceably to him.

37:5  LA: Accidit quoque ut visum somnium referret fratribus suis : quæ causa majoris odii seminarium fuit.
     EN: He also happened to recount a dream he had seen to his brothers, and this sowed the seeds of greater hatred.

37:6  LA: Dixitque ad eos : Audite somnium meum quod vidi :
     EN: He said to them, “Hear the dream I have seen.

37:7  LA: putabam nos ligare manipulos in agro : et quasi consurgere manipulum meum, et stare, vestrosque manipulos circumstantes adorare manipulum meum.
     EN: I thought we were binding sheaves in the field, and my sheaf seemed to rise and stand, while your sheaves stood around and bowed to my sheaf.”

37:8  LA: Responderunt fratres ejus : Numquid rex noster eris ? aut subjiciemur ditioni tuæ ? Hæc ergo causa somniorum atque sermonum, invidiæ et odii fomitem ministravit.
     EN: His brothers answered, “Will you be our king? Shall we be subjected to your rule?” Thus his dreams and words supplied fuel for envy and hatred.

37:9  LA: Aliud quoque vidit somnium, quod narrans fratribus, ait : Vidi per somnium, quasi solem, et lunam, et stellas undecim adorare me.
     EN: He saw another dream also, and recounted it to his brothers, saying, “In a dream I saw the sun, moon, and eleven stars bowing to me.”

37:10  LA: Quod cum patri suo, et fratribus retulisset, increpavit eum pater suus, et dixit : Quid sibi vult hoc somnium quod vidisti ? num ego et mater tua, et fratres tui adorabimus te super terram ?
     EN: When he told his father and brothers, his father rebuked him and said, “What does this dream you have seen mean? Shall I, your mother, and your brothers bow to you upon the ground?”

37:11  LA: Invidebant ei igitur fratres sui : pater vero rem tacitus considerabat.
     EN: His brothers envied him, but his father silently considered the matter.

37:12  LA: Cumque fratres illius in pascendis gregibus patris morarentur in Sichem,
     EN: While his brothers were staying in Shechem, tending their father's flocks,

37:13  LA: dixit ad eum Israël : Fratres tui pascunt oves in Sichimis : veni, mittam te ad eos. Quo respondente,
     EN: Israel said to him, “Your brothers are tending the sheep in Shechem. Come, I will send you to them.” When he answered,

37:14  LA: Præsto sum, ait ei : Vade, et vide si cuncta prospera sint erga fratres tuos, et pecora : et renuntia mihi quid agatur. Missus de valle Hebron, venit in Sichem :
     EN: “I am ready,” Israel said to him, “Go and see whether all is well with your brothers and the animals, and report to me how things are.” Sent from the valley of Hebron, he came to Shechem.

37:15  LA: invenitque eum vir errantem in agro, et interrogavit quid quæreret.
     EN: A man found him wandering in the field and asked what he was seeking.

37:16  LA: At ille respondit : Fratres meos quæro : indica mihi ubi pascant greges.
     EN: He answered, “I am seeking my brothers. Tell me where they are tending the flocks.”

37:17  LA: Dixitque ei vir : Recesserunt de loco isto : audivi autem eos dicentes : Eamus in Dothain. Perrexit ergo Joseph post fratres suos, et invenit eos in Dothain.
     EN: The man said to him, “They have left this place, for I heard them saying, ‘Let us go to Dothan.’” Joseph went after his brothers and found them in Dothan.

37:18  LA: Qui cum vidissent eum procul, antequam accederet ad eos, cogitaverunt illum occidere :
     EN: When they saw him in the distance, before he approached them, they plotted to kill him.

37:19  LA: et mutuo loquebantur : Ecce somniator venit :
     EN: They said to one another, “Here comes the dreamer.

37:20  LA: venite, occidamus eum, et mittamus in cisternam veterem : dicemusque : Fera pessima devoravit eum : et tunc apparebit quid illi prosint somnia sua.
     EN: Come, let us kill him and throw him into an old cistern. We will say, ‘A savage beast devoured him,’ and then it will be clear what use his dreams are to him.”

37:21  LA: Audiens autem hoc Ruben, nitebatur liberare eum de manibus eorum, et dicebat :
     EN: Reuben heard this and strove to deliver him from their hands, saying,

37:22  LA: Non interficiatis animam ejus, nec effundatis sanguinem : sed projicite eum in cisternam hanc, quæ est in solitudine, manusque vestras servate innoxias : hoc autem dicebat, volens eripere eum de manibus eorum, et reddere patri suo.
     EN: “Do not take his life or shed blood. Throw him into this cistern in the wilderness, but keep your hands innocent.” He said this because he wanted to rescue him from their hands and return him to his father.

37:23  LA: Confestim igitur ut pervenit ad fratres suos, nudaverunt eum tunica talari et polymita :
     EN: As soon as he reached his brothers, they stripped him of his long, many-colored tunic

37:24  LA: miseruntque eum in cisternam veterem, quæ non habebat aquam.
     EN: and threw him into an old cistern that held no water.

37:25  LA: Et sedentes ut comederent panem, viderunt Ismaëlitas viatores venire de Galaad, et camelos eorum portantes aromata, et resinam, et stacten in Ægyptum.
     EN: Sitting down to eat bread, they saw Ishmaelite travelers coming from Gilead, with their camels carrying spices, resin, and stacte to Egypt.

37:26  LA: Dixit ergo Judas fratribus suis : Quid nobis prodest si occiderimus fratrem nostrum, et celaverimus sanguinem ipsius ?
     EN: Judah said to his brothers, “What do we gain if we kill our brother and conceal his blood?

37:27  LA: melius est ut venundetur Ismaëlitis, et manus nostræ non polluantur : frater enim et caro nostra est. Acquieverunt fratres sermonibus illius.
     EN: It is better that he be sold to the Ishmaelites, so that our hands are not defiled, for he is our brother and our flesh.” His brothers agreed to his words.

37:28  LA: Et prætereuntibus Madianitis negotiatoribus, extrahentes eum de cisterna, vendiderunt eum Ismaëlitis, viginti argenteis : qui duxerunt eum in Ægyptum.
     EN: As Midianite merchants passed by, they drew him from the cistern and sold him to the Ishmaelites for twenty silver pieces, and these brought him to Egypt.

37:29  LA: Reversusque Ruben ad cisternam, non invenit puerum :
     EN: Reuben returned to the cistern and did not find the boy.

37:30  LA: et scissis vestibus pergens ad fratres suos, ait : Puer non comparet, et ego quo ibo ?
     EN: He tore his garments, went to his brothers, and said, “The boy is nowhere to be seen! And I—where shall I go?”

37:31  LA: Tulerunt autem tunicam ejus, et in sanguine hædi, quem occiderant, tinxerunt :
     EN: They took his tunic and dipped it in the blood of a young goat they had killed.

37:32  LA: mittentes qui ferrent ad patrem, et dicerent : Hanc invenimus : vide utrum tunica filii tui sit, an non.
     EN: They sent men to carry it to their father and say, “We found this. See whether it is your son's tunic or not.”

37:33  LA: Quam cum agnovisset pater, ait : Tunica filii mei est : fera pessima comedit eum, bestia devoravit Joseph.
     EN: His father recognized it and said, “It is my son's tunic. A savage beast has eaten him; a wild animal has devoured Joseph.”

37:34  LA: Scissisque vestibus, indutus est cilicio, lugens filium suum multo tempore.
     EN: He tore his garments, put on sackcloth, and mourned his son for a long time.

37:35  LA: Congregatis autem cunctis liberis ejus ut lenirent dolorem patris, noluit consolationem accipere, sed ait : Descendam ad filium meum lugens in infernum. Et illo perseverante in fletu,
     EN: All his children gathered to ease their father's grief, but he refused consolation, saying, “I shall go down mourning to my son in the realm of the dead.” While he continued weeping,

37:36  LA: Madianitæ vendiderunt Joseph in Ægypto Putiphari eunucho Pharaonis, magistro militum.
     EN: the Midianites sold Joseph in Egypt to Potiphar, Pharaoh's court official and commander of the soldiers.

GENESIS 38

38:1  LA: Eodem tempore, descendens Judas a fratribus suis, divertit ad virum Odollamitem, nomine Hiram.
     EN: At that time Judah went down from his brothers and turned aside to an Adullamite man named Hirah.

38:2  LA: Viditque ibi filiam hominis Chananæi, vocabulo Sue : et accepta uxore, ingressus est ad eam.
     EN: There he saw the daughter of a Canaanite man named Shua, took her as his wife, and went in to her.

38:3  LA: Quæ concepit, et peperit filium, et vocavit nomen ejus Her.
     EN: She conceived, bore a son, and named him Er.

38:4  LA: Rursumque concepto fœtu, natum filium vocavit Onan.
     EN: She conceived again and named the son born to her Onan.

38:5  LA: Tertium quoque peperit : quem appellavit Sela ; quo nato, parere ultra cessavit.
     EN: She also bore a third, whom she called Shelah. After his birth she stopped bearing children.

38:6  LA: Dedit autem Judas uxorem primogenito suo Her, nomine Thamar.
     EN: Judah gave his firstborn Er a wife named Tamar.

38:7  LA: Fuit quoque Her primogenitus Judæ nequam in conspectu Domini : et ab eo occisus est.
     EN: Er, Judah's firstborn, was wicked in the Lord's sight, and He killed him.

38:8  LA: Dixit ergo Judas ad Onan filium suum : Ingredere uxorem fratris tui, et sociare illi, ut suscites semen fratri tuo.
     EN: Judah said to his son Onan, “Go in to your brother's wife and be joined to her, so that you may raise up offspring for your brother.”

38:9  LA: Ille sciens non sibi nasci filios, introiens ad uxorem fratris sui, semen fundebat in terram, ne liberi fratris nomine nascerentur.
     EN: Knowing that the children would not be born for himself, whenever he went in to his brother's wife he spilled his seed upon the ground, so that children would not be born in his brother's name.

38:10  LA: Et idcirco percussit eum Dominus, quod rem detestabilem faceret.
     EN: Therefore the Lord struck him down, because he did a detestable thing.

38:11  LA: Quam ob rem dixit Judas Thamar nurui suæ : Esto vidua in domo patris tui, donec crescat Sela filius meus : timebat enim ne et ipse moreretur, sicut fratres ejus. Quæ abiit, et habitavit in domo patris sui.
     EN: For this reason Judah said to his daughter-in-law Tamar, “Remain a widow in your father's house until my son Shelah grows up,” for he feared that he too would die like his brothers. She went and lived in her father's house.

38:12  LA: Evolutis autem multis diebus, mortua est filia Sue uxor Judæ : qui, post luctum consolatione suscepta, ascendebat ad tonsores ovium suarum, ipse et Hiras opilio gregis Odollamites, in Thamnas.
     EN: After many days passed, Judah's wife, the daughter of Shua, died. When he had received comfort after mourning, he went up to the shearers of his sheep at Timnah with Hirah the Adullamite, shepherd of his flock.

38:13  LA: Nuntiatumque est Thamar quod socer illius ascenderet in Thamnas ad tondendas oves.
     EN: Tamar was told that her father-in-law was going up to Timnah to shear the sheep.

38:14  LA: Quæ, depositis viduitatis vestibus, assumpsit theristrum : et mutato habitu, sedit in bivio itineris, quod ducit Thamnam : eo quod crevisset Sela, et non eum accepisset maritum.
     EN: She took off her widow's garments and put on a veil. Changing her dress, she sat at a fork in the road leading to Timnah, because Shelah had grown up and she had not received him as a husband.

38:15  LA: Quam cum vidisset Judas, suspicatus est esse meretricem : operuerat enim vultum suum, ne agnosceretur.
     EN: When Judah saw her, he thought she was a prostitute, for she had covered her face so that she would not be recognized.

38:16  LA: Ingrediensque ad eam, ait : Dimitte me ut coëam tecum : nesciebat enim quod nurus sua esset. Qua respondente : Quid dabis mihi ut fruaris concubitu meo ?
     EN: He approached her and said, “Allow me to lie with you,” for he did not know she was his daughter-in-law. She answered, “What will you give me to enjoy lying with me?”

38:17  LA: dixit : Mittam tibi hædum de gregibus. Rursumque illa dicente : Patiar quod vis, si dederis mihi arrhabonem, donec mittas quod polliceris.
     EN: He said, “I will send you a young goat from the flocks.” She replied, “I will allow what you wish if you give me a pledge until you send what you promise.”

38:18  LA: Ait Judas : Quid tibi vis pro arrhabone dari ? Respondit : Annulum tuum, et armillam, et baculum quem manu tenes. Ad unum igitur coitum mulier concepit,
     EN: Judah said, “What do you wish to be given as a pledge?” She answered, “Your ring, your bracelet, and the staff you hold in your hand.” Thus by one act of intercourse the woman conceived.

38:19  LA: et surgens abiit : depositoque habitu quem sumpserat, induta est viduitatis vestibus.
     EN: She rose and departed, took off the dress she had put on, and clothed herself in her widow's garments.

38:20  LA: Misit autem Judas hædum per pastorem suum Odollamitem, ut reciperet pignus quod dederat mulieri : qui cum non invenisset eam,
     EN: Judah sent the young goat through his Adullamite shepherd to recover the pledge he had given the woman. When he did not find her,

38:21  LA: interrogavit homines loci illius : Ubi est mulier quæ sedebat in bivio ? Respondentibus cunctis : Non fuit in loco ista meretrix.
     EN: he asked the men of that place, “Where is the woman who sat at the fork in the road?” They all answered, “There has been no prostitute in this place.”

38:22  LA: Reversus est ad Judam, et dixit ei : Non inveni eam : sed et homines loci illius dixerunt mihi, numquam sedisse ibi scortum.
     EN: He returned to Judah and said, “I did not find her, and the men of that place told me that no prostitute had ever sat there.”

38:23  LA: Ait Judas : Habeat sibi, certe mendacii arguere nos non potest, ego misi hædum quem promiseram : et tu non invenisti eam.
     EN: Judah said, “Let her keep it. Certainly she cannot accuse us of lying: I sent the young goat I promised, and you did not find her.”

38:24  LA: Ecce autem post tres menses nuntiaverunt Judæ, dicentes : Fornicata est Thamar nurus tua, et videtur uterus illius intumescere. Dixitque Judas : Producite eam ut comburatur.
     EN: After three months they reported to Judah, saying, “Your daughter-in-law Tamar has committed fornication, and her womb appears to be swelling.” Judah said, “Bring her out to be burned.”

38:25  LA: Quæ cum duceretur ad pœnam, misit ad socerum suum, dicens : De viro, cujus hæc sunt, concepi : cognosce cujus sit annulus, et armilla, et baculus.
     EN: As she was being led to punishment, she sent word to her father-in-law, saying, “By the man to whom these belong I have conceived. Recognize whose ring, bracelet, and staff these are.”

38:26  LA: Qui, agnitis muneribus, ait : Justior me est : quia non tradidi eam Sela filio meo. Attamen ultra non cognovit eam.
     EN: He recognized the objects and said, “She is more righteous than I, because I did not give her to my son Shelah.” Yet he did not know her again.

38:27  LA: Instante autem partu, apparuerunt gemini in utero : atque in ipsa effusione infantium unus protulit manum, in qua obstetrix ligavit coccinum, dicens :
     EN: When the birth drew near, twins appeared in her womb. As the children were coming forth, one put out a hand, and the midwife tied a scarlet thread to it, saying,

38:28  LA: Iste egredietur prior.
     EN: “This one will come out first.”

38:29  LA: Illo vero retrahente manum, egressus est alter : dixitque mulier : Quare divisa est propter te maceria ? et ob hanc causam, vocavit nomen ejus Phares.
     EN: But he drew his hand back, and the other came out. The woman said, “Why has the barrier been broken through for you?” For this reason she named him Perez.

38:30  LA: Postea egressus est frater ejus, in cujus manu erat coccinum : quem appellavit Zara.
     EN: Afterward his brother came out with the scarlet thread on his hand, and she called him Zerah.

GENESIS 39

39:1  LA: Igitur Joseph ductus est in Ægyptum, emitque eum Putiphar eunuchus Pharaonis, princeps exercitus, vir ægyptius, de manu Ismaëlitarum, a quibus perductus erat.
     EN: Joseph was brought to Egypt. Potiphar, Pharaoh's court official and commander of the army, an Egyptian man, bought him from the Ishmaelites who had brought him there.

39:2  LA: Fuitque Dominus cum eo, et erat vir in cunctis prospere agens : habitavitque in domo domini sui,
     EN: The Lord was with him, and he prospered in everything. He lived in his master's house,

39:3  LA: qui optime noverat Dominum esse cum eo, et omnia, quæ gerebat, ab eo dirigi in manu illius.
     EN: and his master knew very well that the Lord was with him and directed everything he did in his hands.

39:4  LA: Invenitque Joseph gratiam coram domino suo, et ministrabat ei : a quo præpositus omnibus gubernabat creditam sibi domum, et universa quæ ei tradita fuerant :
     EN: Joseph found favor before his master and ministered to him. His master placed him over everything, and he governed the house entrusted to him and all that had been handed over to him.

39:5  LA: benedixitque Dominus domui Ægyptii propter Joseph, et multiplicavit tam in ædibus quam in agris cunctam ejus substantiam :
     EN: The Lord blessed the Egyptian's house because of Joseph and multiplied all his possessions, both in the house and in the fields.

39:6  LA: nec quidquam aliud noverat, nisi panem quo vescebatur. Erat autem Joseph pulchra facie, et decorus aspectu.
     EN: His master concerned himself with nothing except the bread he ate. Joseph was handsome in face and beautiful in appearance.

39:7  LA: Post multos itaque dies injecit domina sua oculos suos in Joseph, et ait : Dormi mecum.
     EN: After many days his mistress cast her eyes upon Joseph and said, “Sleep with me.”

39:8  LA: Qui nequaquam acquiescens operi nefario, dixit ad eam : Ecce dominus meus, omnibus mihi traditis, ignorat quid habeat in domo sua :
     EN: Refusing utterly to consent to the wicked deed, he said to her, “See, my master has entrusted everything to me and does not know what he has in his house.

39:9  LA: nec quidquam est quod non in mea sit potestate, vel non tradiderit mihi, præter te, quæ uxor ejus es : quomodo ergo possum hoc malum facere, et peccare in Deum meum ?
     EN: There is nothing outside my authority or withheld from me except you, who are his wife. How, then, can I do this evil and sin against my God?”

39:10  LA: Hujuscemodi verbis per singulos dies, et mulier molesta erat adolescenti : et ille recusabat stuprum.
     EN: With words like these the woman troubled the young man day after day, but he refused the sexual wrongdoing.

39:11  LA: Accidit autem quadam die ut intraret Joseph domum, et operis quippiam absque arbitris faceret :
     EN: One day Joseph entered the house to perform some work without anyone present.

39:12  LA: et illa, apprehensa lacinia vestimenti ejus, diceret : Dormi mecum. Qui relicto in manu ejus pallio fugit, et egressus est foras.
     EN: She grasped the edge of his garment and said, “Sleep with me.” Leaving his cloak in her hand, he fled and went outside.

39:13  LA: Cumque vidisset mulier vestem in manibus suis, et se esse contemptam,
     EN: When the woman saw the garment in her hands and that she had been rejected,

39:14  LA: vocavit ad se homines domus suæ, et ait ad eos : En introduxit virum hebræum, ut illuderet nobis : ingressus est ad me, ut coiret mecum : cumque ego succlamassem,
     EN: she called the men of her household and said to them, “See, he has brought in a Hebrew man to mock us. He came in to lie with me, and when I cried out

39:15  LA: et audisset vocem meam, reliquit pallium quod tenebam, et fugit foras.
     EN: and he heard my voice, he left the cloak I was holding and fled outside.”

39:16  LA: In argumentum ergo fidei retentum pallium ostendit marito revertenti domum,
     EN: She kept the cloak as proof of her story and showed it to her husband when he returned home,

39:17  LA: et ait : Ingressus est ad me servus hebræus quem adduxisti, ut illuderet mihi :
     EN: saying, “The Hebrew servant you brought came in to mock me.

39:18  LA: cumque audisset me clamare, reliquit pallium quod tenebam, et fugit foras.
     EN: When he heard me cry out, he left the cloak I was holding and fled outside.”

39:19  LA: His auditis dominus, et nimium credulus verbis conjugis, iratus est valde :
     EN: His master heard this and, too readily believing his wife's words, became very angry.

39:20  LA: tradiditque Joseph in carcerem, ubi vincti regis custodiebantur, et erat ibi clausus.
     EN: He handed Joseph over to the prison where the king's prisoners were kept, and he was confined there.

39:21  LA: Fuit autem Dominus cum Joseph, et misertus illius dedit ei gratiam in conspectu principis carceris.
     EN: But the Lord was with Joseph, had mercy on him, and gave him favor in the sight of the prison's governor.

39:22  LA: Qui tradidit in manu illius universos vinctos qui in custodia tenebantur : et quidquid fiebat, sub ipso erat.
     EN: He entrusted to him all the prisoners held in custody, and whatever was done was under his direction.

39:23  LA: Nec noverat aliquid, cunctis ei creditis : Dominus enim erat cum illo, et omnia opera ejus dirigebat.
     EN: He concerned himself with nothing, having entrusted everything to him, for the Lord was with Joseph and directed all his work.

GENESIS 40

40:1  LA: His ita gestis, accidit ut peccarent duo eunuchi, pincerna regis Ægypti, et pistor, domino suo.
     EN: After these things, two court officials, the king of Egypt's cupbearer and baker, offended their lord.

40:2  LA: Iratusque contra eos Pharao (nam alter pincernis præerat, alter pistoribus),
     EN: Pharaoh became angry with them, for one was chief of the cupbearers and the other chief of the bakers,

40:3  LA: misit eos in carcerem principis militum, in quo erat vinctus et Joseph.
     EN: and sent them to the prison of the commander of the soldiers, where Joseph also was confined.

40:4  LA: At custos carceris tradidit eos Joseph, qui et ministrabat eis : aliquantulum temporis fluxerat, et illi in custodia tenebantur.
     EN: The prison keeper entrusted them to Joseph, who ministered to them. Some time passed while they were held in custody.

40:5  LA: Videruntque ambo somnium nocte una, juxta interpretationem congruam sibi :
     EN: Both saw a dream on the same night, each with an interpretation fitting him.

40:6  LA: ad quos cum introisset Joseph mane, et vidisset eos tristes,
     EN: Joseph came in to them in the morning, saw that they were sad,

40:7  LA: sciscitatus est eos, dicens : Cur tristior est hodie solito facies vestra ?
     EN: and asked them, “Why are your faces sadder today than usual?”

40:8  LA: Qui responderunt : Somnium vidimus, et non est qui interpretetur nobis. Dixitque ad eos Joseph : Numquid non Dei est interpretatio ? referte mihi quid videritis.
     EN: They answered, “We have seen a dream, and there is no one to interpret it for us.” Joseph said to them, “Does not interpretation belong to God? Tell me what you saw.”

40:9  LA: Narravit prior, præpositus pincernarum, somnium suum : Videbam coram me vitem,
     EN: The chief cupbearer recounted his dream first: “I saw a vine before me,

40:10  LA: in qua erant tres propagines, crescere paulatim in gemmas, et post flores uvas maturescere :
     EN: with three branches, gradually growing buds; after the blossoms, the grapes ripened.

40:11  LA: calicemque Pharaonis in manu mea : tuli ergo uvas, et expressi in calicem quem tenebam, et tradidi poculum Pharaoni.
     EN: Pharaoh's cup was in my hand. I took the grapes, squeezed them into the cup I held, and handed the cup to Pharaoh.”

40:12  LA: Respondit Joseph : Hæc est interpretatio somnii : tres propagines, tres adhuc dies sunt :
     EN: Joseph answered, “This is the dream's interpretation: the three branches are three days yet to come,

40:13  LA: post quos recordabitur Pharao ministerii tui, et restituet te in gradum pristinum : dabisque ei calicem juxta officium tuum, sicut ante facere consueveras.
     EN: after which Pharaoh will remember your service and restore you to your former rank. You shall give him the cup according to your office, as you used to do before.

40:14  LA: Tantum memento mei, cum bene tibi fuerit, et facias mecum misericordiam : ut suggeras Pharaoni ut educat me de isto carcere :
     EN: Only remember me when it goes well with you, and show me kindness by urging Pharaoh to bring me out of this prison.

40:15  LA: quia furto sublatus sum de terra Hebræorum, et hic innocens in lacum missus sum.
     EN: For I was stolen from the land of the Hebrews, and here, though innocent, I have been thrown into the dungeon.”

40:16  LA: Videns pistorum magister quod prudenter somnium dissolvisset, ait : Et ego vidi somnium : quod tria canistra farinæ haberem super caput meum :
     EN: The chief baker saw that he had wisely explained the dream and said, “I also saw a dream: I had three baskets of flour upon my head.

40:17  LA: et in uno canistro quod erat excelsius, portare me omnes cibos qui fiunt arte pistoria, avesque comedere ex eo.
     EN: In the uppermost basket I was carrying every kind of food made by the baker's art, and birds ate from it.”

40:18  LA: Respondit Joseph : Hæc est interpretatio somnii : tria canistra, tres adhuc dies sunt :
     EN: Joseph answered, “This is the dream's interpretation: the three baskets are three days yet to come,

40:19  LA: post quos auferet Pharao caput tuum, ac suspendet te in cruce, et lacerabunt volucres carnes tuas.
     EN: after which Pharaoh will take away your head and hang you upon a cross, and birds will tear your flesh.”

40:20  LA: Exinde dies tertius natalitius Pharaonis erat : qui faciens grande convivium pueris suis, recordatus est inter epulas magistri pincernarum, et pistorum principis.
     EN: The third day afterward was Pharaoh's birthday. He made a great feast for his servants and, during the banquet, remembered the chief cupbearer and the chief baker.

40:21  LA: Restituitque alterum in locum suum, ut porrigeret ei poculum :
     EN: He restored the one to his place, to offer him the cup,

40:22  LA: alterum suspendit in patibulo, ut conjectoris veritas probaretur.
     EN: and hanged the other upon a gallows, so that the interpreter's truth was proved.

40:23  LA: Et tamen succedentibus prosperis, præpositus pincernarum oblitus est interpretis sui.
     EN: Yet as prosperity followed, the chief cupbearer forgot his interpreter.

GENESIS 41

41:1  LA: Post duos annos vidit Pharao somnium. Putabat se stare super fluvium,
     EN: After two years Pharaoh saw a dream. He thought he stood beside the river,

41:2  LA: de quo ascendebant septem boves, pulchræ et crassæ nimis : et pascebantur in locis palustribus.
     EN: from which seven cows came up, beautiful and very fat, and grazed in the marshy places.

41:3  LA: Aliæ quoque septem emergebant de flumine, fœdæ confectæque macie : et pascebantur in ipsa amnis ripa in locis virentibus :
     EN: Another seven also emerged from the river, ugly and wasted with leanness, and grazed in the green places on the riverbank itself.

41:4  LA: devoraveruntque eas, quarum mira species et habitudo corporum erat. Expergefactus Pharao,
     EN: They devoured those whose appearance and bodily condition were splendid. Pharaoh awoke,

41:5  LA: rursum dormivit, et vidit alterum somnium : septem spicæ pullulabant in culmo uno plenæ atque formosæ :
     EN: then slept again and saw another dream: seven ears of grain were growing upon one stalk, full and beautiful.

41:6  LA: aliæ quoque totidem spicæ tenues, et percussæ uredine oriebantur,
     EN: The same number of other ears came up, thin and struck by blight,

41:7  LA: devorantes omnium priorum pulchritudinem. Evigilans Pharao post quietem,
     EN: devouring all the beauty of the former ears. Pharaoh awoke after his rest,

41:8  LA: et facto mane, pavore perterritus, misit ad omnes conjectores Ægypti, cunctosque sapientes, et accersitis narravit somnium, nec erat qui interpretaretur.
     EN: and when morning came, he was terrified with dread. He sent for all Egypt's interpreters and all its wise men. When they were summoned, he told them the dream, but there was no one to interpret it.

41:9  LA: Tunc demum reminiscens pincernarum magister, ait : Confiteor peccatum meum :
     EN: Then at last the chief cupbearer remembered and said, “I confess my fault.

41:10  LA: iratus rex servis suis, me et magistrum pistorum retrudi jussit in carcerem principis militum :
     EN: The king was angry with his servants and ordered me and the chief baker confined in the prison of the commander of the soldiers.

41:11  LA: ubi una nocte uterque vidimus somnium præsagum futurorum.
     EN: There, on one night, we each saw a dream foretelling the future.

41:12  LA: Erat ibi puer hebræus, ejusdem ducis militum famulus : cui narrantes somnia,
     EN: There was a young Hebrew there, a servant of that same commander of the soldiers. We told him our dreams,

41:13  LA: audivimus quidquid postea rei probavit eventus ; ego enim redditus sum officio meo, et ille suspensus est in cruce.
     EN: and heard everything that the outcome afterward proved: I was restored to my office, and he was hung upon a cross.”

41:14  LA: Protinus ad regis imperium eductum de carcere Joseph totonderunt : ac veste mutata obtulerunt ei.
     EN: Immediately, at the king's command, Joseph was brought from prison. They cut his hair, changed his clothing, and presented him to Pharaoh.

41:15  LA: Cui ille ait : Vidi somnia, nec est qui edisserat : quæ audivi te sapientissime conjicere.
     EN: He said to him, “I have seen dreams, and there is no one to explain them, but I have heard that you interpret them most wisely.”

41:16  LA: Respondit Joseph : Absque me Deus respondebit prospera Pharaoni.
     EN: Joseph answered, “Without me, God will give Pharaoh a favorable answer.”

41:17  LA: Narravit ergo Pharao quod viderat : Putabam me stare super ripam fluminis,
     EN: Pharaoh then recounted what he had seen: “I thought I stood on the riverbank,

41:18  LA: et septem boves de amne conscendere, pulchras nimis, et obesis carnibus : quæ in pastu paludis virecta carpebant.
     EN: and seven cows came up from the river, very beautiful and fat in flesh, cropping the greenery as they grazed in the marsh.

41:19  LA: Et ecce, has sequebantur aliæ septem boves, in tantum deformes et macilentæ, ut numquam tales in terra Ægypti viderim :
     EN: Then seven other cows followed them, so deformed and thin that I have never seen their like in the land of Egypt.

41:20  LA: quæ, devoratis et consumptis prioribus,
     EN: They devoured and consumed the former cows,

41:21  LA: nullum saturitatis dedere vestigium : sed simili macie et squalore torpebant. Evigilans, rursus sopore depressus,
     EN: but showed no sign of being satisfied; they remained as wasted in leanness and ugliness. I awoke, then fell asleep again

41:22  LA: vidi somnium. Septem spicæ pullulabant in culmo uno plenæ atque pulcherrimæ.
     EN: and saw a dream: seven ears of grain were growing on one stalk, full and very beautiful.

41:23  LA: Aliæ quoque septem tenues et percussæ uredine, oriebantur e stipula :
     EN: Seven other ears also came up from the stalk, thin and struck by blight,

41:24  LA: quæ priorum pulchritudinem devoraverunt. Narravi conjectoribus somnium, et nemo est qui edisserat.
     EN: and devoured the beauty of the former ears. I told the dream to the interpreters, and there is no one to explain it.”

41:25  LA: Respondit Joseph : Somnium regis unum est : quæ facturus est Deus, ostendit Pharaoni.
     EN: Joseph answered, “The king's dream is one. God has shown Pharaoh what He is about to do.

41:26  LA: Septem boves pulchræ, et septem spicæ plenæ, septem ubertatis anni sunt : eamdemque vim somnii comprehendunt.
     EN: The seven beautiful cows and the seven full ears are seven years of plenty, and convey the same meaning of the dream.

41:27  LA: Septem quoque boves tenues atque macilentæ, quæ ascenderunt post eas, et septem spicæ tenues, et vento urente percussæ, septem anni venturæ sunt famis.
     EN: The seven thin, lean cows that came up after them, and the seven thin ears struck by the scorching wind, are seven years of coming famine.

41:28  LA: Qui hoc ordine complebuntur :
     EN: They shall be fulfilled in this order:

41:29  LA: ecce septem anni venient fertilitatis magnæ in universa terra Ægypti,
     EN: seven years of great fertility shall come throughout the whole land of Egypt,

41:30  LA: quos sequentur septem anni alii tantæ sterilitatis, ut oblivioni tradatur cuncta retro abundantia : consumptura est enim fames omnem terram,
     EN: followed by seven other years of such barrenness that all the former abundance will be forgotten. Famine shall consume the whole land,

41:31  LA: et ubertatis magnitudinem perditura est inopiæ magnitudo.
     EN: and the greatness of scarcity shall destroy the greatness of plenty.

41:32  LA: Quod autem vidisti secundo ad eamdem rem pertinens somnium : firmitatis indicium est, eo quod fiat sermo Dei, et velocius impleatur.
     EN: That you saw the dream a second time concerning the same matter is a sign of certainty, because God's word shall come to pass and be fulfilled swiftly.

41:33  LA: Nunc ergo provideat rex virum sapientem et industrium, et præficiat eum terræ Ægypti :
     EN: Now therefore let the king provide a wise and capable man and set him over the land of Egypt.

41:34  LA: qui constituat præpositos per cunctas regiones : et quintam partem fructuum per septem annos fertilitatis,
     EN: Let him appoint overseers throughout all the regions, and a fifth part of the produce during the seven years of fertility

41:35  LA: qui jam nunc futuri sunt, congreget in horrea : et omne frumentum sub Pharaonis potestate condatur, serveturque in urbibus.
     EN: that are now about to come, let him gather into storehouses. Let all the grain be stored under Pharaoh's authority and kept in the cities,

41:36  LA: Et præparetur futuræ septem annorum fami, quæ oppressura est Ægyptum, et non consumetur terra inopia.
     EN: prepared for the coming seven-year famine that will oppress Egypt, so that the land is not consumed by scarcity.”

41:37  LA: Placuit Pharaoni consilium et cunctis ministris ejus :
     EN: The counsel pleased Pharaoh and all his ministers.

41:38  LA: locutusque est ad eos : Num invenire poterimus talem virum, qui spiritu Dei plenus sit ?
     EN: He said to them, “Can we find a man like this, full of the Spirit of God?”

41:39  LA: Dixit ergo ad Joseph : Quia ostendit tibi Deus omnia quæ locutus es, numquid sapientiorem et consimilem tui invenire potero ?
     EN: He said to Joseph, “Since God has shown you everything you have spoken, can I find anyone wiser or like you?

41:40  LA: Tu eris super domum meam, et ad tui oris imperium cunctus populus obediet : uno tantum regni solio te præcedam.
     EN: You shall be over my house, and all the people shall obey the command of your mouth. Only in the royal throne shall I rank above you.”

41:41  LA: Dixitque rursus Pharao ad Joseph : Ecce, constitui te super universam terram Ægypti.
     EN: Pharaoh said again to Joseph, “See, I have appointed you over the whole land of Egypt.”

41:42  LA: Tulitque annulum de manu sua, et dedit eum in manu ejus : vestivitque eum stola byssina, et collo torquem auream circumposuit.
     EN: He took the ring from his own hand and placed it on Joseph's hand, clothed him in a fine-linen robe, and placed a gold chain around his neck.

41:43  LA: Fecitque eum ascendere super currum suum secundum, clamante præcone, ut omnes coram eo genu flecterent, et præpositum esse scirent universæ terræ Ægypti.
     EN: He had him ride in his second chariot, with a herald proclaiming that all should bend the knee before him and know that he had been appointed over the whole land of Egypt.

41:44  LA: Dixit quoque rex ad Joseph : Ego sum Pharao : absque tuo imperio non movebit quisquam manum aut pedem in omni terra Ægypti.
     EN: The king also said to Joseph, “I am Pharaoh. Without your command no one shall move hand or foot in the whole land of Egypt.”

41:45  LA: Vertitque nomen ejus, et vocavit eum, lingua ægyptiaca, Salvatorem mundi. Deditque illi uxorem Aseneth filiam Putiphare sacerdotis Heliopoleos. Egressus est itaque Joseph ad terram Ægypti
     EN: He changed his name and called him, in the Egyptian language, Savior of the World. He gave him as wife Asenath, daughter of Potiphera, priest of Heliopolis. Joseph went out into the land of Egypt—

41:46  LA: (triginta autem annorum erat quando stetit in conspectu regis Pharaonis), et circuivit omnes regiones Ægypti.
     EN: he was thirty years old when he stood before King Pharaoh—and traveled through all the regions of Egypt.

41:47  LA: Venitque fertilitas septem annorum : et in manipulos redactæ segetes congregatæ sunt in horrea Ægypti.
     EN: The seven years of fertility came, and the crops, gathered into sheaves, were collected in Egypt's storehouses.

41:48  LA: Omnis etiam frugum abundantia in singulis urbibus condita est.
     EN: All the abundance of produce was stored in each city.

41:49  LA: Tantaque fuit abundantia tritici, ut arenæ maris coæquaretur, et copia mensuram excederet.
     EN: The abundance of wheat was so great that it equaled the sand of the sea, and its quantity exceeded measure.

41:50  LA: Nati sunt autem Joseph filii duo antequam veniret fames : quos peperit ei Aseneth filia Putiphare sacerdotis Heliopoleos.
     EN: Before the famine came, two sons were born to Joseph, whom Asenath, daughter of Potiphera, priest of Heliopolis, bore him.

41:51  LA: Vocavitque nomen primogeniti Manasses, dicens : Oblivisci me fecit Deus omnium laborum meorum, et domus patris mei.
     EN: He named the firstborn Manasseh, saying, “God has made me forget all my labors and my father's house.”

41:52  LA: Nomen quoque secundi appellavit Ephraim, dicens : Crescere me fecit Deus in terra paupertatis meæ.
     EN: He named the second Ephraim, saying, “God has made me increase in the land of my poverty.”

41:53  LA: Igitur transactis septem ubertatis annis, qui fuerant in Ægypto,
     EN: When the seven years of abundance in Egypt had passed,

41:54  LA: cœperunt venire septem anni inopiæ, quos prædixerat Joseph : et in universo orbe fames prævaluit, in cuncta autem terra Ægypti panis erat.
     EN: the seven years of scarcity that Joseph had foretold began to come. Famine prevailed throughout the whole world, but throughout the land of Egypt there was bread.

41:55  LA: Qua esuriente, clamavit populus ad Pharaonem, alimenta petens. Quibus ille respondit : Ite ad Joseph : et quidquid ipse vobis dixerit, facite.
     EN: When Egypt grew hungry, the people cried to Pharaoh, asking for food. He answered them, “Go to Joseph, and do whatever he tells you.”

41:56  LA: Crescebat autem quotidie fames in omni terra : aperuitque Joseph universa horrea, et vendebat Ægyptiis : nam et illos oppresserat fames.
     EN: The famine grew daily throughout the whole land. Joseph opened all the storehouses and sold to the Egyptians, for famine oppressed them also.

41:57  LA: Omnesque provinciæ veniebant in Ægyptum, ut emerent escas, et malum inopiæ temperarent.
     EN: All the provinces came to Egypt to buy food and relieve the suffering of scarcity.

GENESIS 42

42:1  LA: Audiens autem Jacob quod alimenta venderentur in Ægypto, dixit filiis suis : Quare negligitis ?
     EN: Jacob heard that food was being sold in Egypt and said to his sons, “Why are you doing nothing?

42:2  LA: audivi quod triticum venundetur in Ægypto : descendite, et emite nobis necessaria, ut possimus vivere, et non consumamur inopia.
     EN: I have heard that wheat is being sold in Egypt. Go down and buy what we need, so that we may live and not be consumed by scarcity.”

42:3  LA: Descendentes igitur fratres Joseph decem, ut emerent frumenta in Ægypto,
     EN: Joseph's ten brothers went down to buy grain in Egypt,

42:4  LA: Benjamin domi retento a Jacob, qui dixerat fratribus ejus : Ne forte in itinere quidquam patiatur mali :
     EN: while Jacob kept Benjamin at home, saying to his brothers, “Lest some harm happen to him on the journey.”

42:5  LA: ingressi sunt terram Ægypti cum aliis qui pergebant ad emendum. Erat autem fames in terra Chanaan.
     EN: They entered the land of Egypt with others who were going to buy, for there was famine in the land of Canaan.

42:6  LA: Et Joseph erat princeps in terra Ægypti, atque ad ejus nutum frumenta populis vendebantur. Cumque adorassent eum fratres sui,
     EN: Joseph was ruler in the land of Egypt, and at his direction grain was sold to the peoples. When his brothers bowed to him,

42:7  LA: et agnovisset eos, quasi ad alienos durius loquebatur, interrogans eos : Unde venistis ? Qui responderunt : De terra Chanaan, ut emamus victui necessaria.
     EN: he recognized them but spoke harshly as though to strangers, asking them, “Where have you come from?” They answered, “From the land of Canaan, to buy the necessities of life.”

42:8  LA: Et tamen fratres ipse cognoscens, non est cognitus ab eis.
     EN: Though he recognized his brothers, they did not recognize him.

42:9  LA: Recordatusque somniorum, quæ aliquando viderat, ait ad eos : Exploratores estis : ut videatis infirmiora terræ venistis.
     EN: Remembering the dreams he had once seen, he said to them, “You are spies. You have come to see the country's weak places.”

42:10  LA: Qui dixerunt : Non est ita, domine, sed servi tui venerunt ut emerent cibos.
     EN: They said, “Not so, my lord; your servants have come to buy food.

42:11  LA: Omnes filii unius viri sumus : pacifici venimus, nec quidquam famuli tui machinantur mali.
     EN: We are all sons of one man. We have come peaceably, and your servants are plotting no evil.”

42:12  LA: Quibus ille respondit : Aliter est : immunita terræ hujus considerare venistis.
     EN: He answered them, “It is otherwise. You have come to examine the unprotected parts of this land.”

42:13  LA: At illi : Duodecim, inquiunt, servi tui, fratres sumus, filii viri unius in terra Chanaan : minimus cum patre nostro est, alius non est super.
     EN: They said, “We, your servants, are twelve brothers, sons of one man in the land of Canaan. The youngest is with our father; another is no longer alive.”

42:14  LA: Hoc est, ait, quod locutus sum : exploratores estis.
     EN: He said, “It is as I have spoken: you are spies.

42:15  LA: Jam nunc experimentum vestri capiam : per salutem Pharaonis non egrediemini hinc, donec veniat frater vester minimus.
     EN: Now I shall put you to the test. By Pharaoh's life, you shall not leave here until your youngest brother comes.

42:16  LA: Mittite ex vobis unum, et adducat eum : vos autem eritis in vinculis, donec probentur quæ dixistis utrum vera an falsa sint : alioquin per salutem Pharaonis exploratores estis.
     EN: Send one of you to bring him, while you remain bound until what you have said is proved true or false. Otherwise, by Pharaoh's life, you are spies.”

42:17  LA: Tradidit ergo illos custodiæ tribus diebus.
     EN: He committed them to custody for three days.

42:18  LA: Die autem tertio eductis de carcere, ait : Facite quæ dixi, et vivetis : Deum enim timeo.
     EN: On the third day he brought them out of prison and said, “Do what I have said and you shall live, for I fear God.

42:19  LA: Si pacifici estis, frater vester unus ligetur in carcere : vos autem abite, et ferte frumenta quæ emistis, in domos vestras,
     EN: If you are peaceful men, let one of your brothers be bound in prison, while you go and bring the grain you have bought to your homes.

42:20  LA: et fratrem vestrum minimum ad me adducite, ut possim vestros probare sermones, et non moriamini. Fecerunt ut dixerat,
     EN: Bring your youngest brother to me, so that I can prove your words and you may not die.” They did as he said,

42:21  LA: et locuti sunt ad invicem : Merito hæc patimur, quia peccavimus in fratrem nostrum, videntes angustiam animæ illius, dum deprecaretur nos, et non audivimus : idcirco venit super nos ista tribulatio.
     EN: and spoke to one another: “We suffer this deservedly, because we sinned against our brother. We saw the anguish of his soul while he pleaded with us, but did not listen. Therefore this distress has come upon us.”

42:22  LA: E quibus unus Ruben, ait : Numquid non dixi vobis : Nolite peccare in puerum : et non audistis me ? en sanguis ejus exquiritur.
     EN: One of them, Reuben, said, “Did I not tell you, ‘Do not sin against the boy,’ and you would not listen to me? Now his blood is being required.”

42:23  LA: Nesciebant autem quod intelligeret Joseph, eo quod per interpretem loqueretur ad eos.
     EN: They did not know Joseph understood, because he spoke to them through an interpreter.

42:24  LA: Avertitque se parumper, et flevit : et reversus locutus est ad eos.
     EN: He turned away briefly and wept, then returned and spoke to them.

42:25  LA: Tollensque Simeon, et ligans illis præsentibus, jussit ministris ut implerent eorum saccos tritico, et reponerent pecunias singulorum in sacculis suis, datis supra cibariis in viam : qui fecerunt ita.
     EN: He took Simeon and bound him in their presence, and commanded his attendants to fill their sacks with wheat, return each man's money to his sack, and give them provisions for the journey besides. They did so.

42:26  LA: At illi portantes frumenta in asinis suis, profecti sunt.
     EN: They loaded the grain on their donkeys and departed.

42:27  LA: Apertoque unus sacco, ut daret jumento pabulum in diversorio, contemplatus pecuniam in ore sacculi,
     EN: At a lodging place one opened his sack to give his animal fodder and saw the money at the mouth of his sack.

42:28  LA: dixit fratribus suis : Reddita est mihi pecunia, en habetur in sacco. Et obstupefacti, turbatique, mutuo dixerunt : Quidnam est hoc quod fecit nobis Deus ?
     EN: He said to his brothers, “My money has been returned to me; here it is in the sack.” Astonished and troubled, they said to one another, “What is this that God has done to us?”

42:29  LA: Veneruntque ad Jacob patrem suum in terram Chanaan, et narraverunt ei omnia quæ accidissent sibi, dicentes :
     EN: They came to their father Jacob in the land of Canaan and told him everything that had happened to them, saying,

42:30  LA: Locutus est nobis dominus terræ dure, et putavit nos exploratores esse provinciæ.
     EN: “The lord of the land spoke harshly to us and thought we were spies in the country.

42:31  LA: Cui respondimus : Pacifici sumus, nec ullas molimur insidias.
     EN: We answered him, ‘We are peaceful men and are plotting no schemes.

42:32  LA: Duodecim fratres uno patre geniti sumus : unus non est super, minimus cum patre nostro est in terra Chanaan.
     EN: We are twelve brothers born of one father. One is no longer alive; the youngest is with our father in the land of Canaan.’

42:33  LA: Qui ait nobis : Sic probabo quod pacifici sitis : fratrem vestrum unum dimittite apud me, et cibaria domibus vestris necessaria sumite, et abite,
     EN: He said to us, ‘This is how I shall prove that you are peaceful men: leave one of your brothers with me, take the food your households need, and go.

42:34  LA: fratremque vestrum minimum adducite ad me, ut sciam quod non sitis exploratores : et istum, qui tenetur in vinculis, recipere possitis : ac deinceps quæ vultis, emendi habeatis licentiam.
     EN: Bring your youngest brother to me, so that I know you are not spies, and you may recover the one held in bonds and afterward have permission to buy whatever you wish.’”

42:35  LA: His dictis, cum frumenta effunderent, singuli repererunt in ore saccorum ligatas pecunias, exterritisque simul omnibus,
     EN: After saying this, as they emptied the grain, each found his money tied up at the mouth of his sack. When all were terrified together,

42:36  LA: dixit pater Jacob : Absque liberis me esse fecistis : Joseph non est super, Simeon tenetur in vinculis, et Benjamin auferetis : in me hæc omnia mala reciderunt.
     EN: their father Jacob said, “You have made me childless. Joseph is no longer alive, Simeon is held in bonds, and you would take Benjamin away. All these evils have fallen upon me.”

42:37  LA: Cui respondit Ruben : Duos filios meos interfice, si non reduxero illum tibi : trade illum in manu mea, et ego eum tibi restituam.
     EN: Reuben answered him, “Kill my two sons if I do not bring him back to you. Entrust him to my hand, and I will restore him to you.”

42:38  LA: At ille : Non descendet, inquit, filius meus vobiscum : frater ejus mortuus est, et ipse solus remansit : si quid ei adversi acciderit in terra ad quam pergitis, deducetis canos meos cum dolore ad inferos.
     EN: He said, “My son shall not go down with you. His brother is dead, and he alone remains. If any misfortune happens to him in the land to which you are going, you will bring my gray hairs down in sorrow to the realm of the dead.”

GENESIS 43

43:1  LA: Interim fames omnem terram vehementer premebat.
     EN: Meanwhile famine pressed heavily upon the whole land.

43:2  LA: Consumptisque cibis quos ex Ægypto detulerant, dixit Jacob ad filios suos : Revertimini, et emite nobis pauxillum escarum.
     EN: When they had consumed the food they brought from Egypt, Jacob said to his sons, “Return and buy us a little food.”

43:3  LA: Respondit Judas : Denuntiavit nobis vir ille sub attestatione jurisjurandi, dicens : Non videbitis faciem meam, nisi fratrem vestrum minimum adduxeritis vobiscum.
     EN: Judah answered, “That man solemnly warned us under oath, saying, ‘You shall not see my face unless you bring your youngest brother with you.’

43:4  LA: Si ergo vis eum mittere nobiscum, pergemus pariter, et ememus tibi necessaria :
     EN: If you are willing to send him with us, we will travel together and buy what you need.

43:5  LA: sin autem non vis, non ibimus : vir enim, ut sæpe diximus, denuntiavit nobis, dicens : Non videbitis faciem meam absque fratre vestro minimo.
     EN: But if you are unwilling, we will not go. For, as we have often said, the man warned us, ‘You shall not see my face without your youngest brother.’”

43:6  LA: Dixit eis Israël : In meam hoc fecistis miseriam, ut indicaretis ei et alium habere vos fratrem.
     EN: Israel said to them, “To my misery you have done this, telling him that you have another brother.”

43:7  LA: At illi responderunt : Interrogavit nos homo per ordinem nostram progeniem : si pater viveret : si haberemus fratrem : et nos respondimus ei consequenter juxta id quod fuerat sciscitatus : numquid scire poteramus quod dicturus esset : Adducite fratrem vestrum vobiscum ?
     EN: They answered, “The man asked us in detail about our family: whether our father was alive and whether we had a brother. We answered him accordingly, following his questions. Could we have known that he would say, ‘Bring your brother with you’?”

43:8  LA: Judas quoque dixit patri suo : Mitte puerum mecum, ut proficiscamur, et possimus vivere : ne moriamur nos et parvuli nostri.
     EN: Judah also said to his father, “Send the boy with me, so that we may go and live, and we and our little ones may not die.

43:9  LA: Ego suscipio puerum : de manu mea require illum : nisi reduxero, et reddidero eum tibi, ero peccati reus in te omni tempore.
     EN: I take responsibility for the boy; require him from my hand. Unless I bring him back and restore him to you, I shall bear guilt toward you for all time.

43:10  LA: Si non intercessisset dilatio, jam vice alter venissemus.
     EN: If there had been no delay, we could have returned a second time by now.”

43:11  LA: Igitur Israël pater eorum dixit ad eos : Si sic necesse est, facite quod vultis : sumite de optimis terræ fructibus in vasis vestris, et deferte viro munera, modicum resinæ, et mellis, et storacis, stactes, et terebinthi, et amygdalarum.
     EN: Their father Israel said to them, “If it must be so, do as you wish. Take some of the land's finest produce in your containers and bring the man gifts: a little resin, honey, storax, stacte, terebinth nuts, and almonds.

43:12  LA: Pecuniam quoque duplicem ferte vobiscum : et illam, quam invenistis in sacculis, reportate, ne forte errore factum sit :
     EN: Take twice the money with you, and bring back what you found in the sacks, lest it happened by mistake.

43:13  LA: sed et fratrem vestrum tollite, et ite ad virum.
     EN: Take your brother also and go to the man.

43:14  LA: Deus autem meus omnipotens faciat vobis eum placabilem : et remittat vobiscum fratrem vestrum quem tenet, et hunc Benjamin : ego autem quasi orbatus absque liberis ero.
     EN: May my God Almighty make him favorable to you, so that he sends back with you the brother he holds, and this Benjamin also. As for me, I shall be as one bereaved and childless.”

43:15  LA: Tulerunt ergo viri munera, et pecuniam duplicem, et Benjamin : descenderuntque in Ægyptum, et steterunt coram Joseph.
     EN: The men took the gifts, twice the money, and Benjamin, went down to Egypt, and stood before Joseph.

43:16  LA: Quos cum ille vidisset et Benjamin simul, præcepit dispensatori domus suæ, dicens : Introduc viros domum, et occide victimas, et instrue convivium : quoniam mecum sunt comesturi meridie.
     EN: When he saw them and Benjamin together, he commanded the steward of his house, saying, “Bring the men into the house, slaughter animals, and prepare a feast, for they shall eat with me at noon.”

43:17  LA: Fecit ille quod sibi fuerat imperatum, et introduxit viros domum.
     EN: He did as he had been commanded and brought the men into the house.

43:18  LA: Ibique exterriti, dixerunt mutuo : Propter pecuniam, quam retulimus prius in saccis nostris, introducti sumus : ut devolvat in nos calumniam, et violenter subjiciat servituti et nos, et asinos nostros.
     EN: They were terrified there and said to one another, “We have been brought in because of the money we carried back in our sacks before, so that he may bring an accusation against us and forcibly subject us and our donkeys to slavery.”

43:19  LA: Quam ob rem in ipsis foribus accedentes ad dispensatorem domus,
     EN: Therefore at the doorway itself they approached the steward of the house

43:20  LA: locuti sunt : Oramus, domine, ut audias nos. Jam ante descendimus ut emeremus escas :
     EN: and said, “Please, my lord, hear us. We came down before to buy food.

43:21  LA: quibus emptis, cum venissemus ad diversorium, aperuimus saccos nostros, et invenimus pecuniam in ore saccorum : quam nunc eodem pondere reportavimus.
     EN: After buying it, when we came to a lodging place, we opened our sacks and found the money at the mouths of the sacks. We have now brought it back in the same weight.

43:22  LA: Sed et aliud attulimus argentum, ut emamus quæ nobis necessaria sunt : non est in nostra conscientia quis posuerit eam in marsupiis nostris.
     EN: We have brought other silver also to buy what we need. We do not know who put that money into our bags.”

43:23  LA: At ille respondit : Pax vobiscum, nolite timere : Deus vester, et Deus patris vestri, dedit vobis thesauros in saccis vestris : nam pecuniam, quam dedistis mihi, probatam ego habeo. Eduxitque ad eos Simeon.
     EN: He answered, “Peace to you; do not fear. Your God and the God of your father has given you treasure in your sacks, for I have the verified money you gave me.” He brought Simeon out to them.

43:24  LA: Et introductis domum, attulit aquam, et laverunt pedes suos, deditque pabulum asinis eorum.
     EN: He brought them into the house, supplied water, and they washed their feet; he also gave fodder to their donkeys.

43:25  LA: Illi vero parabant munera, donec ingrederetur Joseph meridie : audierant enim quod ibi comesturi essent panem.
     EN: They prepared the gifts until Joseph came in at noon, for they had heard they would eat bread there.

43:26  LA: Igitur ingressus est Joseph domum suam, obtuleruntque ei munera, tenentes in manibus suis : et adoraverunt proni in terram.
     EN: Joseph entered his house, and they offered him the gifts they held in their hands and bowed facedown to the ground.

43:27  LA: At ille, clementer resalutatis eis, interrogavit eos, dicens : Salvusne est pater vester senex, de quo dixeratis mihi ? adhuc vivit ?
     EN: He greeted them kindly in return and asked, “Is your aged father, of whom you told me, well? Is he still alive?”

43:28  LA: Qui responderunt : Sospes est servus tuus pater noster, adhuc vivit. Et incurvati, adoraverunt eum.
     EN: They answered, “Your servant our father is well; he is still alive.” They bent down and bowed to him.

43:29  LA: Attollens autem Joseph oculos, vidit Benjamin fratrem suum uterinum, et ait : Iste est frater vester parvulus, de quo dixeratis mihi ? Et rursum : Deus, inquit, misereatur tui, fili mi.
     EN: Joseph raised his eyes and saw Benjamin, his brother by the same mother, and said, “Is this your little brother of whom you told me?” Then he said, “May God have mercy on you, my son.”
