43:30  LA: Festinavitque, quia commota fuerant viscera ejus super fratre suo, et erumpebant lacrimæ : et introiens cubiculum flevit.
     EN: He hurried away, for his heart was deeply moved toward his brother and tears were breaking forth. He entered his chamber and wept.

43:31  LA: Rursumque lota facie egressus, continuit se, et ait : Ponite panes.
     EN: Then he washed his face, came out, restrained himself, and said, “Set out the bread.”

43:32  LA: Quibus appositis, seorsum Joseph, et seorsum fratribus, Ægyptiis quoque qui vescebantur simul, seorsum (illicitum est enim Ægyptiis comedere cum Hebræis, et profanum putant hujuscemodi convivium)
     EN: It was set out separately for Joseph, separately for his brothers, and separately for the Egyptians eating with him, for Egyptians are not permitted to eat with Hebrews and regard such a meal as profane.

43:33  LA: sederunt coram eo, primogenitus juxta primogenita sua, et minimus juxta ætatem suam. Et mirabantur nimis,
     EN: They sat before him, the firstborn according to his birthright and the youngest according to his age, and marveled greatly,

43:34  LA: sumptis partibus quas ab eo acceperant : majorque pars venit Benjamin, ita ut quinque partibus excederet. Biberuntque et inebriati sunt cum eo.
     EN: receiving the portions given them by him. Benjamin received the greater portion, exceeding theirs fivefold. They drank and became drunk with him.

GENESIS 44

44:1  LA: Præcepit autem Joseph dispensatori domus suæ, dicens : Imple saccos eorum frumento, quantum possunt capere : et pone pecuniam singulorum in summitate sacci.
     EN: Joseph commanded the steward of his house, saying, “Fill their sacks with as much grain as they can hold, and put each man's money at the top of his sack.

44:2  LA: Scyphum autem meum argenteum, et pretium quod dedit tritici, pone in ore sacci junioris. Factumque est ita.
     EN: Put my silver cup and the price he paid for the wheat at the mouth of the youngest one's sack.” And so it was done.

44:3  LA: Et orto mane, dimissi sunt cum asinis suis.
     EN: When morning dawned, they were sent away with their donkeys.

44:4  LA: Jamque urbem exierant, et processerant paululum : tunc Joseph accersito dispensatore domus, Surge, inquit, et persequere viros : et apprehensis dicito : Quare reddidistis malum pro bono ?
     EN: They had left the city and gone a little way when Joseph summoned his steward and said, “Rise and pursue the men. When you catch them, say, ‘Why have you repaid good with evil?

44:5  LA: scyphus, quem furati estis, ipse est in quo bibit dominus meus, et in quo augurari solet : pessimam rem fecistis.
     EN: The cup you stole is the one from which my master drinks and by which he is accustomed to practice divination. You have done a very evil thing.’”

44:6  LA: Fecit ille ut jusserat : et apprehensis per ordinem locutus est.
     EN: He did as commanded, caught up with them, and spoke these words in order.

44:7  LA: Qui responderunt : Quare sic loquitur dominus noster, ut servi tui tantum flagitii commiserint ?
     EN: They answered, “Why does our lord speak as though your servants had committed such a disgraceful deed?

44:8  LA: pecuniam, quam invenimus in summitate saccorum, reportavimus ad te de terra Chanaan : et quomodo consequens est ut furati simus de domo domini tui aurum vel argentum ?
     EN: We brought back to you from the land of Canaan the money we found at the tops of our sacks. How, then, could we have stolen gold or silver from your master's house?

44:9  LA: apud quemcumque fuerit inventum servorum tuorum quod quæris, moriatur, et nos erimus servi domini nostri.
     EN: Whichever of your servants is found with what you seek shall die, and we shall be our lord's slaves.”

44:10  LA: Qui dixit eis : Fiat juxta vestram sententiam : apud quemcumque fuerit inventum, ipse sit servus meus, vos autem eritis innoxii.
     EN: He said to them, “Let it be according to your judgment: whoever is found with it shall be my slave, but you shall be innocent.”

44:11  LA: Itaque festinato deponentes in terram saccos, aperuerunt singuli.
     EN: They quickly lowered their sacks to the ground, and each opened his own.

44:12  LA: Quos scrutatus, incipiens a majore usque ad minimum, invenit scyphum in sacco Benjamin.
     EN: He searched them, beginning with the eldest and ending with the youngest, and found the cup in Benjamin's sack.

44:13  LA: At illi, scissis vestibus, oneratisque rursum asinis, reversi sunt in oppidum.
     EN: They tore their garments, loaded their donkeys again, and returned to the town.

44:14  LA: Primusque Judas cum fratribus ingressus est ad Joseph (necdum enim de loco abierat) omnesque ante eum pariter in terram corruerunt.
     EN: Judah went in first to Joseph with his brothers, for Joseph had not yet left the place, and they all fell together to the ground before him.

44:15  LA: Quibus ille ait : Cur sic agere voluistis ? an ignoratis quod non sit similis mei in augurandi scientia ?
     EN: He said to them, “Why have you chosen to act this way? Do you not know that no one equals me in the knowledge of divination?”

44:16  LA: Cui Judas : Quid respondebimus, inquit, domino meo ? vel quid loquemur, aut juste poterimus obtendere ? Deus invenit iniquitatem servorum tuorum : en omnes servi sumus domini mei, et nos, et apud quem inventus est scyphus.
     EN: Judah said to him, “What shall we answer my lord? What shall we say, or what just defense can we offer? God has uncovered your servants' iniquity. See, we are all my lord's slaves, both we and the one with whom the cup was found.”

44:17  LA: Respondit Joseph : Absit a me ut sic agam : qui furatus est scyphum, ipse sit servus meus : vos autem abite liberi ad patrem vestrum.
     EN: Joseph answered, “Far be it from me to act this way. The one who stole the cup shall be my slave, but you may go freely to your father.”

44:18  LA: Accedens autem propius Judas, confidenter ait : Oro, domine mi, loquatur servus tuus verbum in auribus tuis, et ne irascaris famulo tuo : tu es enim post Pharaonem
     EN: Judah came nearer and spoke boldly: “Please, my lord, let your servant speak a word in your hearing, and do not be angry with your servant, for next to Pharaoh you are

44:19  LA: dominus meus. Interrogasti prius servos tuos : Habetis patrem aut fratrem ?
     EN: my lord. You first asked your servants, ‘Have you a father or a brother?’

44:20  LA: et nos respondimus tibi domino meo : Est nobis pater senex, et puer parvulus, qui in senectute illius natus est : cujus uterinus frater mortuus est : et ipsum solum habet mater sua, pater vero tenere diligit eum.
     EN: We answered you, my lord, ‘We have an aged father and a young boy born in his old age, whose brother by the same mother is dead. His mother has him alone left, and his father loves him tenderly.’

44:21  LA: Dixistique servis tuis : Adducite eum ad me, et ponam oculos meos super illum.
     EN: You said to your servants, ‘Bring him to me, and I will set my eyes upon him.’

44:22  LA: Suggessimus domino meo : Non potest puer relinquere patrem suum : si enim illum dimiserit, morietur.
     EN: We explained to my lord, ‘The boy cannot leave his father, for if he leaves him, he will die.’

44:23  LA: Et dixisti servis tuis : Nisi venerit frater vester minimus vobiscum, non videbitis amplius faciem meam.
     EN: You said to your servants, ‘Unless your youngest brother comes with you, you shall not see my face again.’

44:24  LA: Cum ergo ascendissemus ad famulum tuum patrem nostrum, narravimus ei omnia quæ locutus est dominus meus.
     EN: When we went up to your servant our father, we told him everything my lord had spoken.

44:25  LA: Et dixit pater noster : Revertimini, et emite nobis parum tritici.
     EN: Our father said, ‘Return and buy us a little wheat.’

44:26  LA: Cui diximus : Ire non possumus : si frater noster minimus descenderit nobiscum, proficiscemur simul : alioquin illo absente, non audemus videre faciem viri.
     EN: We said to him, ‘We cannot go. If our youngest brother goes down with us, we will travel together. Otherwise, without him, we dare not see the man's face.’

44:27  LA: Ad quæ ille respondit : Vos scitis quod duos genuerit mihi uxor mea.
     EN: He answered, ‘You know that my wife bore me two sons.

44:28  LA: Egressus est unus, et dixistis : Bestia devoravit eum : et hucusque non comparet.
     EN: One went out, and you said, “A wild beast has devoured him,” and he has not appeared to this day.

44:29  LA: Si tuleritis et istum, et aliquid ei in via contigerit, deducetis canos meos cum mœrore ad inferos.
     EN: If you take this one also, and anything happens to him on the way, you will bring my gray hairs down in grief to the realm of the dead.’

44:30  LA: Igitur si intravero ad servum tuum patrem nostrum, et puer defuerit (cum anima illius ex hujus anima pendeat),
     EN: Therefore, if I come to your servant our father and the boy is absent—since his soul depends upon this one's soul—

44:31  LA: videritque eum non esse nobiscum, morietur, et deducent famuli tui canos ejus cum dolore ad inferos.
     EN: and he sees that the boy is not with us, he will die. Your servants will bring his gray hairs down in sorrow to the realm of the dead.

44:32  LA: Ego proprie servus tuus sim qui in meam hunc recepi fidem, et spopondi dicens : Nisi reduxero eum, peccati reus ero in patrem meum omni tempore.
     EN: Let me especially be your slave, for I took the boy into my own keeping and pledged, ‘Unless I bring him back, I shall bear guilt toward my father for all time.’

44:33  LA: Manebo itaque servus tuus pro puero in ministerio domini mei, et puer ascendat cum fratribus suis.
     EN: Therefore I will remain your slave in the boy's place, in my lord's service, and let the boy go up with his brothers.

44:34  LA: Non enim possum redire ad patrem meum, absente puero : ne calamitatis, quæ oppressura est patrem meum, testis assistam.
     EN: For I cannot return to my father without the boy, lest I stand as witness to the calamity that will overwhelm my father.”

GENESIS 45

45:1  LA: Non se poterat ultra cohibere Joseph multis coram astantibus : unde præcepit ut egrederentur cuncti foras, et nullus interesset alienus agnitioni mutuæ.
     EN: Joseph could no longer restrain himself with so many standing before him. He commanded everyone to go outside, so that no stranger would be present when they recognized one another.

45:2  LA: Elevavitque vocem cum fletu, quam audierunt Ægyptii, omnisque domus Pharaonis.
     EN: He raised his voice in weeping, which the Egyptians and Pharaoh's whole household heard.

45:3  LA: Et dixit fratribus suis : Ego sum Joseph : adhuc pater meus vivit ? Non poterant respondere fratres nimio terrore perterriti.
     EN: He said to his brothers, “I am Joseph. Is my father still alive?” His brothers could not answer, overwhelmed with terror.

45:4  LA: Ad quos ille clementer : Accedite, inquit, ad me. Et cum accessissent prope : Ego sum, ait, Joseph, frater vester, quem vendidistis in Ægyptum.
     EN: He said to them gently, “Come near to me.” When they had drawn near, he said, “I am Joseph your brother, whom you sold into Egypt.

45:5  LA: Nolite pavere, neque vobis durum esse videatur quod vendidistis me in his regionibus : pro salute enim vestra misit me Deus ante vos in Ægyptum.
     EN: Do not be afraid or let it seem hard to you that you sold me into these regions, for God sent me ahead of you into Egypt for your preservation.

45:6  LA: Biennium est enim quod cœpit fames esse in terra : et adhuc quinque anni restant, quibus nec arari poterit, nec meti.
     EN: Famine began in the land two years ago, and five years still remain in which neither plowing nor harvesting will be possible.

45:7  LA: Præmisitque me Deus ut reservemini super terram, et escas ad vivendum habere possitis.
     EN: God sent me ahead so that you may be preserved upon the earth and have food to live.

45:8  LA: Non vestro consilio, sed Dei voluntate huc missus sum : qui fecit me quasi patrem Pharaonis, et dominum universæ domus ejus, ac principem in omni terra Ægypti.
     EN: It was not by your plan but by God's will that I was sent here. He has made me as a father to Pharaoh, lord of his whole house, and ruler throughout the land of Egypt.

45:9  LA: Festinate, et ascendite ad patrem meum, et dicetis ei : Hæc mandat filius tuus Joseph : Deus fecit me dominum universæ terræ Ægypti : descende ad me, ne moreris,
     EN: Hurry and go up to my father, and tell him, ‘Your son Joseph sends this message: God has made me lord of the whole land of Egypt. Come down to me without delay.

45:10  LA: et habitabis in terra Gessen : erisque juxta me tu, et filii tui, et filii filiorum tuorum, oves tuæ, et armenta tua, et universa quæ possides :
     EN: You shall live in the land of Goshen and be near me—you, your sons, your sons' sons, your sheep, your herds, and everything you possess.

45:11  LA: ibique te pascam (adhuc enim quinque anni residui sunt famis) ne et tu pereas, et domus tua, et omnia quæ possides.
     EN: There I shall provide for you, for five years of famine still remain, lest you perish, and your household and everything you possess.’

45:12  LA: En oculi vestri, et oculi fratris mei Benjamin, vident quod os meum loquatur ad vos.
     EN: See, your own eyes and the eyes of my brother Benjamin see that it is my mouth speaking to you.

45:13  LA: Nuntiate patri meo universam gloriam meam, et cuncta quæ vidistis in Ægypto : festinate, et adducite eum ad me.
     EN: Tell my father of all my glory and everything you have seen in Egypt. Hurry and bring him to me.”

45:14  LA: Cumque amplexatus recidisset in collum Benjamin fratris sui, flevit : illo quoque similiter flente super collum ejus.
     EN: He embraced his brother Benjamin, fell upon his neck, and wept; Benjamin likewise wept upon his neck.

45:15  LA: Osculatusque est Joseph omnes fratres suos, et ploravit super singulos : post quæ ausi sunt loqui ad eum.
     EN: Joseph kissed all his brothers and wept over each one. After this they dared to speak to him.

45:16  LA: Auditumque est, et celebri sermone vulgatum in aula regis : Venerunt fratres Joseph : et gavisus est Pharao, atque omnis familia ejus.
     EN: The news was heard and widely repeated in the king's court: “Joseph's brothers have come.” Pharaoh and his whole household rejoiced.

45:17  LA: Dixitque ad Joseph ut imperaret fratribus suis, dicens : Onerantes jumenta, ite in terram Chanaan,
     EN: He told Joseph to command his brothers, saying, “Load your animals and go to the land of Canaan.

45:18  LA: et tollite inde patrem vestrum et cognationem, et venite ad me : et ego dabo vobis omnia bona Ægypti, ut comedatis medullam terræ.
     EN: Bring your father and kindred from there and come to me. I will give you all Egypt's good things, so that you may eat the richest of the land.

45:19  LA: Præcipe etiam ut tollant plaustra de terra Ægypti, ad subvectionem parvulorum suorum ac conjugum : et dicito : Tollite patrem vestrum, et properate quantocius venientes.
     EN: Command them also to take wagons from the land of Egypt to carry their little ones and wives, and say, ‘Bring your father and hurry to come as soon as possible.

45:20  LA: Nec dimittatis quidquam de supellectili vestra : quia omnes opes Ægypti vestræ erunt.
     EN: Leave none of your household goods behind, for all Egypt's riches shall be yours.’”

45:21  LA: Feceruntque filii Israël ut eis mandatum fuerat. Quibus dedit Joseph plaustra, secundum Pharaonis imperium, et cibaria in itinere.
     EN: The children of Israel did as they had been commanded. Joseph gave them wagons according to Pharaoh's command, and provisions for the journey.

45:22  LA: Singulis quoque proferri jussit binas stolas : Benjamin vero dedit trecentos argenteos cum quinque stolis optimis :
     EN: He also ordered two robes brought out for each one, but gave Benjamin three hundred silver pieces and five excellent robes.

45:23  LA: tantumdem pecuniæ et vestium mittens patri suo, addens et asinos decem, qui subveherent ex omnibus divitiis Ægypti, et totidem asinas, triticum in itinere, panesque portantes.
     EN: He sent the same amount of money and clothing to his father, adding ten male donkeys carrying some of all Egypt's riches and as many female donkeys carrying wheat and bread for the journey.

45:24  LA: Dimisit ergo fratres suos, et proficiscentibus ait : Ne irascamini in via.
     EN: He sent his brothers away and said as they departed, “Do not become angry on the way.”

45:25  LA: Qui ascendentes ex Ægypto, venerunt in terram Chanaan ad patrem suum Jacob.
     EN: They went up from Egypt and came to their father Jacob in the land of Canaan.

45:26  LA: Et nuntiaverunt ei, dicentes : Joseph filius tuus vivit : et ipse dominatur in omni terra Ægypti. Quo audito Jacob, quasi de gravi somno evigilans, tamen non credebat eis.
     EN: They told him, “Your son Joseph is alive, and he rules over the whole land of Egypt.” When Jacob heard this, he was as though awakening from a deep sleep, but still did not believe them.

45:27  LA: Illi e contra referebant omnem ordinem rei. Cumque vidisset plaustra et universa quæ miserat, revixit spiritus ejus,
     EN: They in turn related the whole course of events. When he saw the wagons and everything Joseph had sent, his spirit revived.

45:28  LA: et ait : Sufficit mihi si adhuc Joseph filius meus vivit : vadam, et videbo illum antequam moriar.
     EN: He said, “It is enough for me if my son Joseph is still alive. I will go and see him before I die.”

GENESIS 46

46:1  LA: Profectusque Israël cum omnibus quæ habebat, venit ad Puteum juramenti : et mactatis ibi victimis Deo patris sui Isaac,
     EN: Israel set out with everything he had and came to the Well of the Oath. There he offered sacrifices to the God of his father Isaac,

46:2  LA: audivit eum per visionem noctis vocantem se, et dicentem sibi : Jacob, Jacob. Cui respondit : Ecce adsum.
     EN: and in a vision of the night heard Him calling, “Jacob, Jacob.” He answered, “Here I am.”

46:3  LA: Ait illi Deus : Ego sum fortissimus Deus patris tui : noli timere, descende in Ægyptum, quia in gentem magnam faciam te ibi.
     EN: God said to him, “I am the most mighty God of your father. Do not fear; go down to Egypt, for I will make you a great nation there.

46:4  LA: Ego descendam tecum illuc, et ego inde adducam te revertentem : Joseph quoque ponet manus suas super oculos tuos.
     EN: I will go down there with you, and I will bring you back from there. Joseph also shall place his hands upon your eyes.”

46:5  LA: Surrexit autem Jacob a Puteo juramenti : tuleruntque eum filii cum parvulis et uxoribus suis in plaustris quæ miserat Pharao ad portandum senem,
     EN: Jacob rose from the Well of the Oath. His sons carried him, with their little ones and wives, in the wagons Pharaoh had sent to carry the old man,

46:6  LA: et omnia quæ possederat in terra Chanaan : venitque in Ægyptum cum omni semine suo,
     EN: and took everything he had possessed in the land of Canaan. He came to Egypt with all his offspring:

46:7  LA: filii ejus, et nepotes, filiæ, et cuncta simul progenies.
     EN: his sons and grandsons, daughters, and all his descendants together.

46:8  LA: Hæc sunt autem nomina filiorum Israël, qui ingressi sunt in Ægyptum, ipse cum liberis suis. Primogenitus Ruben.
     EN: These are the names of the children of Israel who entered Egypt, he and his children. The firstborn was Reuben.

46:9  LA: Filii Ruben : Henoch et Phallu et Hesron et Charmi.
     EN: Reuben's sons were Enoch, Pallu, Hezron, and Carmi.

46:10  LA: Filii Simeon : Jamuel et Jamin et Ahod, et Jachin et Sohar, et Saul filius Chanaanitidis.
     EN: Simeon's sons were Jemuel, Jamin, Ohad, Jachin, Zohar, and Shaul, the son of a Canaanite woman.

46:11  LA: Filii Levi : Gerson et Caath et Merari.
     EN: Levi's sons were Gershon, Kohath, and Merari.

46:12  LA: Filii Juda : Her et Onan et Sela et Phares et Zara ; mortui sunt autem Her et Onan in terra Chanaan. Natique sunt filii Phares : Hesron et Hamul.
     EN: Judah's sons were Er, Onan, Shelah, Perez, and Zerah, but Er and Onan died in the land of Canaan. Perez's sons were Hezron and Hamul.

46:13  LA: Filii Issachar : Thola et Phua et Job et Semron.
     EN: Issachar's sons were Tola, Puvah, Job, and Shimron.

46:14  LA: Filii Zabulon : Sared et Elon et Jahelel.
     EN: Zebulun's sons were Sered, Elon, and Jahleel.

46:15  LA: Hi filii Liæ quos genuit in Mesopotamia Syriæ cum Dina filia sua : omnes animæ filiorum ejus et filiarum, triginta tres.
     EN: These are Leah's sons, whom she bore in Mesopotamia of Syria, along with her daughter Dinah. All the persons of her sons and daughters were thirty-three.

46:16  LA: Filii Gad : Sephion et Haggi et Suni et Esebon et Heri et Arodi et Areli.
     EN: Gad's sons were Ziphion, Haggi, Shuni, Ezbon, Eri, Arodi, and Areli.

46:17  LA: Filii Aser : Jamne et Jesua et Jessui et Beria, Sara quoque soror eorum. Filii Beria : Heber et Melchiel.
     EN: Asher's sons were Imnah, Ishvah, Ishvi, and Beriah, and their sister was Serah. Beriah's sons were Heber and Malchiel.

46:18  LA: Hi filii Zelphæ, quam dedit Laban Liæ filiæ suæ : et hos genuit Jacob sedecim animas.
     EN: These are the sons of Zilpah, whom Laban gave to his daughter Leah. She bore these to Jacob, sixteen persons.

46:19  LA: Filii Rachel uxoris Jacob : Joseph et Benjamin.
     EN: The sons of Rachel, Jacob's wife, were Joseph and Benjamin.

46:20  LA: Natique sunt Joseph filii in terra Ægypti, quos genuit ei Aseneth filia Putiphare sacerdotis Heliopoleos : Manasses et Ephraim.
     EN: Joseph's sons were born to him in the land of Egypt, whom Asenath, daughter of Potiphera, priest of Heliopolis, bore him: Manasseh and Ephraim.

46:21  LA: Filii Benjamin : Bela et Bechor et Asbel et Gera et Naaman et Echi et Ros et Mophim et Ophim et Ared.
     EN: Benjamin's sons were Bela, Becher, Ashbel, Gera, Naaman, Ehi, Rosh, Muppim, Huppim, and Ard.

46:22  LA: Hi filii Rachel quos genuit Jacob : omnes animæ, quatuordecim.
     EN: These are Rachel's sons whom she bore to Jacob, fourteen persons in all.

46:23  LA: Filii Dan : Husim.
     EN: Dan's sons: Hushim.

46:24  LA: Filii Nephthali : Jasiel et Guni et Jeser et Sallem.
     EN: Naphtali's sons were Jahzeel, Guni, Jezer, and Shillem.

46:25  LA: Hi filii Balæ, quam dedit Laban Racheli filiæ suæ : et hos genuit Jacob : omnes animæ, septem.
     EN: These are the sons of Bilhah, whom Laban gave to his daughter Rachel. She bore these to Jacob, seven persons in all.

46:26  LA: Cunctæ animæ, quæ ingressæ sunt cum Jacob in Ægyptum, et egressæ sunt de femore illius, absque uxoribus filiorum ejus, sexaginta sex.
     EN: All the persons who entered Egypt with Jacob and came forth from his thigh, excluding his sons' wives, were sixty-six.

46:27  LA: Filii autem Joseph, qui nati sunt ei in terra Ægypti, animæ duæ. Omnes animæ domus Jacob, quæ ingressæ sunt in Ægyptum, fuere septuaginta.
     EN: Joseph's sons born to him in the land of Egypt were two persons. All the persons of Jacob's household who entered Egypt were seventy.

46:28  LA: Misit autem Judam ante se ad Joseph, ut nuntiaret ei, et occurreret in Gessen.
     EN: He sent Judah ahead of him to Joseph, to announce his coming and have him meet him in Goshen.

46:29  LA: Quo cum pervenisset, juncto Joseph curro suo, ascendit obviam patri suo ad eumdem locum : vidensque eum, irruit super collum ejus, et inter amplexus flevit.
     EN: When he arrived there, Joseph harnessed his chariot and went up to meet his father in that same place. Seeing him, he fell upon his neck and wept as he embraced him.

46:30  LA: Dixitque pater ad Joseph : Jam lætus moriar, quia vidi faciem tuam, et superstitem te relinquo.
     EN: His father said to Joseph, “Now I shall die happy, because I have seen your face and leave you still alive.”

46:31  LA: At ille locutus est ad fratres suos, et ad omnem domum patris sui : Ascendam, et nuntiabo Pharaoni, dicamque ei : Fratres mei, et domus patris mei, qui erant in terra Chanaan, venerunt ad me :
     EN: Joseph said to his brothers and all his father's household, “I will go up and report to Pharaoh, saying, ‘My brothers and my father's household, who were in the land of Canaan, have come to me.

46:32  LA: et sunt viri pastores ovium, curamque habent alendorum gregum : pecora sua, et armenta, et omnia quæ habere potuerunt, adduxerunt secum.
     EN: They are shepherds and tend the feeding of flocks. They have brought with them their livestock, herds, and everything they could possess.’

46:33  LA: Cumque vocaverit vos, et dixerit : Quod est opus vestrum ?
     EN: When he calls you and asks, ‘What is your occupation?’

46:34  LA: respondebitis : Viri pastores sumus servi tui, ab infantia nostra usque in præsens, et nos et patres nostri. Hæc autem dicetis, ut habitare possitis in terra Gessen : quia detestantur Ægyptii omnes pastores ovium.
     EN: you shall answer, ‘We your servants have been shepherds from our childhood to the present, both we and our fathers.’ Say this so that you may live in the land of Goshen, for the Egyptians detest all shepherds.”

GENESIS 47

47:1  LA: Ingressus ergo Joseph nuntiavit Pharaoni, dicens : Pater meus et fratres, oves eorum et armenta, et cuncta quæ possident, venerunt de terra Chanaan : et ecce consistunt in terra Gessen.
     EN: Joseph went in and reported to Pharaoh, saying, “My father and brothers, their sheep and herds, and everything they possess have come from the land of Canaan, and now they are in the land of Goshen.”

47:2  LA: Extremos quoque fratrum suorum quinque viros constituit coram rege :
     EN: He also presented five of his youngest brothers before the king.

47:3  LA: quos ille interrogavit : Quid habetis operis ? Responderunt : Pastores ovium sumus servi tui, et nos et patres nostri.
     EN: He asked them, “What is your occupation?” They answered, “We your servants are shepherds, both we and our fathers.

47:4  LA: Ad peregrinandum in terra tua venimus : quoniam non est herba gregibus servorum tuorum, ingravescente fame in terra Chanaan : petimusque ut esse nos jubeas servos tuos in terra Gessen.
     EN: We have come to sojourn in your land, because there is no pasture for your servants' flocks, as famine has become severe in the land of Canaan. We ask you to grant that we your servants may live in the land of Goshen.”

47:5  LA: Dixit itaque rex ad Joseph : Pater tuus et fratres tui venerunt ad te.
     EN: The king said to Joseph, “Your father and brothers have come to you.

47:6  LA: Terra Ægypti in conspectu tuo est : in optimo loco fac eos habitare, et trade eis terram Gessen. Quod si nosti in eis esse viros industrios, constitue illos magistros pecorum meorum.
     EN: The land of Egypt lies before you. Settle them in the best place and give them the land of Goshen. If you know capable men among them, appoint them overseers of my livestock.”

47:7  LA: Post hæc introduxit Joseph patrem suum ad regem, et statuit eum coram eo : qui benedicens illi,
     EN: After this Joseph brought his father to the king and set him before him. Jacob blessed him,

47:8  LA: et interrogatus ab eo : Quot sunt dies annorum vitæ tuæ ?
     EN: and when asked by him, “How many are the days of the years of your life?”

47:9  LA: respondit : Dies peregrinationis meæ centum triginta annorum sunt, parvi et mali, et non pervenerunt usque ad dies patrum meorum quibus peregrinati sunt.
     EN: he answered, “The days of my sojourning are one hundred thirty years, few and troubled. They have not reached the days of my fathers during which they sojourned.”

47:10  LA: Et benedicto rege, egressus est foras.
     EN: He blessed the king and went out.

47:11  LA: Joseph vero patri et fratribus suis dedit possessionem in Ægypto in optimo terræ loco, Ramesses, ut præceperat Pharao.
     EN: Joseph gave his father and brothers a possession in Egypt in the best place of the land, Rameses, as Pharaoh had commanded.

47:12  LA: Et alebat eos, omnemque domum patris sui, præbens cibaria singulis.
     EN: He sustained them and all his father's household, providing food for each one.

47:13  LA: In toto enim orbe panis deerat, et oppresserat fames terram, maxime Ægypti et Chanaan.
     EN: For bread was lacking throughout the whole world, and famine oppressed the land, especially Egypt and Canaan.

47:14  LA: E quibus omnem pecuniam congregavit pro venditione frumenti, et intulit eam in ærarium regis.
     EN: From these Joseph gathered all the money for the sale of grain and brought it into the king's treasury.

47:15  LA: Cumque defecisset emptoribus pretium, venit cuncta Ægyptus ad Joseph, dicens : Da nobis panes : quare morimur coram te, deficiente pecunia ?
     EN: When the buyers' money ran out, all Egypt came to Joseph, saying, “Give us bread. Why should we die before you because money has failed?”

47:16  LA: Quibus ille respondit : Adducite pecora vestra, et dabo vobis pro eis cibos, si pretium non habetis.
     EN: He answered them, “Bring your livestock, and I will give you food in exchange for them, if you have no money.”

47:17  LA: Quæ cum adduxissent, dedit eis alimenta pro equis, et ovibus, et bobus, et asinis : sustentavitque eos illo anno pro commutatione pecorum.
     EN: When they brought them, he gave them provisions in exchange for horses, sheep, oxen, and donkeys, and sustained them that year in exchange for livestock.

47:18  LA: Venerunt quoque anno secundo, et dixerunt ei : Non celabimus dominum nostrum quod deficiente pecunia, pecora simul defecerunt : nec clam te est, quod absque corporibus et terra nihil habeamus.
     EN: They came again in the second year and said to him, “We will not conceal from our lord that when the money failed, the livestock also ran out. You know that we have nothing except our bodies and land.

47:19  LA: Cur ergo moriemur te vidente ? et nos et terra nostra tui erimus : eme nos in servitutem regiam, et præbe semina, ne pereunte cultore redigatur terra in solitudinem.
     EN: Why should we die before your eyes? We and our land shall be yours. Buy us into royal servitude and provide seed, lest the land be reduced to a wilderness when its cultivator perishes.”

47:20  LA: Emit igitur Joseph omnem terram Ægypti, vendentibus singulis possessiones suas præ magnitudine famis. Subjecitque eam Pharaoni,
     EN: Joseph bought all the land of Egypt, as each person sold his property because of the greatness of the famine. He subjected it to Pharaoh,

47:21  LA: et cunctos populos ejus a novissimis terminis Ægypti usque ad extremos fines ejus,
     EN: and all its peoples, from Egypt's farthest boundaries to its remotest borders,

47:22  LA: præter terram sacerdotum, quæ a rege tradita fuerat eis : quibus et statuta cibaria ex horreis publicis præbebantur, et idcirco non sunt compulsi vendere possessiones suas.
     EN: except the priests' land, which the king had given them. They were also supplied with fixed rations from the public storehouses, and therefore were not compelled to sell their possessions.

47:23  LA: Dixit ergo Joseph ad populos : En ut cernitis, et vos et terram vestram Pharao possidet : accipite semina, et serite agros,
     EN: Joseph said to the peoples, “See, as you perceive, Pharaoh owns you and your land. Take seed and sow the fields,

47:24  LA: ut fruges habere possitis. Quintam partem regi dabitis : quatuor reliquas permitto vobis in sementem, et in cibum familiis et liberis vestris.
     EN: so that you may have produce. You shall give a fifth part to the king; the remaining four parts I leave to you for seed and for food for your households and children.”

47:25  LA: Qui responderunt : Salus nostra in manu tua est : respiciat nos tantum dominus noster, et læti serviemus regi.
     EN: They answered, “Our preservation is in your hand. Only let our lord look favorably upon us, and we shall gladly serve the king.”

47:26  LA: Ex eo tempore usque in præsentem diem, in universa terra Ægypti regibus quinta pars solvitur, et factum est quasi in legem, absque terra sacerdotali, quæ libera ab hac conditione fuit.
     EN: From that time to the present day, a fifth part has been paid to the kings throughout the whole land of Egypt, established as a law, except for the priests' land, which was free of this obligation.

47:27  LA: Habitavit ergo Israël in Ægypto, id est, in terra Gessen, et possedit eam : auctusque est, et multiplicatus nimis.
     EN: Israel lived in Egypt, that is, in the land of Goshen, and possessed it. He increased and multiplied exceedingly.

47:28  LA: Et vixit in ea decem et septem annis : factique sunt omnes dies vitæ illius, centum quadraginta septem annorum.
     EN: He lived there seventeen years, and all the days of his life were one hundred forty-seven years.

47:29  LA: Cumque appropinquare cerneret diem mortis suæ, vocavit filium suum Joseph, et dixit ad eum : Si inveni gratiam in conspectu tuo, pone manum tuam sub femore meo : et facies mihi misericordiam et veritatem, ut non sepelias me in Ægypto :
     EN: When he perceived that the day of his death was approaching, he called his son Joseph and said to him, “If I have found favor in your sight, place your hand beneath my thigh and show me kindness and faithfulness by not burying me in Egypt.

47:30  LA: sed dormiam cum patribus meis, et auferas me de terra hac, condasque in sepulchro majorum meorum. Cui respondit Joseph : Ego faciam quod jussisti.
     EN: Let me rest with my fathers; carry me from this land and bury me in my ancestors' tomb.” Joseph answered him, “I will do as you have commanded.”

47:31  LA: Et ille : Jura ergo, inquit, mihi. Quo jurante, adoravit Israël Deum, conversus ad lectuli caput.
     EN: He said, “Then swear to me.” When he swore, Israel worshiped God, turning toward the head of his bed.

GENESIS 48

48:1  LA: His ita transactis, nuntiatum est Joseph quod ægrotaret pater suus : qui, assumptis duobus filiis Manasse et Ephraim, ire perrexit.
     EN: After these things Joseph was told that his father was ill. He took his two sons, Manasseh and Ephraim, and set out to go to him.

48:2  LA: Dictumque est seni : Ecce filius tuus Joseph venit ad te. Qui confortatus sedit in lectulo.
     EN: The old man was told, “Your son Joseph is coming to you.” He gathered his strength and sat up in bed.

48:3  LA: Et ingresso ad se ait : Deus omnipotens apparuit mihi in Luza, quæ est in terra Chanaan : benedixitque mihi,
     EN: When Joseph came in, he said to him, “God Almighty appeared to me in Luz, which is in the land of Canaan. He blessed me

48:4  LA: et ait : Ego te augebo et multiplicabo, et faciam te in turbas populorum : daboque tibi terram hanc, et semini tuo post te in possessionem sempiternam.
     EN: and said, ‘I will increase and multiply you and make you a multitude of peoples. I will give this land to you and your offspring after you as an everlasting possession.’

48:5  LA: Duo ergo filii tui, qui nati sunt tibi in terra Ægypti antequam huc venirem ad te, mei erunt : Ephraim et Manasses, sicut Ruben et Simeon reputabuntur mihi.
     EN: Therefore your two sons, born to you in the land of Egypt before I came here to you, shall be mine. Ephraim and Manasseh shall be reckoned as mine, like Reuben and Simeon.

48:6  LA: Reliquos autem quos genueris post eos, tui erunt, et nomine fratrum suorum vocabuntur in possessionibus suis.
     EN: But any others you father after them shall be yours, and shall be called by their brothers' names in their inheritances.

48:7  LA: Mihi enim, quando veniebam de Mesopotamia, mortua est Rachel in terra Chanaan in ipso itinere, eratque vernum tempus : et ingrediebar Ephratam, et sepelivi eam juxta viam Ephratæ, quæ alio nomine appellatur Bethlehem.
     EN: For when I was coming from Mesopotamia, Rachel died beside me in the land of Canaan, on the journey itself, and it was springtime. I was approaching Ephrath, and I buried her beside the road to Ephrath, which is also called Bethlehem.”

48:8  LA: Videns autem filios ejus dixit ad eum : Qui sunt isti ?
     EN: Seeing his sons, he said to him, “Who are these?”

48:9  LA: Respondit : Filii mei sunt, quos donavit mihi Deus in hoc loco. Adduc, inquit, eos ad me, ut benedicam illis.
     EN: He answered, “They are my sons, whom God has given me in this place.” He said, “Bring them to me so that I may bless them.”

48:10  LA: Oculi enim Israël caligabant præ nimia senectute, et clare videre non poterat. Applicitosque ad se, deosculatus et circumplexus eos,
     EN: Israel's eyes were dim from extreme old age, and he could not see clearly. When they were brought to him, he kissed and embraced them,

48:11  LA: dixit ad filium suum : Non sum fraudatus aspectu tuo : insuper ostendit mihi Deus semen tuum.
     EN: and said to his son, “I have not been deprived of seeing you; God has shown me your offspring besides.”

48:12  LA: Cumque tulisset eos Joseph de gremio patris, adoravit pronus in terram.
     EN: Joseph took them from his father's lap and bowed facedown to the ground.

48:13  LA: Et posuit Ephraim ad dexteram suam, id est, ad sinistram Israël : Manassen vero in sinistra sua, ad dexteram scilicet patris, applicuitque ambos ad eum.
     EN: He placed Ephraim at his right, that is, at Israel's left, and Manasseh at his left, that is, at his father's right, and brought both near him.

48:14  LA: Qui extendens manum dexteram, posuit super caput Ephraim minoris fratris : sinistram autem super caput Manasse qui major natu erat, commutans manus.
     EN: Israel stretched out his right hand and placed it upon the head of Ephraim, the younger brother, and his left upon Manasseh's head, who was older, crossing his hands.

48:15  LA: Benedixitque Jacob filiis Joseph, et ait : Deus, in cujus conspectu ambulaverunt patres mei Abraham, et Isaac ; Deus qui pascit me ab adolescentia mea usque in præsentem diem :
     EN: Jacob blessed Joseph's sons and said, “God, in whose presence my fathers Abraham and Isaac walked; God, who has fed me from my youth to the present day;

48:16  LA: angelus, qui eruit me de cunctis malis, benedicat pueris istis : et invocetur super eos nomen meum, nomina quoque patrum meorum Abraham et Isaac, et crescant in multitudinem super terram.
     EN: the angel who rescued me from every evil—may He bless these boys. May my name and the names of my fathers Abraham and Isaac be called upon them, and may they increase into a multitude upon the earth.”

48:17  LA: Videns autem Joseph quod posuisset pater suus dexteram manum super caput Ephraim, graviter accepit : et apprehensam manum patris levare conatus est de capite Ephraim, et transferre super caput Manasse.
     EN: Joseph saw that his father had placed his right hand upon Ephraim's head and was displeased. He grasped his father's hand and tried to lift it from Ephraim's head and move it onto Manasseh's head.

48:18  LA: Dixitque ad patrem : Non ita convenit, pater : quia hic est primogenitus, pone dexteram tuam super caput ejus.
     EN: He said to his father, “This is not fitting, father, for this one is the firstborn. Put your right hand upon his head.”

48:19  LA: Qui renuens, ait : Scio, fili mi, scio : et iste quidem erit in populos, et multiplicabitur : sed frater ejus minor, major erit illo : et semen illius crescet in gentes.
     EN: He refused and said, “I know, my son, I know. He too shall become peoples and shall multiply, but his younger brother shall be greater than he, and his offspring shall grow into nations.”

48:20  LA: Benedixitque eis in tempore illo, dicens : In te benedicetur Israël, atque dicetur : Faciat tibi Deus sicut Ephraim, et sicut Manasse. Constituitque Ephraim ante Manassen.
     EN: He blessed them at that time, saying, “In you Israel shall be blessed, and it shall be said, ‘May God make you like Ephraim and like Manasseh.’” Thus he placed Ephraim before Manasseh.

48:21  LA: Et ait ad Joseph filium suum : En ego morior, et erit Deus vobiscum, reducetque vos ad terram patrum vestrorum.
     EN: He said to his son Joseph, “See, I am dying, but God will be with you and bring you back to the land of your fathers.

48:22  LA: Do tibi partem unam extra fratres tuos, quam tuli de manu Amorrhæi in gladio et arcu meo.
     EN: I give you one portion beyond your brothers, which I took from the hand of the Amorite with my sword and bow.”

GENESIS 49

49:1  LA: Vocavit autem Jacob filios suos, et ait eis : Congregamini, ut annuntiem quæ ventura sunt vobis in diebus novissimis.
     EN: Jacob called his sons and said to them, “Gather, so that I may announce what shall come upon you in the last days.

49:2  LA: [Congregamini, et audite, filii Jacob, audite Israël patrem vestrum :
     EN: Gather and hear, sons of Jacob; hear Israel your father.

49:3  LA: Ruben, primogenitus meus, tu fortitudo mea, et principium doloris mei ; prior in donis, major in imperio.
     EN: Reuben, my firstborn, you are my strength and the beginning of my sorrow, first in gifts and greater in authority.

49:4  LA: Effusus es sicut aqua, non crescas : quia ascendisti cubile patris tui, et maculasti stratum ejus.
     EN: You have poured yourself out like water; may you not increase, because you climbed into your father's bed and defiled his resting place.

49:5  LA: Simeon et Levi fratres vasa iniquitatis bellantia.
     EN: Simeon and Levi are brothers, warring instruments of iniquity.

49:6  LA: In consilium eorum non veniat anima mea, et in cœtu illorum non sit gloria mea : quia in furore suo occiderunt virum, et in voluntate sua suffoderunt murum.
     EN: May my soul not enter their counsel, and may my glory not be in their assembly, for in their fury they killed a man, and in their willfulness they undermined a wall.

49:7  LA: Maledictus furor eorum, quia pertinax : et indignatio eorum, quia dura : dividam eos in Jacob, et dispergam eos in Israël.
     EN: Cursed be their fury, because it is stubborn, and their anger, because it is harsh. I will divide them in Jacob and scatter them in Israel.

49:8  LA: Juda, te laudabunt fratres tui : manus tua in cervicibus inimicorum tuorum, adorabunt te filii patris tui.
     EN: Judah, your brothers shall praise you. Your hand shall be upon your enemies' necks; your father's sons shall bow to you.

49:9  LA: Catulus leonis Juda : ad prædam, fili mi, ascendisti : requiescens accubuisti ut leo, et quasi leæna : quis suscitabit eum ?
     EN: Judah is a lion's cub. To the prey, my son, you have gone up. Resting, you lie down like a lion, like a lioness; who shall rouse him?

49:10  LA: Non auferetur sceptrum de Juda, et dux de femore ejus, donec veniat qui mittendus est, et ipse erit expectatio gentium.
     EN: The scepter shall not be taken from Judah, nor a ruler from his thigh, until the one who is to be sent comes, and he shall be the expectation of the nations.

49:11  LA: Ligans ad vineam pullum suum, et ad vitem, o fili mi, asinam suam, lavabit in vino stolam suam et in sanguine uvæ pallium suum.
     EN: Binding his colt to the vineyard, and his female donkey to the vine, O my son, he shall wash his robe in wine and his cloak in the blood of the grape.

49:12  LA: Pulchriores sunt oculi ejus vino, et dentes ejus lacte candidiores.
     EN: His eyes are more beautiful than wine, and his teeth whiter than milk.

49:13  LA: Zabulon in littore maris habitabit, et in statione navium pertingens usque ad Sidonem.
     EN: Zebulun shall live on the seashore, at a harbor for ships, reaching as far as Sidon.

49:14  LA: Issachar asinus fortis accubans inter terminos.
     EN: Issachar is a strong donkey lying down between the boundaries.

49:15  LA: Vidit requiem, quod esset bona et terram, quod optima : et supposuit humerum suum ad portandum, factusque est tributis serviens.
     EN: He saw that rest was good and the land excellent. He bent his shoulder to bear burdens and became a servant under tribute.

49:16  LA: Dan judicabit populum suum sicut et alia tribus in Israël.
     EN: Dan shall judge his people like another tribe in Israel.

49:17  LA: Fiat Dan coluber in via, cerastes in semita, mordens ungulas equi, ut cadat ascensor ejus retro.
     EN: Let Dan be a serpent on the road, a horned snake on the path, biting the horse's heels so that its rider falls backward.

49:18  LA: Salutare tuum expectabo, Domine.
     EN: I shall await Your salvation, Lord.

49:19  LA: Gad, accinctus præliabitur ante eum : et ipse accingetur retrorsum.
     EN: Gad, armed for battle, shall fight before him, and he himself shall arm himself to the rear.

49:20  LA: Aser, pinguis panis ejus, et præbebit delicias regibus.
     EN: Asher's bread shall be rich, and he shall provide delicacies for kings.

49:21  LA: Nephthali, cervus emissus, et dans eloquia pulchritudinis.
     EN: Naphtali is a deer set free, giving words of beauty.

49:22  LA: Filius accrescens Joseph, filius accrescens et decorus aspectu : filiæ discurrerunt super murum.
     EN: Joseph is a growing son, a growing son and beautiful to behold; daughters have run over the wall.

49:23  LA: Sed exasperaverunt eum et jurgati sunt, invideruntque illi habentes jacula.
     EN: But those bearing darts provoked him, quarreled with him, and envied him.

49:24  LA: Sedit in forti arcus ejus, et dissoluta sunt vincula brachiorum et manuum illius per manus potentis Jacob : inde pastor egressus est, lapis Israël.
     EN: His bow rested in strength, and the bonds of his arms and hands were loosened by the hands of the Mighty One of Jacob. From there came forth the shepherd, the stone of Israel.

49:25  LA: Deus patris tui erit adjutor tuus, et omnipotens benedicet tibi benedictionibus cæli desuper, benedictionibus abyssi jacentis deorsum, benedictionibus uberum et vulvæ.
     EN: Your father's God shall be your helper, and the Almighty shall bless you with blessings of heaven above, blessings of the deep lying below, blessings of breasts and womb.

49:26  LA: Benedictiones patris tui confortatæ sunt benedictionibus patrum ejus, donec veniret desiderium collium æternorum : fiant in capite Joseph, et in vertice Nazaræi inter fratres suos.
     EN: Your father's blessings have been strengthened by the blessings of his fathers, until the desire of the everlasting hills should come. May they be upon Joseph's head and upon the crown of the one consecrated among his brothers.

49:27  LA: Benjamin lupus rapax, mane comedat prædam, et vespere dividet spolia.]
     EN: Benjamin is a ravenous wolf; in the morning let him eat prey, and in the evening he shall divide the spoil.”

49:28  LA: Omnes hi in tribubus Israël duodecim : hæc locutus est eis pater suus, benedixitque singulis benedictionibus propriis.
     EN: All these are the twelve tribes of Israel. Their father spoke these things to them and blessed each with his own blessing.

49:29  LA: Et præcepit eis, dicens : Ego congregor ad populum meum : sepelite me cum patribus meis in spelunca duplici quæ est in agro Ephron Hethæi,
     EN: He commanded them, saying, “I am being gathered to my people. Bury me with my fathers in the double cave that is in the field of Ephron the Hittite,

49:30  LA: contra Mambre in terra Chanaan, quam emit Abraham cum agro ab Ephron Hethæo in possessionem sepulchri.
     EN: opposite Mamre in the land of Canaan, which Abraham bought with the field from Ephron the Hittite as a possession for burial.

49:31  LA: Ibi sepelierunt eum, et Saram uxorem ejus : ibi sepultus est Isaac cum Rebecca conjuge sua : ibi et Lia condita jacet.
     EN: There they buried him and his wife Sarah; there Isaac was buried with his wife Rebekah; there also Leah lies buried.”

49:32 (Vg 49:30)  LA: contra Mambre in terra Chanaan, quam emit Abraham cum agro ab Ephron Hethæo in possessionem sepulchri.
     EN: opposite Mamre in the land of Canaan, which Abraham bought with the field from Ephron the Hittite as a possession for burial.

49:33 (Vg 49:32)  LA: Finitisque mandatis quibus filios instruebat, collegit pedes suos super lectulum, et obiit : appositusque est ad populum suum.
     EN: When he finished the commands with which he instructed his sons, he drew his feet onto the bed and died, and was joined to his people.

GENESIS 50

50:1  LA: Quod cernens Joseph, ruit super faciem patris, flens et deosculans eum.
     EN: Joseph saw this and fell upon his father's face, weeping and kissing him.

50:2  LA: Præcepitque servis suis medicis ut aromatibus condirent patrem.
     EN: He commanded his servants the physicians to preserve his father with spices.

50:3  LA: Quibus jussa explentibus, transierunt quadraginta dies : iste quippe mos erat cadaverum conditorum : flevitque eum Ægyptus septuaginta diebus.
     EN: While they fulfilled his commands, forty days passed, for this was the customary time for embalming bodies. Egypt mourned him for seventy days.

50:4  LA: Et expleto planctus tempore, locutus est Joseph ad familiam Pharaonis : Si inveni gratiam in conspectu vestro, loquimini in auribus Pharaonis :
     EN: When the time of mourning was completed, Joseph spoke to Pharaoh's household: “If I have found favor in your sight, speak in Pharaoh's hearing,

50:5  LA: eo quod pater meus adjuraverit me dicens : En morior : in sepulchro meo, quod fodi mihi in terra Chanaan, sepelies me. Ascendam igitur, et sepeliam patrem meum, ac revertar.
     EN: because my father made me swear, saying, ‘See, I am dying. Bury me in my tomb, which I dug for myself in the land of Canaan.’ Let me therefore go up and bury my father, and I shall return.”

50:6  LA: Dixitque ei Pharao : Ascende, et sepeli patrem tuum sicut adjuratus es.
     EN: Pharaoh said to him, “Go up and bury your father as you have sworn.”

50:7  LA: Quo ascendente, ierunt cum eo omnes senes domus Pharaonis, cunctique majores natu terræ Ægypti :
     EN: As he went up, all the elders of Pharaoh's house and all the elders of the land of Egypt went with him,

50:8  LA: domus Joseph cum fratribus suis, absque parvulis, et gregibus atque armentis, quæ dereliquerant in terra Gessen.
     EN: and Joseph's household with his brothers, except the little ones, flocks, and herds, which they left in the land of Goshen.

50:9  LA: Habuit quoque in comitatu currus et equites : et facta est turba non modica.
     EN: Chariots and horsemen were also in his company, making a considerable gathering.

50:10  LA: Veneruntque ad Aream Atad, quæ sita est trans Jordanem : ubi celebrantes exequias planctu magno atque vehementi impleverunt septem dies.
     EN: They came to the Threshing Floor of Atad, which lies beyond the Jordan, and there observed funeral rites with great and vehement mourning for seven days.

50:11  LA: Quod cum vidissent habitatores terræ Chanaan, dixerunt : Planctus magnus est iste Ægyptiis. Et idcirco vocatum est nomen loci illius, Planctus Ægypti.
     EN: When the inhabitants of the land of Canaan saw this, they said, “This is a great mourning for the Egyptians.” Therefore that place was called Mourning of Egypt.

50:12  LA: Fecerunt ergo filii Jacob sicut præceperat eis :
     EN: Jacob's sons did as he had commanded them.

50:13  LA: et portantes eum in terram Chanaan, sepelierunt eum in spelunca duplici, quam emerat Abraham cum agro in possessionem sepulchri ab Ephron Hethæo, contra faciem Mambre.
     EN: They carried him into the land of Canaan and buried him in the double cave that Abraham had bought with the field from Ephron the Hittite, opposite Mamre, as a possession for burial.

50:14  LA: Reversusque est Joseph in Ægyptum cum fratribus suis, et omni comitatu, sepulto patre.
     EN: After burying his father, Joseph returned to Egypt with his brothers and his whole company.

50:15  LA: Quo mortuo, timentes fratres ejus, et mutuo colloquentes : Ne forte memor sit injuriæ quam passus est, et reddat nobis omne malum quod fecimus,
     EN: When their father was dead, Joseph's brothers were afraid and said to one another, “Perhaps he remembers the wrong he suffered and will repay us all the evil we did.”

50:16  LA: mandaverunt ei dicentes : Pater tuus præcepit nobis antequam moreretur,
     EN: They sent him a message, saying, “Before your father died, he commanded us

50:17  LA: ut hæc tibi verbis illius diceremus : Obsecro ut obliviscaris sceleris fratrum tuorum, et peccati atque malitiæ quam exercuerunt in te : nos quoque oramus ut servis Dei patris tui dimittas iniquitatem hanc. Quibus auditis flevit Joseph.
     EN: to say this to you in his words: ‘I beg you to forget your brothers' offense, and the sin and malice they practiced against you.’ We also beg you to forgive this iniquity of the servants of your father's God.” Joseph wept when he heard these things.

50:18  LA: Veneruntque ad eum fratres sui : et proni adorantes in terram, dixerunt : Servi tui sumus.
     EN: His brothers came to him and bowed facedown to the ground, saying, “We are your slaves.”

50:19  LA: Quibus ille respondit : Nolite timere : num Dei possumus resistere voluntati ?
     EN: He answered them, “Do not fear. Can we resist God's will?

50:20  LA: Vos cogitastis de me malum : sed Deus vertit illud in bonum, ut exaltaret me, sicut in præsentiarum cernitis, et salvos faceret multos populos.
     EN: You planned evil against me, but God turned it to good, to exalt me, as you now see, and to save many peoples.

50:21  LA: Nolite timere : ego pascam vos et parvulos vestros : consolatusque est eos, et blande ac leniter est locutus.
     EN: Do not fear. I shall provide for you and your little ones.” He comforted them and spoke kindly and gently.

50:22  LA: Et habitavit in Ægypto cum omni domo patris sui : vixitque centum decem annis. Et vidit Ephraim filios usque ad tertiam generationem. Filii quoque Machir filii Manasse nati sunt in genibus Joseph.
     EN: He lived in Egypt with all his father's household, and lived one hundred ten years. He saw Ephraim's children to the third generation. The children of Machir, Manasseh's son, were also born upon Joseph's knees.

50:23 (Vg 50:22)  LA: Et habitavit in Ægypto cum omni domo patris sui : vixitque centum decem annis. Et vidit Ephraim filios usque ad tertiam generationem. Filii quoque Machir filii Manasse nati sunt in genibus Joseph.
     EN: He lived in Egypt with all his father's household, and lived one hundred ten years. He saw Ephraim's children to the third generation. The children of Machir, Manasseh's son, were also born upon Joseph's knees.

50:24 (Vg 50:23)  LA: Quibus transactis, locutus est fratribus suis : Post mortem meam Deus visitabit vos, et ascendere vos faciet de terra ista ad terram quam juravit Abraham, Isaac et Jacob.
     EN: After these things he spoke to his brothers: “After my death God will visit you and make you go up from this land to the land He swore to Abraham, Isaac, and Jacob.”

50:25 (Vg 50:24)  LA: Cumque adjurasset eos atque dixisset : Deus visitabit vos, asportate ossa mea vobiscum de loco isto :
     EN: He made them swear and said, “God will visit you. Carry my bones with you from this place.”

50:26 (Vg 50:25)  LA: mortuus est, expletis centum decem vitæ suæ annis. Et conditus aromatibus, repositus est in loculo in Ægypto.
     EN: He died when the one hundred ten years of his life were complete. Preserved with spices, he was laid in a coffin in Egypt.
